[CODE] Subject : tracking alert equity double-counted a ghost "scalping" wallet after the scalping_v6 migration
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : operator screenshot showed Telegram equity "$3,047,123" on a portfolio actually in loss -- traced to `_run_paper_cycle_locked`'s tracking-alert cash calculation, which hardcoded `sum([cash_available("scalping"), cash_available("swing"), cash_available("vc")])`, a leftover from the pre-scalping_v6-merge 3-pocket architecture (see the 2026.08.01 entry below: "scalping" wallet rows were migrated to "scalping_v6" that same session). `cash_available()` fails open to the full untouched $1,000,000 when a wallet has no `paper_state` row (documented, intentional degradation elsewhere) -- so `cash_available("scalping")` silently returned a full ghost $1M every cycle, while the real open positions' VALUE (via the unscoped `tracked` list) was added on top without ever being subtracted from that ghost wallet's cash. Pure double-count, inflating displayed equity by exactly the sum of open positions' value -- arithmetic matches the operator's screenshot almost exactly ($3,000,000 + ~$47,123 open value ~= $3,047,123). `format_position_tracking_alert`'s "3 poches combinées" label was also still a hardcoded literal, silently stale since the 6-pocket merge.
Solution : cash/equity now computed from `all_reporting_wallets()` (the real, dynamic set of wallets with either a sourcing gate or an actual `paper_state` row) instead of the hardcoded 3-name list -- self-healing against any future pocket add/rename/retire. `format_position_tracking_alert` gains a `pocket_count` parameter threaded through from the real wallet count, replacing the hardcoded "3".
`paper_trader.py` -- 3 new tests (`test_paper_trader.py`: label never hardcodes a stale count, degrades to generic label when count is absent, and the core regression test asserting equity stays within $200 of the correct 8x-starting-capital total -- not the ~$10,000 gap a double-count would produce), full suite green (8800 passed, 17 skipped).

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject : 7/7 closed scalping trades lost -- broken trailing stop, blind stagnation timeout, V5's fresh-RR check dead, no trend filter
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : operator ("je veut du 3% par jour, trouve une solution") -- diagnostic Workflow (2 agents) found every closed scalping trade (v1/v3/v6, 7 total) lost, all in the -1.7% to -3.6% range, all closed via the blind stagnation timeout, none via the ATR trailing stop. Root cause chain, not 3 independent bugs: (a) `scalping_variants.py::_buy_result` hardcoded `entry_atr_pct = None` on every variant even though V1/V2/V4/V5 already compute a real ATR internally to size their OWN stop -- the value was computed then thrown away, so `paper_trader._effective_trail_pct` silently fell back to the generic 15% trailing stop, wildly too wide for scalping-scale moves (the 7 real losses were 1.7%-3.6%) -- the trailing stop never actually triggered. (b) With the real stop inert, the 3h stagnation timeout (Item deployed 08/01) became the ONLY exit path, and it closed "at whatever the current price is" checking only the UPSIDE (peak_gain_pct) -- blind to how far the price had ALREADY dropped, treating a genuinely flat position and a -3.6% drawdown identically. (c) `scalping_v5` ("no fixed TP, pure trailing stop" by design) had literally ZERO trades ever, regardless of time elapsed: `paper_trader._fresh_rr` unconditionally returned `None` whenever `target` was falsy, which `_execution_rr_still_valid` treats as fail-closed -- silently killing 100% of V5's BUY signals at the pre-execution freshness recheck, invisible (never even reaching the `buy_refused` funnel bucket). (d) Deepest structural gap: none of the 6 scalping entries (5 variants + the legacy RSI-divergence engine) had ANY trend/market-context filter before buying -- just "the oversold indicator just exited its zone", no confirmation a real bounce (vs. a dead-cat one inside a larger downtrend) was forming; confirmed by data, not theory -- `peak_gain_pct = 0.00%` on all 7 trades, none ever ticked a single point above entry after opening.
Solution : (a) `entry_atr_pct` now computed once in `_buy_result` (`atr / entry`, uniform across all 5 variants including V3 whose own stop is structural, never ATR-based) instead of hardcoded `None` -- repairs the trailing stop. (b) stagnation timeout gains a symmetric floor: reuses `SCALPING_STAGNATION_MIN_MOVE_PCT` (already the "no significant move" definition on the upside) on the downside too -- a position down past that threshold is a real drawdown, not stagnation, left for the (now-repaired) trailing stop to handle, never force-closed blind. (c) `_fresh_rr` treats a missing `target` as "no upper bound by design" (`float("inf")`, clears any finite bar) rather than "can't judge" (`None`, fail-closed) -- the invalidation check (structural break) still fully applies underneath. (d) new trend-alignment gate in `scalping_variants._gates_and_candles_uncached`, shared by all 5 variants before any of them even sees a candidate: reuses `momentum_entry._technical_alignment` (EMA12>EMA26 / MACD>signal / bullish candle pattern, 0-3) and its ALREADY-established direct-buy bar (`_ALIGN_SCORE_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY=2`, the same threshold the legacy scalping engine's own direct-buy path enforces) rather than inventing a new number -- `hold_reason="no_trend_alignment"` below the bar. `align_score`/`align_ema`/`align_macd`/`align_pattern` now also propagated into the persisted signal (same real gap as entry_atr_pct -- computed then thrown away, so `risk_guard.compute_entry_alloc`'s conviction sizing fell back to its own "missing signal" MAX tier, never actually earned by a real signal).
Scope note: the legacy RSI-divergence engine (scalping_v6, momentum_entry.py) already gates its OWN direct-buy path on `align_score >= _ALIGN_SCORE_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY` -- its LLM-confirmed path does not enforce this as a hard gate (informational only for the LLM). Left unchanged this round (momentum_entry.py is shared with swing/vc, a much larger blast radius) -- flagged as a real remaining gap, not fixed here.
`skills/scalping_variants.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- new tests (`test_scalping_variants.py`: entry_atr_pct/align_score propagated for V1 and V3 (no native ATR), trend gate rejects/allows on score; `test_paper_trader.py`: fresh_rr no-target returns inf and still rejects a broken structure, stagnation timeout never fires on a real drawdown vs. still fires on a genuinely flat position), full suite green (8800 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `b566a534`).

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject : scalping_v2-v5 silent 4h+ -- shared-budget starvation + websocket never wired to the variants + legacy "scalping" folded into v6
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : operator ("cest vraiment etrange quil se passe rien") -- the 5 scalping_v1-v5 pockets (activated ~4h earlier) had produced only ONE limit order (v1) and ZERO positions/orders for v2-v5, while the legacy "scalping" pocket (meant to be REPLACED, not added to) showed 3 open positions and 642 limit orders -- exactly what the operator believed was "the new system trading". 2-agent research Workflow confirmed live against the prod DB: (1) `momentum_websocket._drain_multi_pocket` (30s, the fast path) had NEVER been updated to know about scalping_v1-v5 -- its loop hardcoded 3 wallets (`scalping`/`swing`/`vc`), so it kept feeding the legacy pocket in parallel, invisible and non-comparable to the new variants. (2) The only path that DID know about the variants (`momentum_discovery_cycle`, 15min heartbeat) has a hard `asyncio.wait_for(timeout=300)` budget shared across ALL pockets, processed sequentially (v1->v2->v3->v4->v5->swing->vc) -- v1 alone consumed 245-283s/300s on every tick (measured across 6 bursts), so v2-v5 never got any window to evaluate even a single candidate. The shared `_gates_cache` (120s TTL, meant to let variants 2-5 reuse variant 1's network fetch for the SAME candidate for free) could never do its job either: its TTL expired long before v1 finished its own full pass.
Explicit operator decision ("corrige et le scalping met le a part en v6" -- fix it, and set the legacy scalping pocket aside as v6): rather than retiring the legacy pocket, it becomes its OWN 6th comparison arm (same RSI-divergence engine, unchanged), with its full history (positions/orders/capital) migrated, never reset to zero.
Solution : (a) new shared function `paper_trader.build_scalping_pocket_entries()` -- the SOLE source of truth for building the scalping pocket list, called by both the heartbeat AND the websocket (previously built independently in both places, the root cause of bug #1). (b) `scalping_v6` = the same legacy engine (`_default_momentum_analyzer(mode="scalping")`), added as a 6th entry. (c) every scalping pocket (v1-v6) now shares the SAME truncated candidate slice (`MAX_SCALPING_VARIANT_CANDIDATES_PER_CYCLE = 10`, preserves the "compared under identical conditions" design intent) instead of the full list (up to 50) each -- a full 6-variant pass now fits inside the shared cache's TTL (v1 pays the real network cost once, v2-v6 reuse the cache), fixing bug #2 without parallelizing network calls (which would have amplified the pressure already under watch on GeckoTerminal). (d) `paper_trader.migrate_legacy_wallet_rows(old, new)` -- renames wallet='scalping' -> 'scalping_v6' in place across the 7 wallet-scoped tables (paper_state/paper_position/paper_position_archive/pending_limit_order/paper_weekly_cycle/momentum_scan_log/rsi_divergence_log), idempotent, never destructive. (e) `risk_guard.migrate_wallet_state(old, new)` -- same migration for the dedicated circuit-breaker state file, refuses to overwrite an existing destination. (f) 2nd real bug found WHILE WRITING (d)'s idempotence test: `_ensure_tables()` unconditionally re-seeded a fresh "scalping" row at $1M on EVERY call (inherited from the 27/07 migration to the 3-pocket architecture) -- without a fix, migration (d) would have been silently undone on the very next cycle. "scalping" removed from the automatic seed (swing/vc only) -- the gate-OFF fallback (legacy "scalping" pocket, no variants) is unaffected, degrading gracefully via lazy creation the same way scalping_v1-v6 already do.
`paper_trader.py`/`momentum_websocket.py`/`risk_guard.py` -- new tests (`test_paper_trader.py`: build_scalping_pocket_entries gate ON/OFF/truncation/v6 analyzer, migrate_legacy_wallet_rows full/idempotent/empty, all_pocket_wallets 6 variants; `test_momentum_websocket.py`: drain correctly dispatches all 6 scalping pockets when the variants gate is on; `test_risk_guard.py`: migrate_wallet_state moves/no-ops/never overwrites), full suite green (8793 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `82728d03`). Production data migration ran clean right after deploy: 1 paper_state row, 3 paper_position, 58 paper_position_archive, 648 pending_limit_order, 1 paper_weekly_cycle, 12019 momentum_scan_log, 136 rsi_divergence_log rows moved "scalping" -> "scalping_v6", zero collision (risk_guard state file had none to migrate -- already cleared earlier the same day). Verified live post-deploy: paper_state has no "scalping" row left, only "scalping_v6".

[CODE] Subject  : Watching limit-order monitoring -- real root cause of the persistent 429, no cache/cap
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : the GeckoTerminal 429 rate stayed nearly identical (59-60/15min) even after fixing the 3 uncoordinated clients (geckoterminal.py, ohlcv.py, base_crawler.py -- entries below). Research Workflow (2 agents) relaunched on the mystery with two distinct angles: an exhaustive audit for a possible 4th client, and a computation of the real load generated vs. the configured throughput. Finding: `limit_orders.process_active_orders()` re-checks EVERY `watching` order tagged `rsi_divergence_pending`/`swing` via `check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` on EVERY WebSocket drain pass (30s), with no cache or cap at all -- unlike every other source in the pipeline (the 5 scalping variants' 120s `_gates_cache`, `MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_DRAIN`, `MAX_EVALUATIONS_PER_HOUR`). Measured live: 104 of 105 active watching orders went through this path -> ~208 REQUEST calls/min against ~21 req/min imposed by the shared lock (ratio ~10.6x). The lock does correctly cap the REAL outbound throughput, but the queue never drains (permanent saturation, zero slack) -- a 429 from a queue that's never emptied, not a plain raw throughput overshoot.
Solution : new hard cap per drain pass (`MAX_RSI_DIVERGENCE_WATCH_CHECKS_PER_DRAIN = 10`) with "least-recently-checked first" rotation (`_rsi_watch_check_last_at`, dict order_id -> timestamp, purged of resolved ids on every pass) -- an order not selected this pass stays `watching` unchanged (equivalent to `wait`), never lost. A fixed cap was chosen over a plain time-based cooldown window: it bounds the worst case regardless of how many orders accumulate (more orders = slower rotation, never more requests), same doctrine as `MAX_MANUAL_CANDIDATES_PER_CYCLE`. Only applies to `watching` orders that go through the RSI-divergence path (costly, GeckoTerminal) -- a plain `watching` order (price comparison only, `check_watching_order`, no real network cost of its own) is never affected.
`limit_orders.py` -- 5 new tests (`test_limit_orders.py`: cap respected per pass, rotation genuinely covers every order across several passes, resolved ids purged, plain orders never capped, rotation-state reset fixture between tests), full suite rerun before deployment.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : vanguard/backend geckoterminal.py -- 4th client found, coordinated but never authenticated
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : same Workflow as the entry above, "exhaustive audit" branch -- a 4th real HTTP call site to GeckoTerminal existed, in `vanguard/backend/app/services/geckoterminal.py::GeckoTerminalClient._fetch_raw` (used by the web backend, distinct from the aria-core monorepo). It did correctly respect the shared throttle (`wait_for_shared_rate_limit()`, so no independent lock that would have accelerated the cumulative throughput) but never sent the `x-cg-demo-api-key` header -- so it was actually pulling at the keyless rate against this same lock calibrated for authenticated traffic. Same bug as the one already fixed the same day on `aria_core/services/ohlcv.py`, never replicated here.
Solution : added the same pattern (`os.environ.get("COINGECKO_DEMO_API_KEY", "").strip()`, `x-cg-demo-api-key` header when present) -- 3 lines, no throttle change needed (already coordinated).
`vanguard/backend/app/services/geckoterminal.py` -- new dedicated test file (`vanguard/backend/tests/test_geckoterminal_client.py`, 5 tests: key sent when configured, absent otherwise, stray whitespace stripped, Accept header always present, candle parsing/sorting unchanged), full vanguard backend suite green (230 passed).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : base_crawler.py -- 3rd GeckoTerminal client, entirely uncoordinated, never authenticated
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : the GeckoTerminal 429 lockout persisted despite every fix shipped that day (cache, API key on ohlcv.py, throughput restored, manual backlog drained and capped). Research Workflow (2 agents) launched on the mystery -- finding confirmed in the code: `base_crawler.py::_fetch_gt` is a THIRD GeckoTerminal client, entirely independent from `services/geckoterminal.py` and `services/ohlcv.py` -- never authenticated (`COINGECKO_DEMO_API_KEY` never sent, so the public tier documented as "5-15 req/min, variable, shared globally by IP", not the fixed ~30/min Demo tier) AND never coordinated with the shared throttle (`wait_for_shared_rate_limit`) every other caller already respects. Same incident class as the one from 21/07 already documented in CLAUDE.md ("two independent GeckoTerminal clients... never coordinated with each other"). Called on every discovery cycle (one of the 6 automated sources), this client alone could explain sustained pressure independent of every fix already applied.
Solution : `_fetch_gt` now calls `wait_for_shared_rate_limit()` before every request and sends `x-cg-demo-api-key` when `COINGECKO_DEMO_API_KEY` is configured -- same pattern as `geckoterminal.py`/`ohlcv.py`.
`base_crawler.py` -- 3 new tests (`test_base_crawler.py`: shared throttle called, key sent when configured, absent otherwise), full suite green (8777 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `42ce3453`). Real effect on the 429 rate to be confirmed after an observation window.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Per-cycle cap on manual candidates (/add) -- fixes the structural cause of the previous chantier
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : direct follow-up to the "manual backlog of 479 contracts" chantier (previous entry) -- the queue had been drained manually, but the real structural cause (no cap on the number of manual candidates processed per cycle) stayed in the code. Explicit operator decision ("cap the number processed per cycle") after ruling out a first option (dropping a candidate from the queue as soon as it enters the 2000-slot honeypot watchlist) -- rejected because it would defeat the real purpose of `/add` (tracking a token until a buy signal, not just until its honeypot status is known).
Solution : new `MAX_MANUAL_CANDIDATES_PER_CYCLE = 15` in `momentum_entry.py` -- only the 15 oldest manual candidates (already sorted `ORDER BY added_at ASC`) are injected into discovery each cycle, always leaving the rest of the budget (50/cycle) to the automated sources. A future bulk addition now drains progressively over several cycles instead of monopolizing the whole budget at once. `reconcile_watchlist_membership` (honeypot watchlist status healing, zero network cost) still runs on the FULL backlog, never just the capped subset -- a candidate not drawn this cycle still gets its honeypot status kept up to date.
`momentum_entry.py` -- 2 new tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: cap respected + automated source never fully starved, `reconcile_watchlist_membership` still sees the full backlog), full suite green (8772 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `d495cac9`).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : GeckoTerminal throttle restored after a 429 burst -- real root cause found (manual backlog of 479 contracts)
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : direct follow-up to the same day's temporary throughput reduction (-50%). The slowdown alone never fully lifted the Cloudflare block -- dug further: `manual_candidate_queue` (the `/add` queue) held 479 contracts, all injected in a single burst at 10:00 UTC (the operator sent a screenshot listing hundreds of tokens to ARIA, extracted all at once instead of the normal 1-2). `discover_momentum_candidates` ALWAYS places manual candidates at the head of the list, up to saturating the 50/cycle cap -- documented and INTENDED behavior since 30/07 for normal usage (1-2 contracts), but which becomes a genuine structural overload with 479 at once: every discovery cycle since that morning spent a large share of its OHLCV fetch quota on this backlog rather than on genuinely new market tokens, inflating the real volume sent to GeckoTerminal well beyond what the calibrated throttle anticipated.
Solution : (1) `_AUTHENTICATED_MIN_INTERVAL`/`_MIN_INTERVAL` (`geckoterminal.py`) restored to their nominal value (2.857s, ~21 req/min) once the real 429 rate fell back to 0-1/10min for over an hour of continuous observation. (2) `manual_candidate_queue` cleared manually (479 -> 0, explicit operator decision) -- 444 of the 479 remain known in `goplus_watchlist` (2000 slots, separate table, cached honeypot status), no useful security information lost. Structural cause (manual prioritization with no dedicated cap) not yet fixed in the code -- remains a real recurrence risk if a future bulk addition via `/add` happens again, backlog noted for a future chantier.
`services/geckoterminal.py` -- geckoterminal+ohlcv+momentum_entry+coherence suite green (478 passed), deployed.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : cap_alloc_to_price_impact ignored the scalping swap fee -- final R/R could collapse well below its own floor
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : found live by the operator (PLAY scalping position, sized at $277 instead of a normal size, "pourquoi cest position sont minuscule"). Diagnosis: `cap_alloc_to_price_impact` computes a size cap to guarantee R/R >= `PRICE_IMPACT_MIN_RR` (1.0) after the order's own price impact -- but it never accounted for the DEX swap fee (`DEX_SWAP_FEE_PCT`, 1%, scalping mode only) that `simulated_fill_price` applies right after, on the SAME allocation, to compute the price ACTUALLY paid. On an already-tight setup, this unanticipated 1% was enough to drop the final structural R/R well below the intended floor (0.067 observed in prod on PLAY, vs. 1.0 expected) -- confirmed by hand-calculation on a reproducible case (0.632 without the fix, exactly 1.0 with it).
Solution : new `apply_swap_fee` parameter on `cap_alloc_to_price_impact` (same signature/doctrine as `simulated_fill_price`, same fee-then-impact ordering) -- `paper_trader.open_position` now passes `apply_swap_fee=(mode == "scalping")` to BOTH calls (already done for `simulated_fill_price`, missing for the size cap). Default `False` = unchanged historical behavior for every existing caller (swing/vc, never affected by this fee). Fail-open on unknown liquidity deliberately kept identical (no scope extension beyond the reported bug).
`risk_guard.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- 4 new tests (`test_risk_guard.py`: default behavior unchanged, stricter cap with the fee, end-to-end integration confirming final R/R == exactly 1.0, total rejection when the fee alone already breaks the floor), full suite green (8769 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `ccd01ff3`).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : GeckoTerminal throttle reduced 50% temporarily (1h, operator decision)
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : persistent HTTP 429 burst despite fixing the real duplication bug (`_gates_cache`) and the missing API key (`ohlcv.py`) -- direct empirical test (curl from the container) confirms a Cloudflare 429 block even with/without the key, generic "Rate Limit exceeded" message. Real throughput measured at the moment of the block: ~3.7 req/min, already well below the calibrated 21/min -- consistent with a Cloudflare cooldown penalty following a PAST excess (before the fixes), not an ongoing continuous overshoot. Explicit operator decision: "reduit le debit de 50% pour soulager temporairement le debit des api" + "et dici 1 heure tu le remettre comme avant" (reduce throughput by 50% to temporarily relieve API load, restore it after 1 hour).
Solution : `_AUTHENTICATED_MIN_INTERVAL`/`_MIN_INTERVAL` (`services/geckoterminal.py`) temporarily doubled (2.857s -> 5.714s, ~21/min -> ~10.5/min) -- `services/ohlcv.py` inherits it automatically via the shared throttle (`use_shared_throttle=True`), a single coordination point changed. A de-escalation gesture, NOT a proven fix -- explicitly documented as reversible, nominal value to restore and the planned time (~16:46 UTC) written as a code comment so a future session doesn't forget it.
`services/geckoterminal.py` -- geckoterminal+ohlcv+momentum_entry+coherence suite green (478 passed), full suite green (8765 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `ba85480f`). **Restoration scheduled ~16:46 UTC within the same session segment.**

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : services/ohlcv.py never sent the COINGECKO_DEMO_API_KEY -- ran on the anonymous tier
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : found live during a slowdown diagnosis (operator: "sa trade beaucoup moin depuis 14h", GeckoTerminal 429 burst). `services/geckoterminal.py` has correctly sent `x-cg-demo-api-key` since 18/07, but `services/ohlcv.py` (a SEPARATE client, the one generating most of the 429s -- 55/87 on a 20min sample) never did: `headers={"Accept": "application/json"}` hardcoded, never updated when authentication was added. It was therefore running at the anonymous rate (~10 req/min, figure verified during the 19/07 incident) instead of the authenticated Demo rate (~30 req/min) its own shared throttle (`use_shared_throttle=True`) already targeted.
**False lead avoided along the way**: the operator's CoinGecko dashboard showed "100 req/min" for their Demo tier -- wrongly interpreted as applying to GeckoTerminal. The code has carried, since 19/07, the exact warning against this confusion (an identical real incident had produced 79% 429 failure in prod for 1h+ on this same false assumption): GeckoTerminal `/onchain` has its OWN rate grid, distinct from CoinGecko's general table -- ~30 req/min with a Demo key, ~10/min without. The existing calibration (21 req/min, 70% margin on 30) therefore stays correct and was not changed.
Solution : `services/ohlcv.py::_get_json` now builds the headers with `x-cg-demo-api-key` if `COINGECKO_DEMO_API_KEY` is configured (same pattern as `geckoterminal.py`), never fabricated if absent. Key confirmed present in prod (verified via the container, value never displayed). Expected effect: this module moves from the anonymous rate to the Demo rate, a real gain (~3x) on the service that produced the most 429s -- not a miracle cure, but a real bug fixed.
`services/ohlcv.py` -- 2 new tests (`test_ohlcv_client.py`: header sent when key configured, absent otherwise), full suite green (8765 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `c937956e`).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Per-cycle cache on _gates_and_candles -- the 5 scalping variants no longer re-fetch the same data 5x
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : found live by the operator ("j'ai l'impression que sa trade beaucoup moin depuis 14h"). Confirmed: near-continuous GeckoTerminal HTTP 429 burst from ~12:54 onward, coinciding exactly with the switch to 5 active scalping pockets. Root cause in the code: each variant (`evaluate_v1_bollinger`..`evaluate_v5_vwap_trailing`) calls its OWN `_gates_and_candles(contract, chain)` -- `paper_trader.py` treats the 5 pockets as 5 independent passes over the SAME `momentum_candidates` list, so each candidate triggered `evaluate_hard_gates` 5x + `_fetch_candles` 5x (GeckoTerminal/CoinMarketCap/Mobula/DexPaprika cascade) for STRICTLY IDENTICAL data (same contract/chain/mode="scalping") -- a direct violation of the "Sobriété" doctrine (never duplicate a call), never anticipated while building the 5-pocket architecture that very morning.
Solution : short cache (`_gates_cache`, 120s TTL, key `(contract, chain)`) in `skills/scalping_variants.py` -- the first call for a candidate populates the cache, the next 4 (the other 4 variants) reuse the same `(pair, candles, hold)` with no network call. TTL scoped to a single cycle (the 5 sequential passes follow each other within seconds to a few minutes), never across cycles -- breaks no freshness guarantee (the real execution price is still separately re-checked right before the buy, step 9 of the momentum process). Opportunistic pruning past 500 entries (no dedicated background task). **Trap found while writing the tests**: every `test_scalping_variants.py` test reuses the same `CONTRACT`/`CHAIN` -- without a clear fixture, the cache would have leaked the 1st test's mock into every following one (false greens/false reds) -- `autouse` fixture added.
`skills/scalping_variants.py` -- 4 new tests (`test_scalping_variants.py`: reuse confirmed with no re-fetch, scoping by contract/chain, pruning past the threshold, no-op below the threshold), full suite green (8763 passed, 17 skipped), deployed (commit `3c5a1294`).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : all_reporting_wallets() -- pocket retired from sourcing (legacy "scalping") disappears from balance/risk views
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : found live by the operator (`/feedback` screenshot: 7 pockets displayed -- V1-V5+Swing+VC -- "je le voit pas" for the legacy RSI scalping wallet, which still had 7 open positions/33 closed ones). Root cause: `all_pocket_wallets()` (created the same day, next entry) serves both as the source of truth for SOURCING (correct that it excludes "scalping" once the 5 variants are active) and for REPORTING/RISK (wrong -- a pocket retired from sourcing still manages its already-open positions and must stay visible/actionable). Real impact beyond mere display: the MACRO circuit breaker (`risk_guard.evaluate_macro_risk`) undercounted total portfolio equity (blind to ~$1M of the legacy pocket), and `/riskresume` would have had no way to lift a circuit breaker triggered on this pocket before its retirement.
Solution : new `paper_trader.all_reporting_wallets()` (async) -- dynamic union of `all_pocket_wallets()` (active pockets) and `SELECT DISTINCT wallet FROM paper_state` (any wallet with a real history row, regardless of whether it still sources). Stable order: active pockets first (never reordered), retired pocket(s) after. Replaces the 4 report/risk usages: `risk_guard.evaluate_macro_risk`, `paper_ledger_report.build_report`, `telegram_bot._feedback_reply` (`/feedback` summary, also fixes the hardcoded "3 portefeuilles" pocket count -> dynamic) and `telegram_bot._handle_risk_resume` (`/riskresume`). `all_pocket_wallets()` stays unchanged and continues to gate SOURCING alone.
`paper_trader.py`/`risk_guard.py`/`paper_ledger_report.py`/`gateway/telegram_bot.py` -- 2 new tests (`test_all_reporting_wallets_matches_pocket_wallets_when_no_legacy_row`, `test_all_reporting_wallets_includes_retired_pocket_with_history`), paper_trader+risk_guard+paper_ledger_report+feedback+riskresume suite green (491 passed), full suite rerun before deployment.

[CODE] Subject  : 5 scalping variants (V1-V5, %B/VWAP/Stochastic) -- A/B/C/D/E comparison
Date : 2026.08.01 / Problem : follow-up to the scalping diagnostic (stagnation timeout, same day) -- the operator provided a spec (5 distinct mean-reversion strategies: Bollinger %B, VWAP Z-score, fast Stochastic %K, combo, VWAP+trailing) to objectively compare several entry signals on the same candidate flow, rather than iterating blindly on the current RSI-only signal. Scope explicitly settled with the operator before coding: current micro-cap sourcing UNCHANGED (the Mid/Large-cap $50M-$1B filter from the same spec is set aside -- a full sourcing pivot, out of this chantier's scope), 5 pockets (not 6 -- the classic RSI scalping is REPLACED, not complemented).
Solution : new indicators `bollinger_percent_b`/`vwap_series`/`vwap_zscore_series`/`stochastic_k_series` (`indicators.py`, Bollinger already present). New `skills/scalping_variants.py` -- 5 deterministic functions (never an LLM, consistent with the "ARIA must be first" doctrine) reusing the SAME hard guardrails as RSI scalping (`evaluate_hard_gates`, mode="scalping") + the shared anti-dump doctrine (never buy in full oversold, only on exit confirmation). Deliberate, documented simplification: position management AFTER entry (ATR trailing stop, TP tiers, stagnation timeout) stays the generic engine already in place for every variant -- V3 ("%K>=85") and V5 ("no TP, exits on reversal") are therefore approximated via their initial target/invalidation rather than a dedicated exit loop per variant (reimplementing the exit 5x would have been disproportionate for a first comparison). New gate `ARIA_SCALPING_VARIANTS_ENABLED` -- replaces the "scalping" pocket with 5 independent pockets (`scalping_v1`..`scalping_v5`, $1M each). **Real security bug found and fixed the same day, before considering the chantier done**: 4 pocket lists hardcoded elsewhere in the code (`risk_guard._MACRO_POCKETS` -- the MACRO circuit breaker would have summed equity over only 3 of the 7 real pockets, blind to the 5 variants' capital; `paper_ledger_report._POCKETS`; `telegram_bot.py`'s `/portfolio` display; `_RISK_RESUME_POCKETS` -- `/riskresume` would have had no way to lift a circuit breaker on a variant, blocking it until the weekly reset) -- all replaced by a single source of truth, `paper_trader.all_pocket_wallets()`. `indicators.py`/`skills/scalping_variants.py` (new)/`paper_trader.py`/`risk_guard.py`/`paper_ledger_report.py`/`gateway/telegram_bot.py` -- dedicated tests (`test_indicators.py`, `test_scalping_variants.py` new, `test_paper_trader.py`), full suite 8754 passed / 17 skipped, `test_coherence.py` green (gate registry updated in the same commit). **Fixed 08/02** (general audit after the scalping_v6 merge): this sweep was NOT exhaustive -- `heartbeat.py::paper_weekly_review_cycle` and 5 sites in `limit_orders.py` still tested `wallet == "scalping"` literally, never covered here. See the dedicated entry higher up in this file ("6 more 'scalping' == literal regressions...").

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Swing limit orders now confirm entry timing via fine-grained (scalping-scale) RSI divergence, not plain price
Date : 2026.07.31 / Probleme : operator's explicit design request -- swing detects a setup on big timeframes (day/4h/1h) + LLM confirms, then "scalping" (the fine-grained lens, 15-30min candles) should confirm the precise entry timing within the already-targeted golden-pocket zone before buying -- rather than the existing mechanism, which triggered a swing limit order the instant price simply returned to the target level, no matter how it got there.
Solution : `check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` (Item #183, 28/07 -- already existed but was scoped to ONE narrow case, `limit_order_reason == "rsi_divergence_pending"`, and used `mode="standard"` for swing) now (1) runs for EVERY swing order in "watching", regardless of `limit_order_reason` (price-drift #175 and golden-pocket-not-yet-formed orders included, not just the original divergence-pending case), and (2) uses `watch_mode="scalping"` (fine-grained candles) for swing, same as the scalping pocket's own orders -- was "standard" before, the exact inverse of what's needed. Scalping's own orders (its own independent trading, unrelated capital) are completely unaffected -- this only changes how a SWING order's watch phase resolves, never scalping's own decisions or the scalping wallet's positions. `format_limit_order_placed_alert` updated to disclose the new mechanism honestly for the price-drift/golden-pocket branch too (previously only the rsi_divergence_pending branch explained the divergence-based wait -- the generic branch would have silently kept claiming a plain price trigger, same class of misleading gap already fixed once, 29/07, for the other branch).
`limit_orders.py` -- 5 tests updated (2 pinned to `wallet="vc"` to keep covering the still-unchanged plain-price mechanism, 1 renamed to assert the new "scalping" mode instead of "standard", 1 adapted to mock the new divergence check) + 4 new tests (swing triggers on confirmed divergence / waits without one / cancels via the same path / VC's own mode unaffected), full limit_orders+paper_trader+momentum_websocket+test_coherence suite green (523 passed), full suite green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Per-cycle candidate cap 20->50, prioritized by scan staleness, + WebSocket hourly ceiling 80->200
Date : 2026.07.31 / Probleme : operator's explicit requests, same conversation as the watchlist 600->2000 raise -- "met le a 50 mais idem toujours les derniers 50 tokens scanne en dernier pour etre sur que tous les token passe au scan" (both the periodic heartbeat's `_momentum_candidates_and_chain_map(limit=20)` and the WebSocket's `MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_DRAIN=20` silently starved candidates beyond the cap forever if the same well-liquid tokens kept resurfacing first in the raw discovery order) then, after verifying `MAX_EVALUATIONS_PER_HOUR=80` (WebSocket) against the real GeckoTerminal ceiling (~1620/h calibrated, docs/api-rate-limit-calibration.md), "oui" to raising it to 200.
Solution : (1) `paper_trader._momentum_candidates_and_chain_map`'s default and all 3 call sites, plus `momentum_websocket.MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_DRAIN`, raised 20->50. (2) new `momentum_scan_log.last_scan_map(pairs)` -- one batched query (never N sequential ones) returning the most recent `scanned_at` per (contract, chain); `_momentum_candidates_and_chain_map` now sorts the full discovery list by this (never-scanned first, then oldest-scanned first -- same round-robin doctrine as `goplus_watchlist.next_due`) BEFORE truncating to the limit, so a candidate stuck past the cap for several cycles in a row now gets priority instead of being starved indefinitely. Best-effort: a lookup failure degrades to the raw discovery order, never blocks a cycle. (3) `MAX_EVALUATIONS_PER_HOUR` 80->200 -- verified real ceiling first: each drained candidate costs 2 GeckoTerminal calls (scalping+swing evaluated separately), so even at 200/h that's ~400 GeckoTerminal calls/h, ~25% of its calibrated 1620/h capacity (was ~10% at 80). Flagged, NOT resolved: the LLM call now made for every swing setup (same-day R/R floor removal) has no calibrated rate/quota anywhere in this codebase -- could become the real bottleneck before GeckoTerminal, to observe over the following days.
`momentum_scan_log.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`momentum_websocket.py` -- 11 new tests (`last_scan_map` batched lookup: empty/never-scanned/most-recent/cross-chain/case-insensitive/many-pairs; discovery prioritization: starved-candidate-wins/fallback-on-failure), full paper_trader+momentum_websocket+momentum_scan_log+test_coherence suite green (437 passed), full suite green (8652 passed, 17 skipped).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Swing R/R floor removed + liquidity 50k->25k + GoPlus watchlist 600->2000 slots
Date : 2026.07.31 / Probleme : operator's explicit, direct requests -- "enleve le r/r minimum et baisser la liquidite a 25k sur swing" then "augmente la taille de 600 a 2000 on fera le trie au fur et a mesure" -- widen swing's discovery funnel (fewer candidates gated out at the door) while relying on the honeypot watchlist and the always-on LLM confirmation to sort quality after the fact rather than before.
Solution : (1) swing (mode != "scalping") no longer has any R/R floor -- neither the deterministic direct-buy path (`_RR_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY`/`_ALIGN_SCORE_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY`, now gated `mode == "scalping"`) nor the pure-HOLD-for-weak-R/R branch (`_RR_AMBIGUOUS_FLOOR`, now only reachable by scalping) -- every swing setup with a formed technical signal always goes through `_llm_confirm_and_gate` (confirmation + security fused), even with a weak/negative R/R, clarified via an explicit operator answer ("toujours confirmation LLM") to a clarifying question asked before implementing. Scalping's own 2.0/1.0 thresholds fully unchanged. Same rationale as the same-day removal of the limit-order R/R floor (Item #252): entry R/R doesn't bound the trailing-stop exit's upside. (2) `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` 50,000->25,000 (swing/standard only, `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD_FEAR` deliberately left at 100,000 -- now x4 instead of the x2 calibrated 20-21/07, a known accepted side effect, operator only asked about the standard floor). (3) `goplus_watchlist.MAX_WATCHLIST_SIZE` 600->2000 -- the old 288s/token GoPlus-only math this number was originally calibrated against is historical (Honeypot.is is now the primary source, batched 100/passage at ~5min cadence -- draining 2000 slots takes ~20 passages, ~1h40, comfortably inside the 48h freshness window); every hardcoded "600 slots" mention in comments/log messages/the /goplusqueue help text updated to match (one made dynamic via the real constant instead of a second hardcoded literal, to prevent this exact kind of drift recurring).
`momentum_entry.py`/`goplus_watchlist.py`/`manual_candidates.py`/`gateway/telegram_bot.py`/`docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` (registry + `test_coherence.py::test_trading_thresholds_match_calibration_doc` updated in the same commit) -- ~15 existing tests adapted (either pinned to `mode="scalping"` where they exercised the now-scalping-only direct-buy/HOLD paths, or given an explicit `confirm_gate=` override) + 2 new tests covering swing's new always-LLM behavior, full suite green (8631+ passed, 17 skipped).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : GoPlus watchlist reconciliation for manual/screenshot candidates -- 97/161 silently missing a slot
Date : 2026.07.31 / Probleme : operator noticed the /add + screenshot discovery queue (`manual_candidate_queue`, 161 entries from a 30/07 screenshot batch) stayed at a constant count and asked to confirm/fix why. Investigation while answering found `add_manual_candidate` only ever ATTEMPTS the `goplus_watchlist` insert ONCE, at add-time (`add_or_touch` returning `False` when the watchlist was momentarily full and the neutral 0.0 score didn't beat the worst entry) -- a rejection is silent (no exception, just `return False`, never logged), and nothing ever retries. Confirmed live: 97 of 161 operator-submitted candidates never had a watchlist slot, even though hundreds of slots had since freed up (evictions via confirmed-honeypot removal, 157/600 occupied at diagnosis time).
Solution : one-off repair (`goplus_watchlist.add_or_touch` re-run for the 97 missing contracts, all 161 now present) + new `manual_candidates.reconcile_watchlist_membership(entries)` called from `discover_momentum_candidates` right after draining `manual_entries` every cycle (~15min) -- finds the genuinely-missing subset in one query (never rewrites an already-present entry's real priority score), retries only those, best-effort, zero network cost. — manual_candidates.py/momentum_entry.py, 4 new tests (heal/skip-present/empty/tolerate-failure), full manual_candidates+momentum_entry+test_coherence suite green (17+417 passed).

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Item #231 -- R/R floor on limit-order candidates, differentiated by pocket
Date : 2026.07.30 / Probleme : operator report while watching a live scalping alert (wIRON: R/R=0.3, +1.0% target vs -3.7% invalidation -- risking ~3.3x what's on the table). Unlike a direct/LLM-confirmed buy, a limit-order candidate (price-drift #175, golden-pocket-not-yet-formed #182, rsi-divergence-pending #183) never had any R/R floor -- it could sit there waiting to trigger a mathematically unjustifiable trade.
Solution : new `limit_orders.meets_limit_order_rr_floor(rr, wallet)` -- scalping >=1.25 (calibrated empirically against 67 resolved scalping orders: real median R/R among actual triggers was 1.75, so reusing the 2.0 direct-buy floor would have rejected half of them; cross-checked against published scalping/15min norms, 1:1-1:1.5 baseline / 1.5-2x on 15min specifically), swing >=2.0 (reuses momentum_entry's own direct-buy floor -- zero resolved swing limit orders exist yet to calibrate against, matches the published 1:2-1:3 swing-timeframe norm). `rr=None` (degenerate setup) fails closed. Checked in `paper_trader.py` right before `create_pending_order` -- rejected candidates never count against any dedup state. — limit_orders.py/paper_trader.py, 6 unit tests + 1 integration rejection test, full suite green (8491 passed).

[CODE] Subject  : Item #227 -- historical trigger rate displayed on limit-order alerts (base rate, never a per-candidate forecast)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Probleme : operator request while watching the live SHIB scalping limit-order alert ("idem je veut une probabilité sur les ordre limite, le taux de chance de reussite que la divergence apparaisse et que tu le voit dans tes log") -- a limit order's alert showed the R/R and the target price, but nothing about how often orders of that same kind actually go on to trigger, even though `pending_limit_order` already resolves every order to `triggered`/`cancelled`/`expired` and was queryable for exactly this.
Solution : new `historical_trigger_rate(reason)` in `limit_orders.py` -- reads every already-resolved order sharing the SAME `limit_order_reason` (`None` for the price-drift path, #175, which never tags one), returns `triggered_count / total` as a plain historical base rate across ALL past orders of that kind -- explicitly NOT a per-candidate prediction (no model conditions it on this specific setup's own features). Below `_MIN_HISTORICAL_TRIGGER_SAMPLE=10` resolved orders of that reason, returns `(None, sample_size)` -- degrades honestly to "pas assez d'historique (N ordres résolus)" in the alert rather than a precise-looking percentage computed on a handful of data points (e.g. 1/2 = 50% means nothing). Both limit-order creation sites in `paper_trader.py` (identical code, `replace_all` edit) now call it right after `estimated_conviction_tier` is set, persisting `historical_trigger_rate`/`historical_trigger_sample` onto the order's `signal_json` so `format_limit_order_placed_alert` can display it without a fresh DB read at render time. `limit_orders.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- new tests for `historical_trigger_rate` (below-sample/above-sample/reason-filtering) + alert-formatting tests, full suite green (8483 passed, 17 skipped).

[CODE] Subject  : Bonding rework v2 -- empirical composite-score unlock + sizing caps + VC/limit-order wiring + staleness (Items #155-158/#161-162/#165/#167)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Probleme : an empirical pass against ~380 real live Base bonding candidates found the #152/28/07 composite score was UNREACHABLE in practice -- 0/380 ever reached the 60/100 threshold. Two remaining structural hard gates were the cause, neither previously checked against a live sample: `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` (10,000$) sat just above a BIMODAL launch-config artifact (92.7% of fresh tokens cluster at ~9,591$, a fixed initial-deposit amount, not a market signal); the holder-concentration hard reject (top10 > 80% once holder_count >= 50) never observed a real token below ~93.8% concentration even at 1000+ holders, contradicting its own single-example justification. A third gate ("no candles", fewer than 5 real trades) was nearly perfectly correlated with the liquidity gate. Separately, no plausibility cap existed on position size relative to a bonding token's fixed supply, the limit-order mechanism was never made bonding-aware (would have called GoPlus with a fake "chain", and skipped the #156 sizing steps entirely), the VC pocket never saw bonding candidates despite its exit design (Take-Seed, long horizon) fitting them far better than scalping/swing (both reset weekly), and nothing decayed a bonding candidate's score for having aged well past a typical bonding window without graduating.
Solution : (1) Item #167 -- `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` lowered 10,000$->5,000$ (still a real floor above the near-zero/corrupted-data range, just no longer vetoing the standard launch configuration itself); the top10-concentration hard gate REMOVED, downgraded to a pure score component (`_TOP10_HOLDER_PCT_SCORE_FLOOR=90.0` full points, `_MAX_TOP10_HOLDER_PCT=100.0` zero points, linear between); "no candles" narrowed to "no trades" (a real entry price is all that's structurally required, `detect_entry` already degrades gracefully on an empty candle list). (2) Item #156 -- `bonding_entry.cap_alloc_to_supply_pct` (tiered 5%/2.5%/1% by conviction, `_MAX_SUPPLY_PCT_BY_TIER`) applied on top of `BONDING_SIZE_REDUCTION` in both the direct-buy path (`paper_trader._open_new_entries_for_wallet`) and the limit-order trigger (`limit_orders._execute_trigger`) -- needs `VirtualToken.total_supply` (real API field, "totalSupply", never captured before). (3) Item #157 -- new `paper_trader._vc_analyzer_with_bonding` routes a bonding-tagged candidate to `evaluate_bonding_entry` for the VC pocket too (never `_default_analyzer`, structurally inapplicable); bonding candidates already tagged in `_momentum_chain_by_contract` are appended to `vc_candidates` -- cross-pocket overlap (same contract open in scalping/swing/vc at once) is an accepted, tracked feature of the 3-pocket architecture, not a bug. (4) Item #158 -- `limit_orders.should_place_limit_order` gains a bonding-only market-cap-proxy floor (`BONDING_LIMIT_ORDER_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD=20,000$`, liquidity_usd as proxy, same doctrine as bonding_entry.py's own liquidity gate); `reanalyze_for_watching` routes a bonding order to a new `_reanalyze_bonding_for_watching` (re-checks dev-holding/liquidity natively, never GoPlus); `_execute_trigger` now applies the same `BONDING_SIZE_REDUCTION`/`cap_alloc_to_supply_pct`/late-cycle steps as the direct-buy path. (5) Items #161/#162 -- `_staleness_penalty_multiplier` shaves up to 50% off the ALREADY-COMPUTED composite score once a bonding token has aged past 30 days without graduating (full decay at 45 days, `_STALENESS_DAYS_THRESHOLD`/`_STALENESS_MAX_DAYS`/`_STALENESS_MAX_PENALTY_PCT`) -- waived entirely by a genuine dated catalyst (`conviction_research.py`'s `posting_cadence == "active"`, Item #162's guardrail), using `VirtualToken.launched_at`. (6) Item #165 -- `bonding_entry.late_cycle_size_multiplier` (0.7x, tighten-only) reads `skills/btc_cycles.py`'s long-term halving-cycle phase (`fetch_current_macro_phase`, own 1h cache -- never adds real network load beyond the cache window), applied in both sizing paths alongside the other bonding-only reductions. A pre-existing test-isolation gap found while verifying all this (not introduced by this session): several `test_paper_trader.py` tests never mocked `_bonding_candidates`/`btc_cycles.fetch_current_macro_phase`, causing a real, hanging network call under some sandbox conditions -- fixed with 2 new autouse fixtures (neutral by default, overridden locally by the tests that specifically exercise those paths) in `test_paper_trader.py`/`test_limit_orders.py`.
`bonding_entry.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`limit_orders.py`/`services/virtuals.py` -- registry `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` + mechanical guardrail `test_coherence.py::test_trading_thresholds_match_calibration_doc` updated in the same commit for every changed/new constant. Full suite green (518 tests across the 5 touched files + test_coherence.py, 71s), full `test_coherence.py` green.

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject  : Items #163/#166 -- evaluated, deliberately NOT built (same bonding rework as above)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Probleme : Item #163 asked whether post-graduation warning signals (smart-money exit, exchange balances, revenue collapse) were worth a 4th bonding volet; Item #166 asked whether a "second pump 1-2 months after the first dump" pattern had real empirical backing before building anything on it.
Solution : #163 -- smart-money-exit already exists as an ENTRY signal (`smart_money.py`) but is wired nowhere as an active position-EXIT check (bonding or standard momentum alike) -- a real gap, but out of this chantier's scope (generic, not bonding-specific). Exchange-balances/revenue-collapse need data sources not currently available/verified for free -- deferred, never guessed at. #166 -- a quick web check found no solid evidence for this specific pattern on Virtuals-style bonding curves (Solana pump.fun literature actually argues the OPPOSITE: structural constraints make a second pump within the bonding phase unlikely) -- nothing built on an unverified pattern, consistent with this project's "measure before building" doctrine.

[CODE] Subject  : Item #128 -- cross-path evaluation dedup (periodic 30min scan vs WebSocket 30s drain)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Probleme : the periodic heartbeat cycle (`discover_momentum_candidates`) and the WebSocket drain (`momentum_websocket.py`) poll the SAME 4 DexScreener discovery endpoints (token-boosts/profiles latest+top+recent) -- confirmed by reading both call sites, not assumed. A trending token surfaces on both feeds around the same real-world time, so the slower periodic path (~30min) can re-run the ENTIRE expensive per-candidate pipeline (honeypot GoPlus, OHLCV, up to 2 LLM calls) on a candidate the WebSocket (~30s cadence) already judged moments earlier -- pure waste, no new information gained.
Solution : `momentum_timing.py` (already the neutral, dependency-free module shared by paper_trader.py/momentum_entry.py to avoid a circular import) gains a small in-process store: `record_evaluation(contract, chain, action)` / `recently_evaluated_action(contract, chain, window_seconds=15min)`. Written from `paper_trader._default_momentum_analyzer`'s `analyzer` closure -- the ONE place both the periodic cycle and the WebSocket drain actually call `evaluate_momentum_entry`, so a single write site covers both callers without touching either evaluation function's many internal early-returns. Consulted ONLY by the periodic discovery (`momentum_entry._add_candidate`) -- deliberately never by the WebSocket path itself: the WebSocket already has its OWN more nuanced adaptive cooldown (`RESCAN_COOLDOWN_SECONDS`, price-move-aware, a >10% move deliberately bypasses its own 4h cooldown) -- a symmetric check here would silently fight that bypass. A `None` verdict (evaluate_momentum_entry bailed early on missing price data, before the expensive steps ever ran) deliberately does NOT block a rescan -- little to protect, and the data may resolve moments later. In-process only, lost on restart with no correctness impact (same doctrine as `momentum_websocket`'s own `_seen`/`_pending` state). `momentum_timing.py`/`momentum_entry.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- 3 new tests in `test_momentum_timing.py` (9 total) + 2 new discovery-skip tests in `test_momentum_entry.py` + 2 new analyzer tests in `test_paper_trader.py`, all green; full suite + `test_coherence.py` to confirm before commit.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Per-signal alignment breakdown (align_ema/align_macd/align_pattern) -- queryable at scale, not just free-text
Date : 2026.07.27 / Problem : operator request while investigating a string of losing momentum trades ("ajoute des log supplementaires sur les donnees qu'il te manque sur les positions") -- `align_score` (0-3) is an aggregate, and every recent losing position happened to show `align_score=1`, but there was no queryable way to tell WHICH of the 3 signals (EMA/MACD/bullish pattern) was the one present without parsing free-text theses one by one.
Solution : `_technical_alignment()` now returns a 3rd element, a `detail` dict (`ema_above`/`macd_above`/`bullish_pattern`, each `True`/`False`/`None` -- `None` = warm-up/insufficient data, never treated as bearish by default, same doctrine as the aggregate score). `evaluate_momentum_entry()`'s result dict gained `align_ema`/`align_macd`/`align_pattern` keys straight from this detail. `paper_trader.py` gained matching `align_ema`/`align_macd`/`align_pattern` INTEGER columns (`_ADDED_COLUMNS`/`_ARCHIVE_ADDED_COLUMNS`/`_POS_FIELDS`, `open_position()` signature and INSERT SQL extended) so every future position persists the breakdown, never just the aggregate. `bonding_entry.py` (the only other caller of the shared `_technical_alignment`) updated in lockstep for the new 3-tuple return -- same function, single source, never a second copy. — momentum_entry.py/paper_trader.py/bonding_entry.py + test updates across test_momentum_entry.py/test_bonding_entry.py (3-tuple mocks), full suite green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Verified curiosity on Telegram images -- real on-chain check before replying (Item #106)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : the operator sent a screenshot of an X post about a token ($Stonkbroker) and asked "elle pourrait etre curieuse et verifier le graphique" -- `_handle_vision_photo` (gateway/telegram_bot.py) only ever answered from a pure visual read of the image (already flagged as non-verified by `vision_rule` in `brain.py::_llm_response`), with zero real on-chain lookup even when a ticker was clearly legible.
Solution : new `skills/image_token_curiosity.py` -- a narrow LLM vision call (`_extract_ticker`, ~20 tokens, structured `TICKER: <symbol>`/`AUCUN` output only, fully separate from the conversational `_llm_response`) reads a candidate ticker off the image; if found, `services/dexscreener.search_pairs(ticker)` (multi-chain, free, already existed) is queried and filtered to an EXACT symbol match, picking the dominant candidate only when its liquidity is at least 3x the runner-up's (`_DOMINANT_LIQUIDITY_RATIO`) -- otherwise explicitly reported as ambiguous rather than guessing which contract the image meant. On a clear match, GoPlus is queried for a real honeypot/tax reading (`_DEXSCREENER_TO_GOPLUS_CHAIN_ID` reused from momentum_entry.py, never a second mapping). The resulting block is injected via `_llm_response`'s existing `extra_system_context` seam (same one already used for VC-followup/ledger context) -- purely informational, never a gate on any real trade decision. Every step degrades honestly to an empty block on failure (LLM unavailable, no search result, ambiguous ticker, GoPlus down) -- never a guessed contract. Along the way, `services/dexscreener.PairSnapshot` gained a `chain_id` field (populated from DexScreener's own `chainId`, verified live present on every pair including multi-chain search results) -- previously silently discarded since every other caller of `_parse_pair` already knew its chain ahead of the call. — skills/image_token_curiosity.py (new)/services/dexscreener.py/gateway/telegram_bot.py + dedicated tests (test_image_token_curiosity.py, new, 22 tests) + additions to test_dexscreener_client.py/test_telegram_photo_handler.py, full suite green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Scalping exit -- bearish RSI divergence [60,80] closes the position (Item #105)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : the scalping mode (Item #101) only ever exits through the shared stop/TP mechanics (ATR trailing stop, R-multiple take-profit tiers) -- no dedicated technical exit mirroring the entry signal (bullish divergence [20,40]) existed for a fast intraday timeframe where a confirmed reversal at the top deserves an immediate exit distinct from a stop being hit.
Solution : new `bearish_rsi_divergence()` in entry_signals.py -- exact mirror of `bullish_rsi_divergence` (HIGH pivots instead of LOW, `RSI_EXIT_DIVERGENCE_MIN/MAX=[60,80]` instead of `[20,40]`). Wired into `_run_paper_cycle_locked`'s position-management loop: for any OPEN position with `mode="scalping"`, fresh scalping-timeframe candles are fetched (`momentum_entry._fetch_candles(..., mode="scalping")`) and checked; a confirmed bearish divergence closes the position immediately UNLESS the portfolio has already reached its daily profit target (`DAILY_FLOOR_TARGET_PROFIT_USD=75,000`, mid-point of the operator's $50-100k range) -- in that case the position is left alone (target already met, no need to force an exit on a still-live setup). `daily_target_reached` is resolved once per cycle from the GLOBAL `trading_mode` (portfolio-wide), never the position's own `mode` field, matching the same seam already used for regime/mode resolution elsewhere in the cycle. — skills/entry_signals.py/paper_trader.py + dedicated tests (test_entry_signals.py, test_paper_trader.py), full suite green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Sujet    : Mode scalping cable de bout en bout dans le pipeline momentum (Item #101, partiel)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Probleme : le test Milly (1M$) doit basculer a 100% scalping une fois pret (decision operateur explicite) -- aucune brique n'existait : ni bougies sous 1h (services/ohlcv.py plafonnait a 1D/4H/1H), ni parametre de mode dans momentum_entry.py, ni RSI calibre pour ce timeframe. Workflow de recherche dedie (3 agents, plafond du projet) a produit un tableau de reglages concret avant construction.
Solution : (1) `services/ohlcv.py` gagne un ladder dedie 15min->30min (`mode="scalping"`), verifie contre un VRAI appel GeckoTerminal (120 bougies 15min exactes sur un pool Base reel, WETH/USDC) -- ne degrade JAMAIS vers le ladder standard day/hour (melangerait des bougies day-scale avec un RSI calibre scalping, `available=False` honnete a la place). (2) `services/geckoterminal.py::GeckoTerminalClient.get_ohlcv` transmet desormais `mode` a son delegue (`services/ohlcv.py`). (3) `entry_signals.py` gagne `SCALPING_RSI_PERIOD=10` (vs 14 swing -- recherche : le forex-generique 5-9 est trop bruyant sur des memecoins deja volatils, 9-11 converge specifiquement avec du 15min) et un parametre `period` sur `detect_entry()`. (4) `momentum_entry.evaluate_momentum_entry()` gagne un parametre `mode="standard"` qui: route `_fetch_candles` vers le ladder scalping (et coupe court aux 4 fournisseurs de secours restants, aucun n'a de granularite sous l'heure) ; passe `SCALPING_RSI_PERIOD` a `detect_entry` ; desactive entierement `conviction_research`/son repli x402 payant (confirme sans valeur predictive a cet horizon ET sans pouvoir de veto dans le pipeline -- gain de vitesse/cout pur, jamais une regression de securite). Le mode est expose dans le dict retourne (`"mode"`) pour que paper_trader/la these sachent d'ou vient le signal. Les garde-fous durs (`evaluate_hard_gates`, honeypot inclus) restent identiques quel que soit le mode.
Reste a faire (Item #101 pas encore complet) : cablage paper_trader.py (sizing par trade adapte a ~100 trades/jour, MAX_POSITIONS desactivable en scalping, cooldown re-entree recalibre, simulation de frais/slippage cumules, these d'entree/sortie 100% transparente golden pocket), bascule reelle du test Milly. — services/ohlcv.py, services/geckoterminal.py, skills/entry_signals.py, momentum_entry.py + tests dedies (test_ohlcv_client.py, test_geckoterminal_client.py, test_entry_signals.py, test_momentum_entry.py), suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Sujet    : Chantier scalping complet -- cooldown, frais DEX, golden pocket temps reel (Item #101, fin)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Probleme : trois derniers manques restants du chantier scalping avant bascule reelle -- cooldown de re-entree calibre pour un rythme swing (2 pertes), aucun frais de swap DEX simule (seul l'impact de prix l'etait), et le canal relay n'exposait ni la zone golden pocket exacte ni les niveaux de sortie a jour.
Solution : (1) `SCALPING_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_LOSSES_PER_CONTRACT=3` (vs 2 swing) -- raisonnement statistique documente (win rate scalping ~50-65%, 2 pertes d'affilee arrivent par pur hasard ~25% du temps sur un contrat viable), applique uniquement quand `trading_mode="scalping"`. (2) `risk_guard.DEX_SWAP_FEE_PCT=1%` (sourcee Uniswap v3, tier standard pour paire volatile), nouveau parametre `apply_swap_fee` sur `simulated_fill_price`/`simulated_exit_price`, lu depuis le `mode` PERSISTE de la position (pas un parametre supplementaire a chaque appelant) dans `open_position`/`close_position`/`reduce_position` -- scope au mode scalping, le chemin standard deja en cours reste inchange au bit pres. (3) `EntrySignal` gagne `gp_low`/`gp_high` (bornes du golden pocket, jusque-la calculees mais jamais retournees), remontees dans le dict `momentum_entry`, persistees sur `paper_position`, citees avec leurs vraies valeurs numeriques dans le texte de these (`entry_signals.py`), et exposees dans `relay_conversation._position_facts_block` avec en plus le STOP ACTIF et les PALIERS DE TP recalcules EN TEMPS REEL (pas figes a l'achat) via les fonctions pures deja existantes (`_compute_active_stop`/`_effective_tp_stages`) -- uniquement sur une position encore ouverte. Bug trouve au passage sur le meme audit : `relay_conversation.py` citait aussi "5 trades" en dur pour le mode plancher (meme bug deja corrige dans `momentum_entry.py`), corrige a la vraie constante `DAILY_TRADE_FLOOR`. — paper_trader.py, risk_guard.py, skills/entry_signals.py, momentum_entry.py, relay_conversation.py + tests dedies (test_paper_trader.py, test_risk_guard.py, test_entry_signals.py, test_momentum_entry.py, test_relay_conversation.py), suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.
Item #101 desormais complet cote pipeline de decision -- reste seulement la bascule reelle du test Milly (`/mode scalping`, decision operateur).

[CODE] Sujet    : Bascule portefeuille-wide standard/scalping + MAX_POSITIONS libre en scalping (Item #101, suite)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Probleme : la primitive de decision (RSI/timeframe/conviction_research) etait prete mais rien ne permettait de choisir le mode ACTIF du test Milly, ni a l'operateur de le declencher -- trou trouve par l'operateur lui-meme ("comment je passe d'un mode a l'autre ?") apres que le code interne (`get_trading_mode`/`set_trading_mode`) ait ete construit SANS aucun point d'acces. Egalement : MAX_POSITIONS (plafond de positions simultanees, pense pour un rythme swing) devait etre laisse libre en scalping, decision operateur explicite ("laisse libre, voyons comment ARIA trade sans la force") -- jamais un chiffre cible de trades/jour a coder, juste observer le comportement naturel.
Solution : nouvelle colonne `paper_state.trading_mode` ('standard'/'scalping', portefeuille entier, jamais par position -- la bascule operateur est un remplacement complet, jamais un melange) + `get_trading_mode()`/`set_trading_mode()`. Resolu UNE FOIS par cycle dans `_run_paper_cycle_locked` (meme pattern que `current_regime`), transmis a `_default_momentum_analyzer`/`evaluate_momentum_entry`. MAX_POSITIONS ignore aux 2 niveaux de defense (boucle du cycle + `open_position()`) uniquement quand `mode="scalping"` -- inchange en mode standard. Nouvelle colonne `paper_position.mode` (comme `entry_regime`) persiste le mode de sourcing de chaque position. Point d'acces cree : commande Telegram `/mode [standard|scalping]` (admin-only, meme patron que `/langue`) -- sans argument affiche le mode courant, avec argument bascule et confirme ; aucune bascule automatique nulle part dans le code (uniquement operateur). — paper_trader.py, gateway/telegram_bot.py + tests dedies (test_paper_trader.py, test_telegram_trading_mode_command.py), suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

[CODE] Sujet    : Message "mode plancher" citait un chiffre en dur perime (5) au lieu du vrai palier (30)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Probleme : trouve via une capture d'ecran Telegram reelle fournie par l'operateur -- la these d'achat affichait "mode plancher (diagnostic 5 trades/jour)" alors que le vrai palier configure (`paper_trader.DAILY_TRADE_FLOOR`) avait ete releve a 30 dans le cadre de l'Item #100 (test agressif 24h). Le texte etait un litteral fige dans `momentum_entry.py`, jamais mis a jour lors du changement 5->30 -- ARIA elle-meme affichait donc un chiffre perime dans sa propre these d'entree.
Solution : import local (lazy, dans la fonction -- `paper_trader` importe deja `momentum_entry`, jamais l'inverse au chargement du module, donc aucun cycle) de `paper_trader.DAILY_TRADE_FLOOR`, le message cite desormais la vraie constante. Nouveau test dedie (`test_floor_mode_message_cites_the_real_daily_floor_constant`) verrouille l'invariant contre toute derive future. — momentum_entry.py/test_momentum_entry.py, suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Sujet    : Divergence RSI acceptee sans plage de survente reelle (39->40 pris pour une divergence)
Date : 2026.07.25 / Probleme : achat reel ZEN (screenshot Telegram fourni par l'operateur) declenche sur "RSI remonte (39 -> 40)" -- l'ancien critere de `bullish_rsi_divergence()` etait PUREMENT relatif (creux de prix plus bas + RSI du creux recent plus haut que le creux precedent), sans aucun plancher/plafond sur la valeur RSI elle-meme. 39/40 n'est pas une vraie zone de survente (le seuil classique de survente RSI est ~30, la zone de rebond visee ~20-40) -- deux points ou le second est marginalement plus haut suffisaient a valider une "divergence", quel que soit le niveau RSI reel. Verifie en reproduisant le vrai chemin de code (`momentum_entry._fetch_candles()`) sur le contrat ZEN reel : bougies 4H (cascade GeckoTerminal, `services/ohlcv.py`), confirmant que le calcul avait bien tourne sur des bougies 4H au moment de l'achat.
Solution : nouvelle contrainte `RSI_DIVERGENCE_MIN=20.0` / `RSI_DIVERGENCE_MAX=40.0` (bornes inclusives) sur la valeur RSI du creux RECENT (r2) dans `bullish_rsi_divergence()` -- hors de cette plage, rejet immediat avant meme la comparaison relative aux creux anterieurs. Timeframe-independant par construction (la fonction ne recoit que des `Candle`, jamais une notion de duree reelle -- meme regle sur 15min/1h/4h/jour, exigence operateur explicite : "que se soit n'importe quel timeframe la lecture doit etre la meme"). — skills/entry_signals.py, 3 nouveaux tests dedies (`test_bullish_divergence_rejected_when_recent_rsi_above_max`/`_below_min`/`test_bullish_divergence_accepted_at_range_boundaries`, monkeypatch de `rsi_series` pour controler directement la valeur RSI au pivot -- calibrer une vraie serie de prix pour un RSI pivot precis au-dessus de 40 s'est avere non-trivial, le RSI Wilder plafonne naturellement autour de ~38 des qu'un nouveau plus bas de prix est requis), suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

[CODE] Sujet    : Libelle "R/R faible" trompeur en mode plancher quand c'est en realite l'alignement technique qui manque
Date : 2026.07.25 / Probleme : trouve en testant le canal relay sur un 2e sujet different (OWB, position ouverte -19,3%) apres le correctif signal qualitatif -- la these d'entree affichait "R/R faible (50.8) accepte" alors qu'un R/R de 50.8 est enorme (largement au-dessus de `_RR_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY=2.0`). Cause reelle : la branche mode plancher (`elif relaxed:`) est atteinte des que la PREMIERE condition d'achat direct (`signal.rr >= 2.0 AND align_score >= 2`) est fausse -- pour n'importe laquelle des deux raisons (R/R insuffisant, alignement insuffisant, ou les deux). Le message generique supposait toujours que c'etait le R/R, meme quand -- comme sur OWB -- c'etait en realite l'alignement technique (`align_score < 2/3`) qui manquait malgre un R/R excellent. ARIA elle-meme, questionnee sur cette position via le relay, a pris ce libelle trompeur au pied de la lettre sans le remettre en question.
Solution : le message distingue desormais explicitement les 3 cas (R/R seul, alignement seul, les deux) avec les vrais chiffres de chacun -- generique par condition verifiee, jamais un pattern sur un cas precis. — momentum_entry.py, 3 nouveaux tests dedies (test_floor_mode_blames_*), suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Sujet    : Score fondamental catastrophique n'était jamais un veto d'achat, seulement une reduction de taille
Date : 2026.07.25 / Probleme : trouve en testant en conditions reelles le canal relay Claude<->ARIA (voir HANDOFF_TELEGRAM.md) -- questionnee sur sa position CHECK (-27,3%, -7 374$), ARIA a fini par identifier elle-meme que sa these d'entree contenait deja un score de potentiel fondamental confirme a 2,0/10 avec une rationale explicite ("contenu web incoherent et contrat different annonce signalent une usurpation probable"), jamais pris en compte au-dela d'une simple degradation de palier de conviction (`risk_guard.FUNDAMENTAL_WEAK_THRESHOLD=4.0`, jamais sous le palier faible -- l'achat a quand meme eu lieu sur le seul merite technique). Le pipeline bonding (`bonding_entry.py`) avait deja resolu ce probleme via un score composite pouvant rejeter (35/35/15/15, seuil 60/100) -- le chemin momentum standard (100% du capital du test 1M$) n'avait pas d'equivalent.
Solution : nouveau seuil `risk_guard.FUNDAMENTAL_REJECT_THRESHOLD=2.5`, distinct et plus strict que `FUNDAMENTAL_WEAK_THRESHOLD` -- sous ce seuil, `momentum_entry.py` rejette directement l'achat (`action="HOLD"`, `hold_reason="fundamental_score_critical"`), quel que soit le R/R ou l'alignement technique. Entre 2,5 et 4,0 : comportement inchange (degrade seulement le palier). Score absent (recherche indisponible/gate OFF) : jamais de rejet, doctrine fail-open inchangee. Generique par score numerique (jamais un filtrage sur les mots du texte de these) -- s'applique a tout token qui obtiendrait un score aussi bas, pas un patch specifique a CHECK. — risk_guard.py/momentum_entry.py, 3 nouveaux tests dedies + suite complete + test_coherence.py verts.

Entre 2,5 et 4,0/10, comportement historique inchange (taille seulement). `ARIA_MARKET_ALERTS_ENABLED` (Otto AI) reste cable dans le tie-breaker LLM du chemin ambigu mais OFF par defaut -- sans effet reel tant que ce gate n'est pas active.

[CODE] Subject  : Bonding entry -- dev-security/holder-concentration hard gates replaced by a weighted composite score (35/35/15/15, threshold 60/100)
Date : 2026.07.24 / Problem : the operator's own follow-up reasoning after the conviction-diligence chantier above: on a token this young, betting on the PRODUCT/TEAM should weigh at least as much as the (structurally thin) on-chain security signals, not be a secondary bonus. Separately, live exploration of the OFFICIAL Virtuals whitepaper (its own "Ask AI" tool) and the real launch form (`app.virtuals.io/create`, explored live, never submitted) confirmed WHY `dev_holding_pct = 0%`/`is_dev_committed = False` on 100/100 real bonding prototypes: team-allocation modules ("Automated Capital Formation"/"Pre-buy Token") are OFF by default, and even when on, team tokens are locked 1 year post-TGE then vested linearly over 6 months (independently cross-checked against an external LLM review before coding -- every structural claim confirmed exact). `dev_holding_pct = 0%` is the protocol's default state, not a virtue signal -- but it's still the best anti-rug signal available at this stage (meaningful the instant it ISN'T 0%), so it stays in the decision, just reweighted.
Solution : new composite score (operator-set weights, confirmed explicitly): 35% dev security (`dev_holding_pct`, scored `35 * (1 - pct/5.0)` once the existing hard reject at >5%/unknown has already been cleared), 35% product/team conviction (`potential_score` from `conviction_research.py` above, scored `potential_score * 3.5`, neutral half-score 17.5 when absent -- never a malus for a quiet/discreet team, per the operator's own explicit nuance), 15% technical setup (R/R margin above the 2.0 floor, 9 pts, + technical-alignment margin above the 2/3 floor, 6 pts -- both already hard-gated before this point, this only scores the MARGIN), 15% holder concentration (`top10_holder_pct`, neutral half-score 7.5 below `_MIN_HOLDERS_FOR_CONCENTRATION_CHECK`, scored normally above it -- the hard reject at >80% with enough holders is UNCHANGED). BUY only if the weighted total reaches `_SCORE_THRESHOLD` (60/100, an explicit starting value, permissive on purpose -- "on commence à soixante sur cent et on la laisse trader, si mauvais résultats on ajustera", same "measure before tightening" doctrine as every other starting constant in this pipeline). The STRUCTURAL hard gates (unknown/dangerous dev_holding_pct, real concentration once there's a big enough sample, insufficient liquidity, no real entry signal, rr/align below the direct-buy floor) are UNCHANGED -- they never became "just another scored component", only the FINAL judgment on what's left is now a weighted call instead of always-pass. `bonding_score` exposed on the returned dict (BUY and the new `score_below_threshold` HOLD alike) for full traceability. Re-tested live against the real bonding market after shipping: still zero rejections on holder concentration below the minimum sample (unchanged), the market currently too young to reach the scoring stage at all (most candidates still lack enough real trades) -- honest, not a regression. `bonding_entry.py` -- 5 new dedicated tests (weight/component sums, the operator's own worked example matched to the cent, a below-threshold HOLD, a product-conviction-vs-dev-security priority sanity check), full suite + `test_coherence.py` to confirm green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Bonding entry gains conviction diligence (product/team/GitHub) -- sizing only, never a gate
Date : 2026.07.24 / Problem : operator's own reasoning, right after seeing the concentration gate fail on the entire real bonding market (see entry below): on a token this young, on-chain metrics don't mean anything yet -- the real edge is a bet on the PRODUCT/TEAM/adoption potential ("elle doit miser + sur le produit, la team, et le potentiel de hausse... c'est une spéculation sur une adoption de la technologie"). Some teams have a real paying product with genuine market traction (operator's own example, HoloStudio/$HOLO); others have no product yet but real, sophisticated tech -- judged by actually reading the GitHub repo, not just checking it exists. Operator's explicit nuance in the same conversation: a quiet/discreet team is not the same as a worthless one -- must never be scored down for low noise.
Solution : reuses `conviction_research.py` as-is (already live on the standard momentum pipeline since 19/07, gated `ARIA_CONVICTION_RESEARCH_ENABLED`) -- never duplicated. New `bonding_entry._socials_to_known_links()` maps `VirtualToken.socials`' real native labels ("WEBSITE"/"TWITTER"/"github"/"telegram"/"warpcast", confirmed live) to the exact labels `conviction_research.research_project_potential`'s `known_links` parsing already recognizes ("Site officiel"/"X (Twitter)"/"GitHub"/"Telegram"/"Farcaster") -- getting "GitHub" right specifically is what triggers `conviction_research._describe_other_known_link`'s REAL substance check (`services/project_activity.py`: repo age/activity, not just presence), directly answering the operator's ask. Called `research_project_potential(token_address, symbol, "base", ...)` -- deliberately `"base"`, not `bonding_entry.CHAIN_MARKER`, so the Tavily search query stays clean and the cache entry is SHARED with the standard pipeline's own diligence on the same contract post-graduation (never a redundant re-search). Wired AFTER every other gate, only on a candidate already about to be bought (same doctrine/latency budget as `momentum_entry.py`) -- `potential_score`/`conviction_process_trail`/`conviction_website_corroborated`/`conviction_posting_cadence` land on the returned dict exactly as the standard pipeline already shapes them, so `paper_trader.compute_entry_alloc` (already reading `sig.get("potential_score")`) picks it up with zero further wiring. Fail-open by design: `potential_score=None` (diligence disabled/unavailable/nothing found) never blocks the BUY, never scores it down -- a discreet, low-signal team is sized normally, not penalized for silence. `bonding_entry.py` -- 6 new dedicated tests (label mapping, known_links forwarding, sizing with/without a conviction result), full suite + `test_coherence.py` to confirm green.
