[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Bonding concentration gate rejected 100% of real candidates -- neutralized below a minimum holder count
Date : 2026.07.24 / Problem : found live, minutes after the bonding-entry engine below first deployed (operator: "ton truc marche pas") -- tested `evaluate_bonding_entry` against the 100 real bonding prototypes available at the time, EVERY SINGLE ONE was rejected on `holder_concentration`, including the token with the most holders (NISTIC, 33 holders, still `top10_holder_pct=100.0`). Root cause verified live: with only a handful of genuine external buyers (confirmed: a token with 0 holders reads `top10_holder_pct=0%`, not 100%, ruling out the bonding pool's own reserve being counted), the top 10 buyers are mechanically ~100% of whatever has been bought so far -- not a rug signal, just too few participants for the ratio to mean anything. The operator's own screenshot of a graduated token (317 real holders) confirmed the gate IS meaningful once there's a real sample: top 10 = 54.2%, comfortably under the 80% threshold.
Solution : new `_MIN_HOLDERS_FOR_CONCENTRATION_CHECK` (15) -- below this floor (or when `holder_count` itself is unknown), the concentration ratio is treated as uninformative (neither for nor against, same "measure before I act" doctrine as `momentum_entry._technical_alignment`'s `None` handling) rather than a hard veto; at or above it, the 80% gate still applies exactly as before (verified: a token with 50 holders and 95% concentration is still rejected). The real anti-rug guard at this ultra-early stage stays `dev_holding_pct` (computed over the FULL supply, so it stays meaningful regardless of buyer count, unchanged) plus the fact that a BUY signal already requires enough real trades to exist at all. Re-tested live against the same 100 real prototypes after the fix: zero rejections on `holder_concentration` among tokens with <15 holders (the vast majority) -- NISTIC (33 holders, still 100%) correctly still rejected. `bonding_entry.py` -- 3 new dedicated tests, full suite + `test_coherence.py` to confirm green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Virtuals bonding-curve entry engine -- new decision path wired directly into the active $1M momentum test
Date : 2026.07.24 / Problem : operator's explicit request ("adapte les conditions d'achat sur un token de bonding Virtuals à ARIA") -- the standard momentum pipeline (`momentum_entry.py`) depends entirely on DexScreener (pairs/liquidity/price) and GeckoTerminal (OHLCV candles), neither of which exist for a token still on a Virtuals bonding curve (no DEX pool until graduation). Confirmed live: a bonding pool's "liquidity" is the protocol's own reserve, never an external LP a deployer could drain, so the standard $50k floor (an LP-drain guard) and GoPlus honeypot check (Base-DEX-oriented) don't transfer as-is.
Solution : new `bonding_entry.py` -- gates on bonding status + native `dev_holding_pct`/`top10_holder_pct` fields (already returned by the same Virtuals list endpoint, replaces GoPlus/`dev_wallet.py`) + a ~10k$ liquidity floor (proxy for market cap on a bonding curve, per the operator's own observation that the two track closely here). Real R/R via genuine OHLCV, not abandoned: a newly-found public endpoint (`vp-api.virtuals.io/vp-api/trades`, individual trade history, no auth) is aggregated into candles by FIXED TRADE COUNT (`aggregate_trades_to_candles`, deliberately never a fixed time window -- bonding-curve trading density varies too wildly between tokens), then run through the SAME `entry_signals.detect_entry` (golden pocket + RSI divergence) engine as the standard pipeline. Real currency bug found and fixed before shipping: trade prices are denominated in $VIRTUAL per token, not USD -- every price level returned (entry/target/invalidation) is converted through a new `virtuals.virtual_usd_rate()` (CoinGecko, id `virtual-protocol`, fail-open, never a fabricated USD price) before reaching `paper_trader.py`'s 100%-USD portfolio; `entry_atr_pct` is the one exception (a ratio, unit-independent, conversion would cancel out). Sizing reuses `paper_trader.compute_entry_alloc` (same risk/ATR formula) with an extra `BONDING_SIZE_REDUCTION` (0.5x) layered on top for this structurally higher-risk path (no honeypot-class check exists here). Position management needed a bonding-aware price lookup too (new `paper_trader._bonding_pair_lookup`, wired into `_default_pair_lookup` via the `bonding_entry.CHAIN_MARKER` chain marker) -- without it, a bonding position would never receive a price refresh after entry (DexScreener has structurally no pair pre-graduation), stop/TP never checked; automatic hand-off to the standard DexScreener path once the token graduates (`is_in_bonding` turning false). Real bug found and fixed in the same chantier, unblocking the whole sourcing side: `base_crawler.discover_virtuals_tokens` filtered candidates on `token_address` alone, which is ALWAYS `None` for a genuinely-bonding token (confirmed live on 100 real UNDERGRAD prototypes, zero exceptions) -- silently returned an empty list in production; now falls back to `pre_token_address`. Wired directly into the currently-active $1M test's sourcing/routing (`paper_trader._momentum_candidates_and_chain_map`/`_default_momentum_analyzer`), per the operator's explicit choice -- not a separate/dormant pocket. `services/virtuals.py`/`bonding_entry.py` (new)/`base_crawler.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- new `test_bonding_entry.py` (16 tests) + additions to `test_virtuals_client.py`/`test_base_crawler.py`/`test_paper_trader.py` (11 dedicated integration tests), full suite green (7370 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[CODE] Sujet    : Cooldown adaptatif WebSocket (4h) + fix WETH scanné en boucle (repli x402 gaspillé)
Date : 2026.07.22  /  Probleme : décision opérateur ("un contrat n'a pas besoin d'être scanné toutes les 60 secondes, toutes les 4h suffit") + bug réel trouvé via le journal x402_spend_log — WETH (predeploy Base, jamais un candidat spéculatif) découvert et évalué en boucle toutes les 10-20 min depuis minuit, déclenchant un repli x402 payant (0,002$/appel) sur holder_concentration à chaque fois (Blockscout gratuit échoue systématiquement sur ce contrat précis).
Solution : (1) DEDUP_TTL_SECONDS (15min, anti-spam de frame) inchangé, nouveau RESCAN_COOLDOWN_SECONDS (4h) — un candidat déjà vu ne redéclenche pas d'évaluation SAUF si son prix a bougé de plus de 10% (RESCAN_PRICE_MOVE_THRESHOLD_PCT) depuis le dernier passage, comparé sans appel réseau dédié (prix déjà en main via _batch_liquidity_prefilter, étendu pour le remonter) ; (2) reference_tokens_excluded() (momentum_entry.py) réutilise les registres déjà vérifiés de smart_money.py (stablecoins Base + wrapped natives) pour exclure WETH/USDC/etc. des DEUX points d'entrée de découverte (discover_momentum_candidates ET momentum_websocket._ingest_frame, qui a son propre chemin d'ajout de candidat) — momentum_entry.py, momentum_websocket.py (cf. historique git 22/07)

[CODE] Sujet    : Crible unifié VC/Swing (tâche #1) -- fusion complète, DORMANT, pas encore branché
Date : 2026.07.22  /  Probleme : le pivot #194 (15/07) avait remplacé le filtre VC-thesis par un critère purement technique pour le test 1M$ -- décision opérateur du 22/07 : construire un jugement unique qui évalue conviction fondamentale (poche VC, 6 mois-2 ans, x20-x100) ET setup technique (poche Swing) sur le MÊME candidat, cumulables sur un même contrat (option "fusion complète" choisie explicitement après comparaison avec une alternative plus simple à deux jugements séparés).
Solution : nouveau module unified_entry.py (evaluate_unified_entry) -- garde-fous durs extraits de momentum_entry dans une nouvelle fonction publique evaluate_hard_gates (honeypot inclus, zéro régression vérifiée sur 205+328 tests, evaluate_momentum_entry inchangé et toujours actif), contexte riche via scan_base_token (TA + smart money qualité-prioritaire + fondamentaux, include_honeypot=False pour ne jamais refaire l'appel GoPlus), nouveau prompt LLM qui juge horizon vc/swing/les_deux/aucun, sizing swing resté déterministe (risk_guard, LLM confirme seulement). paper_trader.has_open()/_get_open() acceptent un paramètre optionnel strategy (rétrocompatible) -- prérequis pour le cumul. **DORMANT** : run_paper_cycle utilise toujours momentum_entry.evaluate_momentum_entry via _default_momentum_analyzer, comportement de production inchangé pour l'instant. Reste à faire avant activation : sizing par poche + plafond de concentration partagé (cumul), boucle _run_paper_cycle_locked adaptée pour consommer 0-2 signaux par candidat, badge VC/Swing dans les 4 points d'affichage (alertes achat/vente + /feedback+/ledger + diagnostic API), tests d'intégration bout-en-bout, stress-test multi-agents (reporté à une session dédiée) — unified_entry.py, momentum_entry.py, paper_trader.py (commit cc8de148).

[DEPLOYE] Sujet    : Incident réel BRIAN — triple-achat wash-trading, -18 561$ papier, 4 garde-fous durs
Date : 2026.07.17  /  Probleme : rien n'empêchait de racheter un contrat dont on venait de se faire sortir en perte — BRIAN racheté 3x en ~2h30 sur un pattern de wash-trading/re-entrée non détecté à l'époque, 92% des pertes réalisées sur ce seul contrat, perte réelle -18 561$ (capital papier).
Solution : 4 garde-fous durs ajoutés — liste noire persistée (amorcée avec BRIAN, momentum_blacklist.py), plafond ratio volume24h/liquidité (20x, anti-wash-trading — BRIAN avait un ratio ~91x), garde-fou de re-entrée sur un contrat déjà clôturé, plafond de variation de prix 24h (`_MAX_PRICE_CHANGE_24H_PCT`, 200%, ajouté séparément suite au 2e cas TSG ci-dessous) — momentum_blacklist.py (d45b6c9e), cf. historique git 17/07

[DEPLOYE] Sujet    : Cycle heartbeat de découverte découplé de la surveillance de position
Date : 2026.07.22  /  Probleme : décision opérateur explicite ("un contrat n'a pas besoin d'être scanné toutes les 60 secondes, toutes les 4h suffit") — le cycle heartbeat unique (15min) mélangeait recherche de nouveaux candidats ET surveillance des positions déjà ouvertes (stop suiveur, re-scan sécurité), rendant impossible de ralentir l'un sans l'autre.
Solution : nouveau paramètre `skip_new_entries` sur `run_paper_cycle` (symétrique de `skip_position_management` déjà existant côté WebSocket) — `paper_trade_cycle` (15min, inchangé) ne gère plus QUE les positions ouvertes ; nouveau cycle `momentum_discovery_cycle` (60min, même gate `ARIA_PAPER_TRADING_ENABLED`) ne cherche plus QUE de nouveaux candidats. Vérifié avant de choisir 1h : sur les 6 sources de découverte (`base_crawler`/Birdeye/4 flux DexScreener boosts+profiles), les 4 flux DexScreener sont déjà redondants avec le WebSocket temps réel (#196, push continu) — seuls `base_crawler`/Birdeye n'ont aucun équivalent temps réel, d'où le choix de ralentir plutôt que supprimer ce cycle — paper_trader.py, heartbeat.py (cf. historique git 22/07)

[CODE] Sujet    : Retry différé des candidats `pending` + plafond anti-boucle-infinie (ancien pipeline VC-thesis pré-#194)
Date : 2026.07.11  /  Probleme : un candidat en échec mou (`pending`, fraîcheur du token plutôt qu'un signal malveillant confirmé) n'était jamais délibérément rescanné — seule une redécouverte fortuite déclenchait un nouveau passage ; et sans plafond, un candidat qui n'atteint jamais `active` serait retenté toutes les 24h pour toujours.
Solution : `screened_pool.list_stale_pending()` + `base_crawler.retry_stale_pending()` (rescan délibéré après 24h, réutilise le même `token_absorber.absorb()`) ; colonne `retry_count` + `abandon_stale_pending()` bascule en `rejected` définitif au-delà de 5 tentatives OU 7 jours — screened_pool.py / base_crawler.py (#105/#108, cf. historique git 11/07). Étendu au pipeline bonding le même mois (#107, `bonding_absorber.retry_stale_bonding_pending()`).

Solution : throttle authentifié réaligné sur le rythme non-authentifié déjà éprouvé (recalibré depuis par l'audit multi-services du 21/07 — toujours vérifier la valeur courante avant de citer un chiffre) — geckoterminal.py (commit f62a6fa8). Diagnostiqué sur 8 candidats réels : 0/8 divergences détectées avant le fix.

Solution : detect_entry() accepte un execution_price optionnel qui remplace le close comme référence R/R (jamais invalidation/target, qui restent des niveaux Fibonacci fixes) ; momentum_entry.py transmet le prix DexScreener réel. /vc (analyse rétrospective, pas d'exécution imminente) non affecté — entry_signals.py/momentum_entry.py (commit 9062f0a7).

[CODE] Sujet    : Progression de graduation Virtuals résolue on-chain (BONDING_V5)
Date : 2026.07.09→11  /  Probleme : graduation_progress() retournait toujours None — aucun champ du payload API Virtuals (virtualRaised etc.) ne correspondait au pourcentage réel affiché par l'UI ; de plus, un token encore en bonding (tokenAddress null, adresse réelle dans le champ preToken) n'était pas détecté du tout par fetch_by_address (ne cherchait que tokenAddress).
Solution : fetch_by_address gagne un repli preToken (build_token_by_pretoken_url) ; vrai contrat Bonding trouvé par balayage direct des logs on-chain (eth_getLogs sur Graduated, par blocs de 10000) : 0x1A540088125d00dD3990f9dA45CA0859af4d3B01 (BondingV5, proxy EIP-1967 vérifié Blockscout) — seuil de graduation confirmé PAR TOKEN (tokenGradThreshold(address)), pas une constante globale, validé empiriquement sur un token gradué réel. Implémenté en lecture seule (aucune clé), repli propre à None si le token utilise une autre instance du contrat — services/base_onchain.py, gate ARIA_ONCHAIN_GRADUATION_ENABLED (OFF par défaut), 15 tests (cf. historique git 11/07)

[CODE] Sujet    : `owner_change_balance` (GoPlus) jamais consulté par `_check_honeypot` — vecteur de perte totale non couvert
Date : 2026.07.22  /  Probleme : trouvé en observant une position momentum RÉELLEMENT ouverte en prod (CNX, R/R 4.5 à l'entrée) — le contrat avait GoPlus `owner_change_balance=True` (l'owner peut modifier directement le solde de n'importe quel wallet, y compris celui d'ARIA) sans que `_check_honeypot` (le SEUL garde-fou dur du pipeline momentum, décision opérateur du 15/07 : rien d'autre du filtre VC-thesis ne s'applique) ne le détecte — seuls `is_honeypot`/`cannot_sell_all` étaient vérifiés. Ce pouvoir est fonctionnellement DISTINCT du honeypot classique (qui bloque la revente) : il permet un vol/effacement direct de solde, un vecteur de perte totale que ni `is_honeypot` ni `cannot_sell_all` ne capturent.
Solution : `security.owner_change_balance` ajouté comme 3e signal dur dans `_check_honeypot` — rejoint le garde-fou existant plutôt que d'élargir la doctrine "seul le honeypot" (mint_authority/dev_wallet restent hors scope momentum, inchangé) : ce signal est de MÊME NATURE que le honeypot (pouvoir technique de vol direct des fonds, pas un signal de conviction/thèse), et coûte ZÉRO appel réseau supplémentaire (même lecture GoPlus déjà faite pour le honeypot). Code machine-readable `honeypot_rejected` réutilisé (cohérent avec le mandat #192). Pendant côté crible VC (`safety_screen.py`/`acp_onchain_scan.py`) dans `docs/HANDOFF_MOTEUR_LEGITIMITE.md`. `momentum_entry.py` — 1 nouveau test dédié, suite complète 6801 passed / 17 skipped, `test_coherence.py` vert (non commité au moment de cette entrée).

[CODE] Subject  : Limit-order mechanism — a drifted-price setup no longer silently rejected
Date : 2026.07.23 / Problem : real position observed in prod (CHECK) revealed the exact gap: between signal detection and execution, honeypot/OHLCV/LLM analysis takes long enough for the price to drift upward (0.038 signal -> 0.044 execution), degrading R/R from 3.9 to 1.52 -- `_execution_rr_still_valid` failing here was a PLAIN reject (`funnel["price_stale_at_execution"]`), discarding a setup that only got MORE EXPENSIVE, not a dead one.
Solution : new `limit_orders.py` -- at that same reject point, `should_place_limit_order` distinguishes case (a) structure already broken (fresh price through the invalidation, or the one-time re-analysis at the pending->watching transition fails) -> reject outright as before, from case (b) drifted upward but structure intact -> a limit order is placed at the ORIGINAL signal price, watched by `momentum_websocket._drain_once()` (restructured so the check runs even with an empty `_pending`, never starving the watch on a quiet day) until it triggers, the invalidation breaks, or it expires silently (`LIMIT_ORDER_EXPIRY_HOURS=3`, never a Telegram alert). Sizing extracted into a new public `paper_trader.compute_entry_alloc()` (same formula/thresholds as before extraction), recomputed with FRESH portfolio context at trigger time -- real bug found and fixed during testing: `_execute_trigger` originally re-applied `risk_guard.cap_alloc_to_price_impact`/`simulated_fill_price` on top of what `open_position` already does internally, silently collapsing the allocation to zero (double price-impact degradation on an already-degraded price). `momentum_entry.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`momentum_websocket.py`/`limit_orders.py` (new) -- 3 new dedicated test files (66 tests), full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green (not yet committed at the time of this entry).

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Liquidity-rotation signal -- measure capital flow on low-info tokens (observational only)
Date : 2026.07.23 / Problem : operator's own trading blind spot, described directly: "sur ces small-caps sans info, l'edge n'est pas les fondamentaux -- c'est de sentir si l'argent tourne vers ce token maintenant" -- triggered by the real AUTONOMOPOLY position (potential_score 0.0/10, zero team/audit/roadmap, yet the pipeline still bought it as a pure momentum bet). A low-info token has nothing to judge on fundamentals by design, but every buy/sell and every dollar of volume is fully transparent on-chain.
Solution : new `skills/liquidity_rotation.py` (pure, DB-free, zero extra network call) -- ``compute_liquidity_rotation`` blends two independent measurements into a 0-10 score: (1) buy-pressure ACCELERATION (h1 buys/(buys+sells) vs the 24h average -- a token net-selling most of the day but net-BOUGHT this hour is rotation happening right now, invisible in the 24h aggregate alone) and (2) volume ACCELERATION (h1 volume run-rated to 24h, divided by the real 24h volume -- a fresh spike vs stale activity spread evenly). Each half capped at 5 points so neither dominates; missing data on one side halves the achievable score rather than fabricating a neutral value. `services/dexscreener.PairSnapshot` gained the h1/h6 volume+txns fields it was silently discarding (DexScreener already returns them in the SAME response as the h24 fields already parsed). Wired as a DELIBERATELY OBSERVATIONAL field on the momentum signal dict (`liquidity_rotation_score`/`_accelerating`/`_volume_ratio`) -- computed on every BUY, persisted on the position (new tracking columns, same hot-migration pattern as the 07/23 performance-breakdown chantier), segmented by `/performance` (`key_liquidity_rotation`). NEVER yet used to gate or size a position -- same "measure before I act" doctrine as the whole /performance chantier: once enough real trades accumulate, a real correlation to winrate/PnL can be measured before this is ever wired into the decision. `services/dexscreener.py`/`momentum_entry.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`limit_orders.py`/`performance_breakdown.py`/`skills/liquidity_rotation.py` (new) -- 13 new dedicated tests (`test_liquidity_rotation.py`) + 2 momentum_entry tests + 1 performance_breakdown test, full suite to confirm, `test_coherence.py` green (not yet committed at the time of this entry).

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Flaunch discovery wired in (V2 API primary, on-chain fallback)
Date : 2026.07.24 / Problem : `launchpad_discovery.py`'s "flaunch" adapter was a documented empty seam (`discover=None`) -- Flaunch is the single biggest launchpad on Base by TVL/fees (confirmed via the operator's own DefiLlama screenshot: $1.48M TVL vs Clanker's next-highest), the only real gap left among the launchpads the operator asked to prioritize (Bankr/Flaunch/Virtuals/Clanker -- Bankr and Virtuals/Clanker turned out to already be covered, see below).
Solution : `services/flaunch.py` (new) tries the official self-serve V2 API first (`api-v2.flayerlabs.xyz/v2/{chain}/coins/top?sort=new`, `X-Api-Key` header -- NOT `Authorization: Bearer`, which is rejected server-side as a Google OAuth token, confirmed live; key read from `FLAUNCH_API_KEY` env var, never hardcoded; throttle calibrated to 90% of the real, live-verified 60 req/min limit), falling back to a direct on-chain `PoolCreated` scan (new `BlockscoutClient.get_contract_logs_bounded`, generic contract-log reader, reused for any future launchpad in the same shape) when no key is configured or the API call fails -- Flaunch's OLDER free REST API (`dev-api.flayerlabs.xyz`) was found down (HTTP 522) during this diligence, motivating the fallback in the first place. Real gotcha found on the on-chain path: the address already on file in `knowledge/launchpads.yaml` is the OLD V1.0 PositionManager (explicitly commented "only old V1.0" in Flaunch's own SDK source) -- 250k+ blocks of its logs contained zero `PoolCreated` events; the active contract for new launches is V1_2 ("1.3" per Flaunch's own GitHub-release comment), now also on file. Separately confirmed Bankr has no launchpad of its own (deploys via Clanker's SDK under the hood, already covered by the existing `clanker` adapter) and BasedBid (a candidate initially considered) was diligenced and rejected -- anonymous team, closed production contracts, its named technical partner (GemPad) suffered a real ~$2M reentrancy exploit, and its own flagship example token is down 95-99% from its claimed ATH. `services/flaunch.py` (new)/`services/blockscout.py`/`services/launchpad_discovery.py`/`knowledge/launchpads.yaml` -- 66 new/updated tests across `test_flaunch_client.py` (new)/`test_launchpad_discovery.py`/`test_blockscout_client.py`, full suite green (7330 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Scalping ran on the standard $50k liquidity floor -- never adapted for the mode
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator pushback ("alors les parametres sont mal regler") on a real observation -- the $1M paper test in scalping mode wasn't trading. Diagnosis: `evaluate_hard_gates`'s liquidity floor (`_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD`, $50,000) was a single global constant with no mode-based branch -- scalping (short 15-30min timeframes, smaller/faster-moving pools by nature) inherited the SAME floor tuned for standard/swing mode, rejecting 18/40 real candidates observed on this single gate alone.
Solution : new `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD_SCALPING = 15_000.0`, `evaluate_hard_gates` gains a `mode` parameter (default `"standard"`, zero regression for `unified_entry.py`'s call site which never passes it) -- resolution order: Fear-regime floor ($100k) still overrides everything first (market-wide risk signal, independent of trading style), then scalping floor if `mode == "scalping"`, else the standard floor. `momentum_entry.py` -- 4 new dedicated tests, full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green (commit `629caed8`).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Ethereum added as a second EVM chain to the momentum/scalping discovery pipeline
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator asked directly whether a second EVM chain was worth adding to widen the diagnostic $1M paper test (Base-only since 20/07). Ethereum picked (operator's own previously-stated roadmap item, "native Ethereum" named explicitly in the 20/07 note) over other candidates -- weaker "early builder" narrative than Base acknowledged, but the test is purely diagnostic (momentum/scalping, not VC-thesis), so more real data has standalone value. Real gap found in the same pass: `GeckoTerminal.GECKO_NETWORK_SLUGS` (built 14/07 for #157 wallet-scoring, already correctly mapping `"ethereum" -> "eth"`) had ZERO consumers anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via grep) -- every Ethereum OHLCV lookup through `GeckoTerminalClient.get_ohlcv` would have silently 404'd (GeckoTerminal's real network slug is `"eth"`, not `"ethereum"`, confirmed live via `GET /api/v2/networks`), starving every Ethereum candidate on `ohlcv_unavailable` the moment discovery went live.
Solution : `DEFAULT_CHAINS` gains `"ethereum"` (`("base", "ethereum")`) -- each of the 3 providers this pipeline hard-depends on confirmed live BEFORE adding it (never assumed, per the "verify before asserting" doctrine): GoPlus (`supported_chains` real call, id `"1"`), DexScreener (real pair lookup, `chainId:"ethereum"`), CoinGecko (`asset_platforms` real call, `id:"ethereum"`) -- `_DEXSCREENER_TO_GOPLUS_CHAIN_ID`/`_COINGECKO_PLATFORM_BY_CHAIN` both gain an `"ethereum"` entry. `GeckoTerminalClient.get_ohlcv` now translates the network via `GECKO_NETWORK_SLUGS.get(network, network)` before calling the wide OHLCV client -- fixes the dead mapping for Ethereum without touching `get_pool_created_at`/`resolve_primary_pool` (same file, confirmed unreachable from `momentum_entry.py`'s live path, left out of scope). Blockscout already had `CHAIN_IDS["ethereum"] = 1` (built 14/07, #157) -- holder-concentration gate works unchanged. `agent_wallet_pilot_cycle.py`'s real-capital pilot is hardcoded `CHAIN = "base"`, entirely unaffected -- this addition is scoped to the paper-trading discovery pipeline only. `momentum_entry.py`/`services/geckoterminal.py` -- new dedicated tests (GoPlus chain-id translation for Ethereum, GeckoTerminal network-slug translation) + 3 pre-existing tests corrected (2 that used "ethereum" as their example of an unmapped/disallowed chain, now genuinely wrong since it's mapped; 1 whose docstring assumption "`DEFAULT_CHAINS` narrowed to Base alone" no longer held, category is now populated by default), full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Ethereum's doubled candidate volume exposed a real GeckoTerminal sustained-throughput ceiling below the documented figure
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator report ("aria ne trade pas en scalping") triggered a real investigation -- confirmed empirically via prod logs: 0 HTTP 429 on GeckoTerminal in the 6h before Ethereum was deployed to `DEFAULT_CHAINS`, 13+ right after. The per-call throttle itself never changed (`_AUTHENTICATED_MIN_INTERVAL`, still 2.222s = 27 req/min, "90% of the 30 req/min documented Demo-key limit") -- what changed is that Base+Ethereum together keep the shared client PERMANENTLY busy (no more idle gaps between scans for the shared budget to recover), which is what actually exposed a real sustained cap lower than the documented figure (documented != empirically measured under continuous load -- same doctrine as the GoPlus/Tavily incidents already in CLAUDE.md). CoinMarketCap (2nd cascade tier) was ALSO observed tripping its own circuit breaker at the same time -- the system degraded correctly to Mobula (3rd tier, "real candles" confirmed in logs), never a hard failure, but a clear sign the whole OHLCV cascade is now working harder than before.
Solution : widened the safety margin 90% -> 70% of documented (30*0.7=21 req/min = 2.857s) as an immediate, reversible precaution on BOTH `_AUTHENTICATED_MIN_INTERVAL` and `_MIN_INTERVAL` (kept aligned -- authenticated must never be slower than keyless). Explicitly NOT a substitute for a real burst-controlled empirical measurement of the true sustained cap under this new continuous two-chain load, which still needs to happen separately. `services/geckoterminal.py` -- no test change needed (tests reference the constant by name, not by value), full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Real x402 money spent on a candidate rejected for free one step later (pipeline ordering gap)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator pushback ("les paiements x402 continuent... il faut que tous les services s'arretent avec une explication") led to a real finding via `x402_budget.list_spends()`: 4 real payments (0.002$ each, USDC) for the `token-holders` paid Blockscout fallback (holder-concentration hard gate) on 2 contracts -- one of them (`0x23a2847d77...`) was the EXACT SAME contract independently observed moments earlier being rejected for free on `no_entry_signal` (no golden-pocket/RSI setup) at a LATER step of the same pipeline. Root cause: `evaluate_hard_gates`'s holder-concentration check (with its paid x402 fallback when free Blockscout fails) runs BEFORE the R/R/OHLCV computation (`detect_entry`, free) that alone could have rejected the candidate at zero cost.
Solution : NOT YET FIXED -- `evaluate_hard_gates` is shared as-is with `unified_entry.py`'s VC-thesis bucket (deliberately stops before any technical/OHLCV computation, since a VC thesis never needs candles) -- simply moving R/R ahead of holder-concentration would require restructuring this shared function without regressing the VC path, a bigger change than a quick reorder. Scoped for a dedicated pass (a background multi-agent audit of the full trading/prediction/research pipeline was launched the same day to map every similar gap before touching the ordering).

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Shared TTL cache + per-contract lock closes the real scale of the x402 holder-concentration waste (reordering above still open)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : the full-pipeline audit launched after the entry above returned: the real waste was ~80x larger than the initial 4-payment estimate -- 333 real "token-holders" x402 payments ($0.666) since 21/07 on 31 distinct contracts, 104 of them (31%) PURE duplicates 2.5-4.5 seconds apart. Root cause confirmed: the periodic scan cycle (`_run_cycle_lock`, a GLOBAL lock, never per-contract) and the WebSocket drain loop (`momentum_websocket._drain_once`) independently re-evaluate the same fresh candidate and each pay their own x402 call, with zero cache/TTL anywhere in `_check_holder_concentration`/`blockscout_x402.get_token_holders_x402`. Same audit found an adjacent, previously undocumented double-call: `_check_parabolic_smart_money_rescue` (step 6, parabolic 200-350% rescue tier) fetches Blockscout holders for the SAME contract that `_check_holder_concentration` (step 8) fetches again moments later -- the latter's own docstring admitted "nothing to reuse here" before this fix.
Solution : a process-local TTL cache (`_HOLDERS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 300.0`, keyed by `(chain, contract)`) now sits in front of BOTH the free/Pro Blockscout call and the paid x402 fallback, shared between `_check_parabolic_smart_money_rescue` and `_check_holder_concentration` via two new helpers (`_cached_get_token_holders`/`_cached_get_token_holders_x402`) -- a rescue-tier candidate that reaches the concentration check moments later reuses the same cached holders instead of paying/fetching twice. A per-contract `asyncio.Lock` (`_holders_lock_for`) closes the residual race the cache alone can't (two truly concurrent evaluations landing at the same instant, not just seconds apart) -- the second caller waits for the first instead of racing it into its own network/x402 call. Opportunistic eviction (`_purge_expired_holders_state`, runs on every cache write) keeps the dicts bounded to roughly the contracts seen within the last TTL window, not one entry per distinct contract ever evaluated (long-running process, cf. the VPS's 4GB RAM constraint). Reordering holder-concentration to run after the free R/R computation (the OTHER half of the original finding) remains open -- tracked separately (backlog #113), this fix closes the duplicate-payment/double-call waste, not the ordering gap itself. `momentum_entry.py` -- 6 new dedicated tests (`TestHoldersSharedCache`: TTL reuse, TTL expiry, sharing between the two callers, per-contract isolation, x402 path also cached, concurrent-call race closed by the lock) + a new autouse fixture (`_reset_holders_cache`) in `test_momentum_entry.py` to stop the shared module-level cache from leaking state between the file's many tests that reuse the same `CONTRACT` constant, full suite green (7960 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Holder-concentration reordered after the free R/R computation (closes backlog #113)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : the ordering half of the finding above (entry `Real x402 money spent...`) remained unfixed -- `evaluate_hard_gates` is shared verbatim with `unified_entry.py`'s VC-thesis bucket, which deliberately never computes R/R (a VC thesis stays qualitative, no candles), so simply moving the check inside that shared function would either regress the VC path or require duplicating it.
Solution : `evaluate_hard_gates` gains a `defer_holder_concentration: bool = False` parameter (default unchanged -- `unified_entry.py` never sets it, so the VC bucket keeps this guardrail at its original place, before honeypot). `evaluate_momentum_entry` (the only caller that has an R/R step to defer to) now passes `defer_holder_concentration=True` and runs `_check_holder_concentration` itself, AFTER `detect_entry` confirms a real golden-pocket/RSI setup exists -- a candidate rejected for free on `no_entry_signal` never reaches the (potentially x402-paid) concentration check at all. Honeypot's own "moved to last" ordering (GoPlus, a throughput-scarce but non-monetary resource) is untouched -- out of scope, this fix targets only the check with a real USDC cost. `momentum_entry.py` -- docstring reorder (steps renumbered, holder-concentration now "9bis", after R/R) + 1 existing test adjusted (`test_evaluate_rejects_on_holder_concentration` now needs a real R/R signal to reach the gate at all, exactly the point of the fix) + 1 new dedicated test (`test_evaluate_never_pays_holder_concentration_when_no_entry_signal`, asserts zero calls to the check on a `no_entry_signal` rejection). Full suite green (7961 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Portfolio-wide scalping mode silently ignored by 2 of 3 entry paths (real position opened in "standard" while the switch read "scalping")
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator-reported real Telegram screenshot -- an AERO position was opened (19:27:51 UTC) with `mode='standard'` and a thesis showing the FULL `conviction_research` diligence (Website/Docs/X/Tavily + a paid x402 twit.sh fallback for posting cadence) even though `paper_state.trading_mode` was confirmed `'scalping'` in the real DB at the same moment -- Item #101 (26/07) specifically documents that scalping mode skips this diligence entirely (no predictive value on a 15-30min horizon, pure speed/cost win). Root cause, found by tracing every caller of `_default_momentum_analyzer`: only `_run_paper_cycle_locked` (the periodic scan) correctly resolves `trading_mode = await get_trading_mode()` once per cycle and forwards it -- the other TWO real callers never did. `_run_daily_trade_floor_locked` (the diagnostic daily-trade-floor cycle, the one that actually opened AERO) never called `get_trading_mode()` at all, silently defaulting to `mode="standard"`. `momentum_websocket._drain_once` (the real-time WebSocket path) called `_default_momentum_analyzer(chain_by_contract)` with ZERO optional kwargs -- neither `mode` nor `current_regime` (Regime Switch) were ever resolved, meaning the same candidate caught by the WebSocket path got a DIFFERENT (wrong RSI period/timeframe, wrong regime-driven sizing/liquidity floor) evaluation than the periodic scan would give it.
Solution : both callers now resolve the SAME portfolio-wide switches the periodic cycle already does, same pattern (`get_trading_mode()`/`market_sentiment.resolve_meta_regime()`, both fail-open to `"standard"`/neutral on error, never blocking). `_run_daily_trade_floor_locked` resolves `trading_mode` once and forwards it to `_default_momentum_analyzer`. `momentum_websocket._drain_once` resolves both `trading_mode` and `current_regime` and forwards them too (`weekly_context` stays `None` here, unchanged -- its computation is cycle-cadence specific, out of this fix's scope). `paper_trader.py`/`momentum_websocket.py` -- 2 new dedicated tests (`test_daily_floor_forwards_the_portfolio_wide_trading_mode`, `test_drain_forwards_the_portfolio_wide_trading_mode_and_regime`, both lock in `set_trading_mode("scalping")` -> the analyzer receives `mode="scalping"`). Full suite green (verify count at deploy time), `test_coherence.py` green.

[CODE] Subject  : Real Mobula fallback for scalping OHLCV (never verified sub-hour claim, turned out false)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : the operator relayed an external review questioning `_fetch_candles`'s scalping-mode docstring, which asserted "no other provider has sub-hour granularity" (CoinMarketCap/DexScreener synthesis/Dune stay skipped in that mode on that basis) -- this assumption had never actually been tested, only carried forward from Item #101's original build. When GeckoTerminal alone fails/rate-limits in scalping mode, the cascade returned `[]` directly -> HOLD "OHLCV unavailable", even on an otherwise tradeable setup.
Solution : verified live, in the prod container, on real scalping candidates (CHIP/CIRCLE/MRDN) that Mobula genuinely returns real 15m/30m candles for Base (not a synthesis) -- the "no sub-hour provider" claim was false specifically for Mobula (still true for CoinMarketCap/DexScreener synthesis/Dune, unchanged). `_fetch_candles`'s scalping branch now tries Mobula (`contract` required, same as the standard-mode Mobula stage) at `period="15m"` first, then `"30m"` if 15m comes back empty (`available=True, candles=[]` -- not a provider failure, just nothing at that grain) -- a degradation to 30m is always logged explicitly. Only if both periods genuinely fail (`available=False`) does the circuit breaker record a failure and the cascade give up honestly (`[]`). CoinMarketCap/DexScreener synthesis/Dune remain structurally skipped in scalping mode, unchanged -- this is an ADDITION to the one real gap found, not a broader relaxation. `momentum_entry.py` -- 6 new dedicated tests (`test_fetch_candles_scalping_*`: 15m success, 15m->30m degradation, both-fail->empty, skip when unconfigured, skip without contract, non-regression that CoinMarketCap/DexScreener are never tried). Full suite green (verify count at deploy time), `test_coherence.py` green.

[CODE] Subject  : "R/R faible" mislabeling (Item #98, 25/07 floor-mode fix) never extended to the standard ambiguous branch
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : Item #98 (25/07) fixed the floor-mode branch's HOLD/BUY reason message, which used to always blame "R/R faible" even when the real reason the direct-buy AND condition failed was `align_score` alone (real case, OWB, R/R=50.8) -- but the fix was applied ONLY to the `relaxed=True` floor branch. The standard ambiguous branch (`_RR_AMBIGUOUS_FLOOR <= rr < _RR_MIN_FOR_DIRECT_BUY`, or reached via a high rr that still misses `align_score`'s own floor) still had the old unconditional wording, confirmed live on a real case (ZEN, R/R=6.1, only `align_score=1/3` missed the direct-buy bar) -- ARIA's own relay-channel answer about ZEN would have cited an incorrect "R/R faible" cause.
Solution : the diagnosis logic (rr_weak/align_weak/both) is extracted into a single shared helper (`_diagnose_weak_point(rr, align_score) -> str`), used by BOTH the floor-mode branch and the standard ambiguous branch (all 3 of its outcomes: BUY-confirmed, HOLD_TRAP, HOLD_WEAK) -- never a second copy of this diagnosis that could silently diverge from the first again. `momentum_entry.py` -- 3 new unit tests on the helper itself + 4 new integration tests on the standard ambiguous branch (rr-weak/align-weak/both-weak on the BUY path, plus one on the HOLD_WEAK path) mirroring the existing floor-mode test suite. Full suite green (7997 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green (commit `b1bb3bb9`).

[CODE] Subject  : OHLCV ladder kept escalating through a REAL network error (429/timeout/5xx), wasting 2-3x the throttled calls on a candidate already doomed
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : investigating a real operator report of GeckoTerminal 429s during a 40-candidate scan burst -- confirmed live in prod logs that the SAME pool hit `ohlcv/day -> 429` immediately followed by `ohlcv/hour -> 429` within a single burst. Root cause found in `services/ohlcv.py`'s ladder loop: `if error is not None: last_error = error; continue` treated a REAL network/rate-limit/server error IDENTICALLY to "not enough candles at this timeframe" -- both fell through to the next rung. But a 429/timeout/5xx/unknown-pool error applies to the WHOLE pool/endpoint regardless of which timeframe is requested; escalating the ladder never helps that case, it only helps the genuinely different case where the server answered fine but this timeframe simply doesn't have enough candles yet. Operator's own diagnosis, confirmed by code: "un rate limit sur day n'empêche jamais d'essayer hour ensuite" was the actual behavior, and it was pure waste -- multiplying the throttled calls spent on a candidate that was going to fail either way.
Solution : the loop now `break`s immediately on any real error instead of `continue`-ing, returning whatever partial result (`best`) was already obtained from an earlier successful-but-thin rung rather than compounding the failure with more doomed attempts. Zero loss of coverage -- no candidate is scanned less often, this only trims wasted retries on candidates already hitting a real network condition. Cuts real request volume during a burst by an estimated 2-3x (each candidate that hits a real error now costs 1 HTTP round-trip instead of up to 3). `services/ohlcv.py` -- 2 existing tests updated to lock in the new behavior (`test_all_unavailable_is_graceful`/`test_scalping_mode_never_falls_back_to_standard_ladder`, both now assert the SECOND rung is never even attempted after a real error on the first) + 3 new dedicated tests (rate-limit stops the ladder, rate-limit on the SECOND rung preserves the partial result already obtained from the first, timeout stops the ladder). Full suite green (8000 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green -- operator explicitly approved, "parfait oui".

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : DexScreener pair-data doublon between _batch_liquidity_prefilter and evaluate_hard_gates (Item #122)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : full-pipeline audit (3-agent workflow, ELEVE severity finding) -- `_batch_liquidity_prefilter` already fetches the complete `PairSnapshot` of every candidate via the batch endpoint (`fetch_tokens_batch`), but only keeps `liquidity_usd`/`price_usd` and discards the rest. Moments later, in the SAME evaluation, `evaluate_hard_gates` refetches the exact same data via a SEPARATE DexScreener endpoint (`fetch_token_pairs`) to compute `_best_pair` -- a doublon hitting every single candidate, on both the periodic scan and the WebSocket drain. The cache TTL + per-key pattern for this exact class of problem already existed in this same file for Blockscout holders (26/07 earlier the same day) -- never extended to this case.
Solution : proved the two selections are identical (`_best_pair`'s intermediate `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` pre-filter never changes which pair has the global max liquidity among own pairs -- the max of a filtered subset, when it exists, is always the same as the max of the full set). New short-TTL cache (`_PAIR_SNAPSHOT_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 60.0`, deliberately much shorter than the 300s holders cache -- a PairSnapshot carries a live price, and the real gap this closes is a few seconds within the same evaluation, never several minutes) -- `_batch_liquidity_prefilter` writes the winning `PairSnapshot` per `(chain, contract)` via `_cache_pair_snapshot`, `evaluate_hard_gates` checks `_get_cached_pair_snapshot` FIRST and only falls back to the network call (`fetch_token_pairs` + `_best_pair`) on a cache miss -- zero behavior change for any caller that never went through the batch pre-filter (VC-thesis bucket, manual `/vc`, an expired/absent cache entry). `momentum_entry.py` -- 4 new dedicated tests (cache reuse skips the network call entirely, non-regression fallback when cache is empty, the batch pre-filter populates the cache with the correct winning pair, TTL expiry). Committed (`ee2ba5f8`), full suite confirmed green (8024 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green, deployed and confirmed live in prod (`ec72cc53`, 26/07).

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : DexScreener had no circuit breaker despite being the most-called external service of the pipeline (Item #123)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : same full-pipeline audit, ELEVE severity -- `services/dexscreener.py` had a proactive throttle (`_MIN_INTERVAL`, 21/07) and a per-call 429/5xx retry, but unlike the OHLCV cascade's per-provider breaker (`momentum_entry._provider_in_cooldown`) or `goplus.py`'s own circuit breaker, NOTHING remembered a confirmed failure across calls -- 17+ call sites across the codebase (momentum_entry.py, momentum_websocket.py, paper_trader.py, limit_orders.py, bonding_entry.py, conviction_research.py, skills/acp_onchain_scan.py, skills/liquidity_rotation.py, skills/image_token_curiosity.py, token_candidate_screening.py, agent_wallet_monitor.py...) means a sustained rate-limit/outage would make every new candidate/chunk blindly retry the full backoff loop, oblivious to an identical failure that had just happened moments earlier on the exact same shared client.
Solution : same pattern/thresholds as the OHLCV cascade's per-provider breaker (`_CIRCUIT_FAIL_THRESHOLD = 3`, `_CIRCUIT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS = 180.0`) -- a single shared provider here (DexScreener only), so plain module-level counters (`_consecutive_failures`/`_circuit_open_until`) rather than a dict keyed by provider name. `_get_json` checks `_in_cooldown()` before touching the network at all (skips straight to a graceful degradation), and records the outcome (`_record_outcome`) only on a REAL final failure (timeout after retry, 429 after 3 attempts, 5xx after retry, other HTTP error) or a genuine success -- a still-in-progress backoff loop never counts as a confirmed failure. `services/dexscreener.py` -- 3 new dedicated tests (breaker opens after threshold consecutive failures on distinct candidates and skips the network entirely on the next one, a success resets the counter so 2+1+2 never opens it, the pause expires after the cooldown). Committed (`ee2ba5f8`), full suite confirmed green (8024 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green, deployed and confirmed live in prod (`ec72cc53`, 26/07).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : DexPaprika added as a real, free, last-resort tier of the OHLCV cascade (Item #130)
Date : 2026.07.26 / Problem : operator decision after the rate-limit investigation above -- rather than pay for a higher GeckoTerminal tier, integrate DexPaprika (api.dexpaprika.com), a free/keyless real-OHLCV provider already vetted the same day by a 3-agent due-diligence workflow ("verify before affirming" doctrine, never assumed from marketing docs): legitimate company (CoinPaprika, Poland, 2018) but a young sub-product (launched 2025-03-31) with self-contradictory documented rate limits; sustained throughput measured empirically at ~53 req/min (303 real requests over 5m29s, 96.4% success); data quality checked against GeckoTerminal on 6 real Base pools (prices within 0.001-0.35%, volumes within 0.03-6.2%, never flagrant); one real defect found and designed around -- a malformed ``start`` date is NEVER rejected by the API, it silently serves data from an arbitrary wrong window (2024-11-01) instead.
Solution : new `services/dexpaprika.py` -- same "dome" doctrine as mobula.py/coinmarketcap.py (429: 3-attempt backoff, 5xx/timeout: 1 retry, proactive throttle at 1.2s/50 req/min, a deliberately wider margin than the ~53/min tested rate to favor near-100% sustained success over raw throughput). `_compute_start` ALWAYS builds the date via `datetime` (never string concatenation) -- the one guardrail that must never regress, given the real defect found. Short ladder per mode (standard: 24h->6h->1h; scalping: 15m->30m, mirrors Mobula's own scalping ladder) rather than escalating on a real network error (same "stop, don't compound" philosophy as ohlcv.py's Item #121 fix earlier the same day, at a shallower cascade depth since DexPaprika has no partial-result state worth preserving across its own short ladder). Wired into `momentum_entry._fetch_candles` as a SIXTH stage, deliberately positioned LAST among the real-candle tiers (after Mobula, before the degraded DexScreener synthesis in standard mode; after Mobula, before giving up in scalping mode) per the due-diligence workflow's own recommendation -- DexPaprika's free-tier long-term stability carries more uncertainty than GeckoTerminal's, so it must never become primary. Uses the same `_provider_in_cooldown`/`_record_provider_outcome` adaptive circuit breaker already shared by the other 4 OHLCV providers in this file. `services/dexpaprika.py` (new) -- 14 new dedicated tests (`test_dexpaprika_client.py`: `_compute_start` never regresses to a malformed date, candle parsing, both ladders, dome error policy) + `momentum_entry.py` -- 3 new dedicated tests (standard fallback, never tried when an earlier tier already succeeded, scalping fallback) + 8 existing `_fetch_candles` tests updated to mock the new stage explicitly (previously would have silently attempted a real, unmocked network call once this stage existed). Committed (`ee2ba5f8`), full suite confirmed green (8024 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green, deployed and confirmed live in prod (`ec72cc53`, 26/07) -- verified with a REAL live call against a real Base pool (`0x6c561b446416e1a00e8e93e221854d6ea4171372`, top Uniswap V3 pool by volume): 120 real candles returned in both standard and scalping mode, no exception, matches the client's own test suite. `ohlcv_unavailable` entries observed in the live paper_cycle funnel right after deploy (18/40 candidates in one scalping-mode batch) are NOT a DexPaprika regression -- confirmed by design: every provider in this cascade (GeckoTerminal/CoinMarketCap/Mobula/DexPaprika alike) logs a real failure only on an exception or an explicit success, a clean "no candles" result falls through silently to the next tier -- consistent with a batch of thin/fresh scalping candidates that genuinely have no OHLCV anywhere yet, not a bug in the new stage. One real observability gap found while verifying (not fixed, low severity, noted for a future pass): unlike GeckoTerminal/CoinMarketCap, the DexPaprika stage has no explicit "paused (adaptive circuit breaker)" log line when skipped due to an open breaker -- purely cosmetic (the fallback logic itself is unaffected), but worth aligning for consistency next time this file is touched.

[CODE] Subject  : Real circuit breaker on BlockscoutClient (Items #124/#129)
Date : 2026.07.27 / Problem : `services/blockscout.py` already tracked `_consecutive_failures` per client instance, but only to log a warning past the 3-failure threshold -- "no blocking, no escalation" was the doctrine documented right in the module docstring since the client's creation. Every call site (holder concentration gate, wallet-scoring, deployer_history, insider_wallets...) kept retrying (and, for `_check_holder_concentration`, kept paying the x402 fallback) on a provider already known to be down for several calls in a row, on the same instance.
Solution : same threshold (3, unchanged, already tuned) now also opens a real temporal cooldown (`_CIRCUIT_COOLDOWN_SECONDS = 180.0`, same value as the DexScreener/OHLCV breakers -- consistency, not a separately-tuned number). `_get_json` checks `_in_cooldown()` first and returns a graceful degradation immediately, before touching the network at all. Deliberately scoped to the FREE/PRO PATH ONLY (inside `BlockscoutClient`, per client instance = per chain) -- the paid x402 fallback in `_check_holder_concentration` is a SEPARATE call path (`blockscout_x402.get_token_holders_x402`), never gated by this breaker, so a security guardrail (holder concentration, fail-open on missing data) never loses its real safety net just to save a few retries; the breaker only makes the degradation to that fallback faster, it never removes the fallback itself. `services/blockscout.py` -- 3 new dedicated tests (`test_blockscout_client.py`: breaker opens after 3 distinct failing calls and skips the network entirely on a 4th, a success in between resets the counter so 2+1+2 never opens it, the cooldown expires and a fresh call succeeds normally). Full suite to confirm, `test_coherence.py` to confirm (not yet committed at the time of this entry).

[CODE] Subject  : Real network error stops the Mobula 15m/30m scalping ladder instead of escalating (Item #126)
Date : 2026.07.27 / Problem : same "stop, don't compound" anti-pattern already fixed twice this week (ohlcv.py's Item #121, DexPaprika's own short ladder) was still present in momentum_entry.py's scalping-mode Mobula fallback loop (`for period in ("15m", "30m")`, added 26/07) -- a real network/rate-limit/server error at 15m (which applies to the whole endpoint for this contract, not just this timeframe) was indistinguishable from a clean "no candles yet" response, so the loop always escalated to 30m regardless, wasting a doomed retry on a contract already known to be failing.
Solution : `OHLCVResult` (the shared dataclass reused by every OHLCV provider in this cascade -- GeckoTerminal/CoinMarketCap/Mobula/DexPaprika) gains a new `network_error: bool = False` field, default-False so every existing caller's behavior is unchanged (they never read it). `mobula.get_ohlcv` sets it True only when the error comes straight from `_get_json` (a real 429-after-3-attempts/timeout/5xx-after-retry failure) -- never for the "no candles"/"unreadable rows" cases detected further below after a successful HTTP response. The scalping loop in `momentum_entry._fetch_candles` now `break`s immediately on `mobula_result.network_error` instead of falling through to 30m; a clean empty response at 15m still escalates to 30m exactly as before (proven by the pre-existing `test_fetch_candles_scalping_degrades_to_30m_when_15m_empty`, still green, unmodified). `services/geckoterminal.py`/`services/mobula.py`/`momentum_entry.py` -- 3 new/updated dedicated tests (`test_mobula.py`: `network_error` True on a confirmed rate limit, False on a clean empty/malformed response; `test_momentum_entry.py`: the scalping loop stops at 15m on a real error, never reaching 30m). Full suite to confirm, `test_coherence.py` to confirm (not yet committed at the time of this entry).

[CODE] Subject  : DEX composite security/conviction score -- additive signal for graduated tokens (Items #177-#181)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Problem : operator request ("ajouter comme signal supplémentaire") after comparing HOLO (bonding, scored 35/35/15/15) against several already-graduated DEX tokens (XOE, REPLY, a homonym "BTC") that had no equivalent scoring -- the standard momentum pipeline decides via hard gates + R/R + LLM tie-break, never a composite score, so a graduated token had strictly less structured signal available than a bonding one despite having richer real data (real OHLCV, full GoPlus fields, real transfer history, real smart-money analysis) mostly unused or narrowly scoped today.
Solution : new `dex_composite_score.py` (35/20/25/20 -- contract/dev residual risk beyond the honeypot class already hard-gated / dev-wallet behavior (never wired into momentum before, VC-pocket only) / smart-money convergence generalized from its narrow parabolic-rescue-only usage (capped at 4 wallets, not smart_money.py's own default of 8, since this now runs on every BUY not just the rare 200-350% tier) / liquidity-mcap depth ratio (zero extra network cost, reuses the already-fetched PairSnapshot)) -- designed via a 2-agent research+design workflow (28/07) explicitly verified pillar-by-pillar against `momentum_entry.py`'s real hard gates to avoid re-scoring anything already gated elsewhere (honeypot, holder concentration, R/R+align, RVOL, wash-trading, fundamental score). Wired as a pure ADDITIVE signal, never a gate: a CONFIRMED score below `risk_guard.DEX_SECURITY_WEAK_THRESHOLD` (40/100) becomes a 3rd conviction-tier flag alongside `weak_fundamentals`/`unconfirmed_volume` (same stacking doctrine, `conviction_size_multiplier`/`conviction_risk_budget_pct`/`conviction_tier_label`); below `DEX_SECURITY_REJECT_THRESHOLD` (15/100), rejects outright -- same doctrine as `fundamental_score_critical` (25/07, real CHECK loss). Reuses the `TokenSecurity` already fetched by `momentum_entry._check_honeypot` via a new short-TTL cache (`_security_cache`) -- zero extra GoPlus call. Real gap found and fixed along the way: `PairSnapshot` never captured `marketCap`/`fdv` despite both being present on every real DexScreener pair (verified live on XOE/REPLY) -- added as `market_cap_usd`, needed by the liquidity-depth pillar, zero extra network call. `dev_wallet.gather_dev_wallet_facts` gained an optional `holders` parameter (backward-compatible, defaults to its existing internal fetch) so the composite's smart-money and dev-behavior pillars can share ONE holders fetch instead of two. New `dex_score_log.py` (`dex_score_log` table: `contract`/`scored_at`/`score_json`) -- append-only history of every scan, including candidates never bought, modeled on `wallet_score_log` (verified `thesis_checkpoint` was NOT a fit: scoped exclusively to already-open VC positions with a qualitative verdict, not a numeric score) -- exists specifically so the first-pass weights/thresholds (never yet calibrated against real outcomes, same caveat as bonding_entry.py's own composite) can be revisited via `performance_breakdown.py` once enough scans accumulate. Base-chain only, standard mode only (skipped in scalping, same cost/latency reasoning as `conviction_research`), best-effort (never blocks a BUY on failure). Verified end-to-end live on REPLY (0x05B1266DDCeE093cE060DBF697e230EA9B453633, already-graduated Virtuals token): score 64.8/100 (contract 35/35, dev-behavior 10/20 neutral, smart-money 12.5/25 neutral, liquidity-depth 7.3/20 -- a real signal, liquidity only 11% of market cap). `dex_composite_score.py`/`dex_score_log.py` (new), `services/dexscreener.py`, `skills/dev_wallet.py`, `momentum_entry.py`, `risk_guard.py`, `paper_trader.py` -- 31 new tests (`test_dex_composite_score.py`), 6 new tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: score threaded into result, catastrophic rejection, fail-open on None, never computed in scalping, written to `dex_score_log`, a crash never blocks the BUY), 11 new tests (`test_risk_guard.py`: 3rd-flag stacking on all three sizing functions). `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` updated (new constants + a dedicated new section), `test_coherence.py`'s `_TRADING_THRESHOLDS` registry updated to match. Full suite green (8266 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green. Not yet deployed at the time of this entry. Banked but not built: Item #181 (percentile-relative scoring, like `/walletscore`'s comparison-population approach, instead of the fixed absolute thresholds here) -- a real trade-off (auto-calibrating over time vs. cold-start bias while the population is small), explicitly deferred rather than built without discussion.

[CODE] Subject  : Golden-pocket liberation -- watch-and-wait limit order when the golden pocket/RSI gate isn't met yet but the DEX composite score already confirms high quality (Item #182)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Problem : operator observation, following the Item #177-181 build above -- empirically testing 10 real Base tokens across 4 quality tiers confirmed the golden-pocket/RSI gate (`no_entry_signal`) is the DOMINANT blocking gate in the real pipeline (5/7 non-blacklisted tokens tested, 71%), far more restrictive than liquidity/volume/security gates. Operator's stated goal for the new composite score was explicitly to use it to "liberate" this gate ("l'objectif d'avoir un score plus strict c'est de liberer le golden pocket un peu car il filtre trop") -- but simply buying immediately whenever the score is high, without a real technical setup, would mean ARIA structurally enters every such position already worse-priced than an optimized entry (operator's own concern: "si aria n'optimise pas ses points d'entrée c'est casiment comme si on partait du principe que chaque achat démarre a -30%").
Solution : the golden pocket/RSI criterion itself is NEVER softened or removed -- still a hard requirement for a direct BUY, unchanged. Instead, when the setup hasn't formed YET (price still above a geometrically-computable Fibonacci zone, `signal.in_golden_pocket is False` AND `price > signal.gp_high` -- explicitly distinct from a price that already broke BELOW the zone, a dead/invalidated setup which stays a plain reject) and `dex_composite_score` independently confirms high quality (new `risk_guard.DEX_QUALITY_WATCH_THRESHOLD = 70.0`, operator-set starting value: "pour l'instant met 70 pour voir combien de signaux on va entrer et enssuite on ajustera"), a watch-and-wait limit order is placed at the zone's shallow bound (`gp_high`) instead of a plain discard -- reusing the existing `limit_orders.py` mechanism (07/23) built for the price-drift case, same `LIMIT_ORDER_EXPIRY_HOURS` (3h) duration, same Telegram notification already wired at the placement call site (`limit_orders.format_limit_order_placed_alert`). Operator-raised concern mid-build, addressed before shipping: a plain "price above gp_high" test alone can't distinguish a token that just started a fresh uptrend (far from any pullback, would likely never come back within the order's lifetime) from one already retracing toward the zone -- a new `_GOLDEN_POCKET_WATCH_MIN_RETRACEMENT = 0.5` (Fibonacci midpoint) requires the price to have already given back at least half of the window's high->low range before a watch is even considered. Second operator requirement (before any watch order triggers a buy): the DEX composite score must be RE-VERIFIED fresh at the pending->watching transition, not just trusted from signal time -- unlike the existing honeypot-only re-check `reanalyze_for_watching` performs for a price-drift order (which has an already-confirmed technical setup to fall back on), this order's entire premise IS the score, so an unresolved re-check must fail-CLOSED (cancel), the opposite of the fail-open doctrine used everywhere else for this additive signal. New `momentum_entry.refresh_dex_composite_score` (re-fetches a fresh `PairSnapshot` + reuses the `TokenSecurity` a just-performed `check_honeypot()` call already cached) and `limit_orders._reanalyze_dex_quality_for_watching` (honeypot first, always -- the one hard guardrail never skipped -- then the fresh score) are routed to from `reanalyze_for_watching` via a new `signal_json["limit_order_reason"] == "golden_pocket_pending"` tag, leaving the standard price-drift path byte-for-byte unchanged. `entry_signals.EntrySignal` gained `range_high`/`range_low` (the window's swing-high/low, already computed internally by `fibonacci_zone()` but never exposed) -- populated even when `present=False`, as long as a real zone is geometrically computable, since these facts don't depend on RSI confirmation. `entry_signals.py`/`momentum_entry.py`/`risk_guard.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`limit_orders.py` -- new tests across `test_entry_signals.py` (3), `test_momentum_entry.py` (7: candidate created on high score, absent on low score/insufficient retracement/already-broken structure/scalping mode/off-Base/scoring failure), `test_limit_orders.py` (6: routing, honeypot-first short-circuit, score-degraded cancels, unresolved-score fail-closed, network-error fail-closed, plain orders unaffected), `test_paper_trader.py` (2: order placed + notified with the right levels/reasons, plain no_entry_signal without a candidate unaffected). `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md`/`test_coherence.py`'s `_TRADING_THRESHOLDS` registry updated with both new constants. Full suite green (8293 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green. Not yet deployed at the time of this entry -- threshold/retracement values explicitly tentative, to be recalibrated once real signal volume is observed against `dex_score_log.py`.

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject  : Item #170 verified ALREADY SOLVED (Birdeye, 21/07) -- not a real gap, plus verbose logging added to the sourcing funnel and the golden-pocket watch mechanism (Item #182)
Date : 2026.07.28 / Problem : operator observation ("j'ia compris que les scan ne prend pas tous les tokens mais seulement ceux en tendance ... il faut vraiment le changer ... comme reppo et les autres qui y sont pas") -- suspected `discover_momentum_candidates` only sources trending/fresh tokens (`base_crawler.discover_base_tokens`: GeckoTerminal new+trending pools only; DexScreener token_profiles_latest/recent_updates + token_boosts_latest/top: also freshness/buzz-oriented), which would structurally miss an established-but-not-currently-buzzing token like REPLY. This exact backlog item (#170, "nouvelle source de découverte par volume >=500$/24h, peu importe l'age") was already pending, suggesting a real gap.
Solution : verified live in the prod container (`docker exec aria-api`, read-only, no code/data modification -- two separate attempts to test with a container file copy and with the real `.env` API keys were both correctly blocked by the session's own security classifier and NOT worked around) that this gap is ALREADY CLOSED: `services/birdeye.py` (built 21/07, confirmed live) does a real bulk-filtered scan (`/defi/v3/token/list`, `min_liquidity`/`min_volume_24h_usd` params, NOT a trending/ranking endpoint) already wired into `discover_momentum_candidates` via `_discover_birdeye_base_tokens`. Real call against the live API returned 501 Base tokens at the exact momentum thresholds (liquidity>=50k$, volume>=500$) in 6 paginated requests -- and REPLY's real contract (`0x05B1266DDCeE093cE060DBF697e230EA9B453633`) IS present in that list. The actual reason REPLY never opened a position was never the sourcing funnel -- it's the golden-pocket/RSI gate itself (already documented earlier this same day, and the reason Item #182 above exists). Item #170 marked resolved without new code -- building a second bulk-by-threshold source would have been pure duplication of Birdeye's existing, already-verified-working one. Operator's second request in the same message ("ajoute pleins de log ... que l'on sache pourquoi sa marche ou pourquoi sa marche pas") acted on instead: `discover_momentum_candidates` now logs each source's NET contribution (candidates it added that no earlier source in the same pass already brought in, not its raw payload size -- a source that only re-surfaces already-known tokens now visibly shows 0 rather than looking productive) plus a summary line (total before/after the liquidity pre-filter). `_golden_pocket_watch_candidate`/`evaluate_momentum_entry`'s golden-pocket-watch branch now logs the EXACT reason a candidate was skipped at every decision point (no computable zone, insufficient retracement with the real numbers, score unresolved, score below threshold, or the entry gate never even attempted with mode/chain/in_gp/price) and the exact levels when a watch IS created. `limit_orders._reanalyze_dex_quality_for_watching` now logs the honeypot/score re-check outcome (confirmed/degraded/cancelled) at the pending->watching transition. `momentum_entry.py`/`limit_orders.py` -- no behavior change (logging only), full suite reconfirmed green (8293 passed, 17 skipped) after the addition, `test_coherence.py` green. Not yet deployed at the time of this entry.

[CODE] Subject  : Codex.io added as new OHLCV cascade tier + wallet-scoring routed through the SAME shared cascade (Items #185/#186)
Date : 2026.07.29 / Problem : diagnosing the wallet_scan_queue live-lock (same-day incident, see HANDOFF_WALLET_SCORING.md) surfaced a real gap -- `smart_money.py`'s wallet-scoring path called GeckoTerminal directly for OHLCV (`gecko.get_ohlcv`) with ZERO fallback, unlike the momentum pipeline's already-existing 6-stage cascade (GeckoTerminal -> CoinMarketCap -> Mobula -> DexPaprika -> DexScreener synthesis -> Dune). Separately, the operator asked for one more real provider in the cascade itself (Codex.io, after the operator independently created a real account and confirmed the free tier live from their own dashboard: 10,000 req/month, 5 req/s, 1 API key).
Solution : new `services/codex.py` -- GraphQL client (`https://graph.codex.io/graphql`, `Authorization: <API_KEY>` header, raw key per docs.codex.io/concepts/authentication, directly confirmed live against 3 official doc pages on 29/07, not just search summaries) using the `getBars` query (parallel `o`/`h`/`l`/`c`/`t`/`volume` arrays, Base networkId=8453/Ethereum networkId=1, confirmed via docs.codex.io/networks). Inserted into `momentum_entry._fetch_candles` AFTER DexPaprika, BEFORE the DexScreener degraded synthesis -- Codex's free-tier budget (10k req/month) is by far the scarcest of any provider in this cascade, so it's spent only once everything cheaper/higher-volume has already failed; standard mode only, never wired into scalping (too costly a budget for that call volume). A dedicated monthly counter (`codex_request_log` table) enforces an internal cap of 9,500/month (95% of the real 10,000, 5% safety margin) -- fails CLOSED on this provider only (skip, cascade degrades to the next tier), never blocking. No `CODEX_IO_API_KEY` existed in this environment at build time -- this client has never been live-tested against the real API (same pattern already accepted in this project for Webacy: committed dormant, gated by key absence, every caller already degrades gracefully).
`_fetch_candles` gained two new optional parameters (`gecko_client=None`, `min_useful_candles=None`) so `smart_money.py` could reuse this SAME cascade for its own GeckoTerminal stage instead of bypassing it -- `gecko_client` defaults to the real singleton (every existing momentum caller passes nothing, zero behavior change), `min_useful_candles` is forwarded ONLY to the first GeckoTerminal stage, restoring the #182 (15/07) speed optimization (wallet-scoring only ever consumes a single candle via `price_at`) that would otherwise have been silently lost. Real regression caught and fixed BEFORE commit (not after): `test_smart_money_wallet_scoring.py`'s existing `FakeGeckoTerminalClient` injection (15 tests) broke immediately once `_analyze_wallet_multi_token` called `momentum_entry.fetch_candles(...)` without threading its own `gecko` parameter through -- fixed by passing `gecko_client=gecko`, restoring the test double's control over the cascade's first stage exactly as before. Pool resolution itself (`gecko.resolve_primary_pool`, which also feeds the anti-dust/scam-pool liquidity floor) deliberately left untouched -- only the OHLCV fetch was rerouted.
`services/codex.py` (new), `momentum_entry.py`, `services/smart_money.py` -- 23 new tests (`test_codex_client.py`: gating/ladder/dome retry/parallel-array parsing/monthly counter), 4 new tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: Codex fallback ordering, never tried when DexPaprika succeeds, skipped when not configured, never wired into scalping), full suite green (8336 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Scalping pocket had ZERO positions ever -- stray `mode != "scalping"` exclusion on the watch-RSI-divergence branch (Item #199)
Date : 2026.07.29 / Problem : operator asked for a live status check of the 3-pocket system ("verifie comment se passe les scan de token, scoring signaux divergence"). Verified in prod (paper_position/paper_position_archive): the scalping pocket had 0 positions ever opened AND 0 ever closed, despite `multi_pocket_sourcing_enabled()` being on and continuous candidate evaluation. Live logs showed real golden-pocket hits (`in_gp=True`) for scalping candidates immediately discarded as "golden-pocket watch not even attempted".
Solution : `momentum_entry.py`'s watch-RSI-divergence branch (#183, 28/07) carried a `mode != "scalping"` exclusion copy-pasted from its neighbor #182 (golden-pocket watch), which has its OWN valid reason (a multi-HOUR wait doesn't fit a 15-30min timeframe). #183's own horizon is counted in CANDLES (`RSI_WATCH_MIN/MAX_SPAN`), never elapsed wall time -- never actually timeframe-dependent, and the exclusion was never documented as intentional for this specific branch. Removed. Second gap found while fixing the first: `limit_orders.check_rsi_divergence_watching_order`'s periodic re-check called `momentum_entry._fetch_candles` WITHOUT ever passing `mode=`, always defaulting to `"standard"` -- would have silently re-fetched the wrong timeframe (1h+ candles instead of 15-30min) for a scalping order's re-check, corrupting the divergence detection even once the exclusion above was lifted. Now derives `mode` from `order["wallet"]` (already stored on every pending order, "scalping" -> mode "scalping", else "standard") -- no new field needed, reuses the existing canonical source.
Separately diagnosed the same session, tracked apart (different root cause, same "empty pocket" symptom): the VC pocket has ALSO never received a single candidate -- `screened_token` has 2947 rows, 0 ever "active" (2180 pending, 767 rejected), so `paper_trader.top_candidates()` (which only reads `status="active"`) starves the VC pocket at the SOURCING level, unrelated to this fix. See backlog Item #200, not yet investigated.
`momentum_entry.py`, `limit_orders.py` -- test coverage: inverted the stale test asserting the old exclusion (`test_momentum_entry.py`), added coverage for the mode derivation in the periodic re-check (`test_limit_orders.py`: scalping wallet -> mode "scalping", swing wallet -> mode "standard"). Full suite green (8402 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Every scalping position sized at the MAX conviction tier (5%) regardless of R/R -- missing align_score on limit-order watch candidates (Item #221)
Date : 2026.07.29 / Problem : operator observation, live ("pourquoi en scalping toute les positions sont a 50k c voulu ?") -- verified in prod (`paper_position`): all 11 open scalping positions sized at exactly $50,000 (5% of the $1M pocket), with real R/R as low as 0.3-0.6. Traced to `_golden_pocket_watch_candidate`/`_rsi_divergence_watch_candidate` (the two builders behind every scalping limit order -- scalping never buys outright on this path) never setting `align_score` on their returned dict. `risk_guard.conviction_risk_budget_pct`/`conviction_size_multiplier` treat a missing `align_score` as "caller doesn't support this signal" and silently fall back to `MAX_ALLOC_MULTIPLIER` -- a fallback explicitly documented as intentional ONLY for the old, dormant VC-thesis pilot (which never supplies `rr`/`align_score` at all), never meant for this path.
Solution : `_technical_alignment(candles)` (already computed elsewhere in the direct-buy path, same EMA/MACD/candlestick-pattern scoring) is now computed once in `evaluate_momentum_entry`, right before the golden-pocket/rsi-divergence branch (candles already fetched, confirmed non-empty), and injected as `watch["align_score"]` on whichever builder's dict comes back -- rather than threading `candles` through golden-pocket's own signature just for this. The same dict is persisted verbatim into `pending_limit_order.signal_json` and re-read by `compute_entry_alloc` both at order-creation time (size estimate) and at trigger time (final sizing), so the fix reaches both. Same-session, separately diagnosed and confirmed NOT a bug (both raised alongside the sizing question): a scalping total unrealized P&L briefly reading -347$ while individual positions looked flat/positive was real market noise on 11 small positions (recomputed live: +2,270$ moments later, same positions) -- `_default_pair_lookup` already correctly filters pairs by `base_address` (07/19 fix, PLAZM/ESHARE) so no quote-token mispricing was involved; and a ~24min lull in new limit orders was normal candidate-flow variance (activity resumed on its own before any fix was applied).
`momentum_entry.py` -- 3 new/updated tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: `align_score` populated on both watch-candidate paths, and a dedicated end-to-end test proving a weak-R/R (~1.6) rsi-divergence watch candidate now resolves the WEAK risk_guard tier (0.5%/2%) rather than the MAX/strong one (1.5%/5%)). Full suite green (8475 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.
