[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Price/candle sanity check on the OHLCV cascade -- GeckoTerminal returned candles at a completely wrong price scale for one real pool (Item #222)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : guardian-mode audit (post-Item #221 monitoring, operator absent) found a real ordering-of-magnitude bug live: a golden-pocket-watch limit order for NPC (0xb166e8b140d35d9d8226e40c09f757bac5a4d87d) had `target=$1918.98`/`invalidation=$1850.34` while the token's real spot price (confirmed via DexScreener, same pool address, `_best_pair`'s own `base_address` filter already correct) is $0.005751 -- a ~330,000x scale mismatch. Reproduced live: `geckoterminal_client.get_ohlcv` called directly against the exact same pool_address returned genuinely wrong candles (~$1873-1958, the same order of magnitude as ETH) -- a real data bug on GeckoTerminal's side for this specific pool, not a logic error in our own pool resolution. Had this order triggered, the resulting position's stop/target would sit at a price level the real token could structurally never reach, defeating the trailing stop's entire protective purpose. A DB-wide scan confirmed this was isolated to this one token (3 affected `pending_limit_order` rows, all NPC, out of 70 total) -- all 3 were already self-resolved (`state="cancelled"`, `cancel_reason="invalidation_crossed"`, the existing invalidation-vs-spot-price check on the watching path caught the mismatch and cancelled within minutes of entering "watching") -- no real position was ever opened on these absurd levels.
Solution : `_fetch_candles` (the shared 6-stage OHLCV cascade used by every caller in the codebase) is renamed internally to `_fetch_candles_impl` (unchanged body) and wrapped by a new `_fetch_candles` that applies `_candles_price_consistent(candles, pair)` on the final result -- rejects (treats as `[]`, same "OHLCV unavailable" degradation already in place) if the candles' last close diverges by more than 1000x from `pair.price_usd` (the already-independently-confirmed real spot price, same `PairSnapshot` every caller already resolves via `_best_pair`'s `base_address` filter). Deliberately checks only the CASCADE'S FINAL result rather than each of the 6 stages individually -- a real tradeoff (an inconsistent GeckoTerminal result isn't retried against CoinMarketCap/Mobula/etc., the caller just gets an extra HOLD instead) chosen for safety and low regression risk over completeness: touching 9 separate return points inside an already heavily-tested, critical cascade for a bug observed exactly once, on exactly one token, was judged disproportionate. Tolerance deliberately very wide (1000x) -- a last-resort sanity net against a catastrophic scale mismatch, never meant to second-guess normal price volatility. All 4 real call sites (`evaluate_momentum_entry`, `limit_orders.py`'s rsi-divergence re-check, `paper_trader.py`'s weekly-reset robust close price, `paper_trader.py`'s scalping bearish-divergence exit check) already pass `pair=`, so all are covered by this one change with zero call-site edits needed.
`momentum_entry.py` -- 2 new tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: rejects a >1000x-inconsistent candle set, accepts a consistent one) confirming the existing 344 tests already covering `_fetch_candles`'s 6-stage cascade (which all pass `pair=None` or an already-consistent `pair`) needed zero changes. Full suite green (8477 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green. No cleanup action needed on the 3 already-cancelled NPC orders.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : `momentum_discovery_cycle` lowered 30 -> 15 min -- scalping missed a full pump/reversal on PRXVT between two passes (Item #233)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator diagnostic request on a real DexScreener PRXVT/VIRTUAL chart showing a clean pump-then-reversal ("encore une fois le scalping arrive trop tard"). Traced via `momentum_scan_log` (20 rows, hold_reason varied incl. 4h of `honeypot_unavailable`) + the real DexScreener liquidity/volume/priceChange + the source code of both discovery paths: `momentum_websocket.py` (~30s drain, but scoped to 4 DexScreener boost/profile ENDPOINTS -- structurally blind to an organic price/volume move, PRXVT never matched them) and `momentum_discovery_cycle` (the periodic REST scan, wide multi-source sourcing via `discover_momentum_candidates`, but running at 30min -- too slow for a pump that fully forms and unwinds inside a single 15-30min scalping window).
Solution : operator approved 2 fixes (A: tighten the periodic cadence, B: broaden the WebSocket's own criteria) -- this entry is A only, B is a separate, more structural design not yet scoped. Lowered `momentum_discovery_cycle` 30 -> 15min (`heartbeat.py`). The 07/23 comment justifying 30min ("15min would stress the calibrated GoPlus/GeckoTerminal ceilings") is now stale: Item #212-217 (29/07) already moved `_check_honeypot` off a synchronous GoPlus call on this exact path (it now reads `goplus_watchlist.get_fresh()`, a separately-refreshed 5min background cycle, HOLDing as `honeypot_pending` on a miss rather than calling the network here) -- GoPlus is no longer a shared constraint for this cadence at all. Verified the remaining real costs (GeckoTerminal OHLCV, 27/min calibrated; DexScreener sourcing, 60/min confirmed, `docs/api-rate-limit-calibration.md`) are both enforced by their own client-level throttle regardless of how often this cycle fires, and `_run_cycle_lock` already forbids two passes from overlapping -- halving the interval raises the total hourly candidate volume, never the instantaneous rate against either ceiling.
`heartbeat.py` -- no test locks this interval's exact value (verified before changing it); full suite green (8493 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Manual candidate injection into discovery pool -- 7th source, /add + bare-paste (Item #236)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator diagnostic ("pourquoi que 1 par heure" on GoPlus watchlist growth) traced the 6 automated discovery sources (`base_crawler` GeckoTerminal new/trending pools, Birdeye bulk, 4 DexScreener boost/profile endpoints) to real structural caps (GeckoTerminal not paginated beyond page 1, Birdeye/DexScreener endpoints each narrow in scope) -- operator wanted to manually inject tokens spotted on a DexScreener screener page (dozens visible at once) while the automated sources' coverage improves separately.
Solution : new `manual_candidates.py` (persisted SQLite queue, 7-day TTL if never bought, cleaned up via `remove_manual_candidate` on a successful `open_position` regardless of source). Drained as a 7th source in `discover_momentum_candidates` (`momentum_entry.py`) -- goes through the exact same hard gates (honeypot/liquidity/volume/wash-trading/holder concentration/R-R) as any auto-discovered candidate, never a buy shortcut. Two entry points on the operator side: `/add <addr1> [addr2 ...] [chain]` (admin, multiple space/newline-separated addresses per message, default chain "base"), and a bare-paste auto-detect in `_handle_message` (`_looks_like_bare_manual_candidates`) for a message consisting ONLY of address(es) -- no `/add` prefix needed, since the operator plans to paste tokens one at a time while scrolling a screener. Deliberately does NOT fire on a message containing more than one non-address token (avoids misfiring on a real question that merely mentions an address). A duplicate submission is a silent no-op (`INSERT OR IGNORE`, original `added_at`/TTL preserved).
`manual_candidates.py` (new), `momentum_entry.py`, `paper_trader.py`, `gateway/telegram_bot.py` -- new `test_manual_candidates.py` (9 tests) + `test_telegram_add_command.py` (7 tests) + additions to `test_momentum_entry.py`/`test_paper_trader.py`. Full suite green (8575 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Manual candidate injection from a screenshot -- multi-ticker extraction, explicit-caption-only (Item #236 follow-up, #237)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator wanted a THIRD, faster way to feed the /add queue (Item #236 above): a real DexScreener screener page shows dozens of tokens at once ("ou mieux aria ajoute les tokens avec le screenshot que je lui envoie") -- retyping each ticker/contract manually defeats the point of a screener view.
Solution : extended `skills/image_token_curiosity.py` (the existing single-ticker "verified curiosity" check from 26/07, which resolves ONE ticker per image for pure informational context, never a write) with `_extract_all_tickers` (multi-line LLM vision extraction, deduplicated, capped at `_MAX_TICKERS_PER_IMAGE=30` against a runaway/hallucinated list) + `queue_tokens_from_screenshot` (resolves each ticker to a real contract via the SAME `_pick_dominant_match` dominant-liquidity logic -- never guesses between unrelated tokens sharing a symbol -- and queues every resolved one into `manual_candidates`, same downstream gates as `/add`). Gated behind a NEW explicit caption signal (`_caption_requests_manual_add`, `/add`/"ajoute"/"add" keyword) in `_handle_vision_photo` -- deliberately NOT the default/unconditional path: a screenshot sent for an ordinary question (e.g. "is this legit?") must never silently queue tokens as a side effect, same doctrine already applied to `_caption_is_avatar_upload` (incident #147). Always reports resolved vs. unresolved counts, never a silent/bare confirmation.
`skills/image_token_curiosity.py`, `gateway/telegram_bot.py` -- new tests in `test_image_token_curiosity.py` (8 tests: extraction, dedup, cap, queue+report, degradation on failure) + `test_telegram_photo_handler.py` (6 tests: caption keyword detection, routing away from the normal LLM vision reply, non-regression on an ordinary caption). Full suite green (8589 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Cross-chain false ambiguity in the screenshot-queue resolver -- real Base tokens (GITLAWB, KellyClaude) misreported as unresolved (Item #236/#237 follow-up, #238)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator live-tested Item #237 (screenshot queuing) and pointed out two of the 6 "unresolved" tickers from the real DexScreener trending screenshot were, in fact, real liquid Base tokens (GITLAWB, $1.09M liquidity; KellyClaude, $648K) -- verified live against DexScreener's own search API (`api.dexscreener.com/latest/dex/search`): both had a SAME-TICKER pair on a chain this pipeline never trades ("robinhood" $2.71M for GITLAWB, "ethereum" $406K for KellyClaude) sitting within `_DOMINANT_LIQUIDITY_RATIO` (3x) of the real Base match by raw liquidity -- `_pick_dominant_match` compared liquidity across ALL chains blind to what `momentum_entry.DEFAULT_CHAINS` (`("base",)`, verified) can actually trade, so an out-of-scope chain's pair could out-rank or too-closely-trail the real match and trip the ambiguity guard for a token that was never actually ambiguous within the chains this pipeline cares about.
Solution : `_pick_dominant_match` gains an optional `chains: tuple[str, ...] | None` kwarg -- when given, restricts the candidate pool to those chains (case-insensitive) BEFORE the dominance comparison, so an unsupported-chain pair can no longer poison the decision for the one chain that matters (it's dropped downstream anyway by `_add_candidate`'s own chain filter, so comparing against it was pure noise). `queue_tokens_from_screenshot` now passes `chains=momentum_entry.DEFAULT_CHAINS`. `verify_token_mention` (the original 26/07 single-ticker informational curiosity check, never gated by what the pipeline trades) is UNCHANGED -- calls `_pick_dominant_match` without `chains`, same unscoped behavior as before.
`skills/image_token_curiosity.py` -- 5 new unit tests on `_pick_dominant_match` (chain-scoping, still-ambiguous-within-scope, chains-case-insensitive) + 1 integration test reproducing the exact GITLAWB incident end-to-end through `queue_tokens_from_screenshot`. Full suite green (8594 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Same-token false ambiguity in the screenshot-queue resolver -- a real single contract's OWN second pool mistaken for a competing token (BRETT) (Item #238 follow-up, #239)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator live-tested the Item #238 fix and asked directly why BRETT (a real, extremely well-known, single Base contract) still reported "unresolved". Verified live against DexScreener's search API + the real codebase `search_pairs` call: BRETT has exactly ONE Base address (`0x532f27101965dd16442e59d40670faf5ebb142e4`) but 19 distinct pools across Uniswap/Aerodrome/Pancakeswap/Sushiswap/Hydrex/Baseswap -- `_pick_dominant_match` compared RAW POOL liquidity without grouping by address first, so the token's own 2nd-biggest pool ($598K, Aerodrome) got compared against its own main pool ($903K, Uniswap) as if they were two DIFFERENT competing tokens (ratio 1.51x, under the 3x `_DOMINANT_LIQUIDITY_RATIO` threshold) -- tripping the ambiguity guard against itself. A real, single, liquid token was misreported as ambiguous with no second real contract involved at all.
Solution : `_pick_dominant_match` now groups the exact-symbol (and chain-scoped, per Item #238) candidates by `base_address` FIRST, ranks by AGGREGATE (summed) liquidity across each address's own pools, and only then runs the dominance comparison BETWEEN DISTINCT addresses -- a token's own additional pools can never again be mistaken for a competing token. Returns the single most-liquid pool of the winning address (same fields callers need: `base_address`/`chain_id`/price/volume for `verify_token_mention`'s display). Applies unconditionally (both the chain-scoped queue path and the unscoped `verify_token_mention` informational path) since the root flaw -- comparing at pool granularity instead of token granularity -- was never specific to the chain-scoping fix.
`skills/image_token_curiosity.py` -- 3 new unit tests on `_pick_dominant_match` (own-pools-never-a-competitor reproducing BRETT exactly, distinct-tokens-still-compared-by-aggregate-liquidity, distinct-tokens-with-multiple-pools-still-ambiguous-when-genuinely-close) + 1 integration test reproducing the exact BRETT incident end-to-end through `queue_tokens_from_screenshot`. Full suite green (8598 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Manually-queued candidates silently starved by discovery-list truncation + fast-track watchlist entry (Item #236/#241)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : real DB audit at operator's request ("cest pas normal quelle reste a 117") found 35 of 42 genuinely-new manually-queued candidates had NEVER been evaluated (no `momentum_scan_log`/`momentum_rejection_cache` row at all, not rejected -- just never reached). Root cause: `discover_momentum_candidates` appended manual candidates LAST (after base_crawler + Birdeye), and the caller (`paper_trader._momentum_candidates_and_chain_map`) hard-truncates the merged list to 20 BEFORE any evaluation -- whenever automated sources alone contributed >=20 net-new entries (routine), every manual candidate silently fell past the cap. Separately, the operator clarified the real goal ("je veux juste qu'ils entrent rapidement dans la watchlist") after finding `evaluate_hard_gates` checks honeypot LAST (after liquidity/volume/wash-trading) -- a manually-curated (already visibly liquid) token was routinely rejected on `volume_too_low` before ever reaching the honeypot check / `goplus_watchlist` at all.
Solution : (1) manual candidates are now processed FIRST in `discover_momentum_candidates` (before the 6 automated sources), so they always claim the front of the merged list and survive the downstream `[:20]` truncation -- consequence, accepted (matches "je remplis moi-meme"): automated discovery is effectively paused on a pass whenever the manual backlog alone reaches the cap. (2) `manual_candidates.add_manual_candidate` now ALSO queues directly into `goplus_watchlist` (`add_or_touch`) at add-time, decoupled entirely from the buy-decision gates -- being in the watchlist only means "honeypot status kept warm in the background" (Item #212), never "cleared to buy"; the buy decision still runs the exact same full gate sequence regardless. New optional `liquidity_usd`/`volume_24h_usd` kwargs feed a real priority score (via `goplus_watchlist.compute_priority_score`) when already known (`queue_tokens_from_screenshot`'s own DexScreener match) -- `None` (the bare `/add` case) degrades to a neutral 0.0 score, which still claims a slot given the watchlist has room (117/600 at the time).
`momentum_entry.py`, `manual_candidates.py`, `skills/image_token_curiosity.py` -- 1 new test on candidate ordering (`test_discover_momentum_candidates_manual_queue_processed_before_automated_sources`) + 4 new tests on watchlist queueing (real score from real data, neutral default, best-effort failure tolerance, actual DB row written). Full suite green (8603 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Two bonding-score malus mechanisms tied to low market cap / bonding status removed, per new standing operator directive (Item #242)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : while hunting a sub-$25K-mcap candidate at the operator's request, HOLO (a legitimate, well-known project, 307 holders, 66.7/100 on fundamentals alone) was cut to 33.4/100 by the #161/#162 "organic decline" (staleness) penalty, purely for having stayed non-graduated (i.e. small/in bonding) a while. The operator's ruling on seeing this: "non aria doit jamais appliquer de malus sur un jeton a faible market cap et surtout en bonding" -- ARIA must never score a token down specifically for being small or still in bonding. Audit of `bonding_entry.py` found exactly two mechanisms that fit that pattern: (1) the staleness penalty (fires ONLY on small/non-graduated tokens), (2) the holder-concentration score below `_MIN_HOLDERS_FOR_CONCENTRATION_CHECK` (50), which a 28/07 research pass had lowered from a neutral half (7.5/15) to a near-zero fraction (0.2, i.e. 3.0/15) specifically because the token had too few holders -- itself a direct function of being new/small.
Solution : (1) `_staleness_penalty_multiplier` and its constants (`_STALENESS_DAYS_THRESHOLD`/`_STALENESS_MAX_DAYS`/`_STALENESS_MAX_PENALTY_PCT`/`_STALENESS_WAIVER_POSTING_CADENCE`) removed entirely from `bonding_entry.py`, along with its invocation in `evaluate_bonding_entry` -- never tuned, since any tuning would still be "a malus for staying small/bonding," exactly what's now ruled out. (2) `_HOLDER_CONCENTRATION_UNINFORMATIVE_SCORE_FRACTION` reverted 0.2 -> 0.5 (back to the pre-28/07 neutral-half value) -- below the sample-size floor the ratio is genuinely uninformative, so it's scored neutral (benefit of the doubt), never low. `conviction_posting_cadence` (the field staleness used to consume) is unaffected -- it's still surfaced in the result dict as general context, just no longer feeds a penalty. Structural hard gates unrelated to this pattern (min liquidity $5,000 as a corrupted-data floor, dev-holding rug gate, honeypot-equivalent checks) are untouched -- this directive targets score-scaling malus, not sanity floors.
`bonding_entry.py` -- removed the entire staleness test section (11 tests) from `test_bonding_entry.py`, updated 2 worked-example assertions whose expected `bonding_score` shifted with the fraction change (68.4->72.9, 28.8->33.3, both verdicts unchanged). Also updated the two mechanical guardrails this change touches: `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` (removed the 4 staleness rows, added a removal note + the reverted holder-concentration fraction) and `test_coherence.py::_TRADING_THRESHOLDS` (dropped the 4 staleness constants, now tracks `_HOLDER_CONCENTRATION_UNINFORMATIVE_SCORE_FRACTION=0.5` instead) -- both required by `test_trading_thresholds_match_calibration_doc`, which caught the drift on the first full-suite run. Full suite green (8603 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Scalping limit-order R/R silently rejected at the exact #231 floor -- rounding-before-threshold-comparison bug (Item #243)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator noticed scalping buys that used to go through ~12h earlier (R/R around 1.0) had stopped clearing at R/R around 1.25, and asked directly whether the new limit-order mechanism was the cause. Investigation confirmed the timeline: commit 0b69606d (Item #231, 08:43 UTC same day, ~13h before the report) introduced the FIRST-EVER R/R floor on scalping/swing limit-order candidates (`limit_orders._RR_MIN_LIMIT_ORDER_SCALPING=1.25`) -- before it, ANY R/R (even the wIRON 0.3 that motivated #231) could become a watched limit order. Digging further into the exact comparison found a real, independent precision bug: `momentum_entry._rsi_divergence_watch_candidate`/`_golden_pocket_watch_candidate` computed `rr = round((target-entry)/(entry-invalidation), 1)` -- rounding to ONE decimal BEFORE the floor check. Python's round-half-to-even makes an exact 1.25 round DOWN to 1.2 (1.2 and 1.3 are equidistant from 1.25, 1.2 is the even digit) -- `limit_orders.meets_limit_order_rr_floor`'s `rr >= 1.25` then saw 1.2 < 1.25 and rejected a candidate the floor's own stated design says should pass. Confirmed with real (non-contrived) floating-point arithmetic: entry/target/invalidation values constructed so the TRUE intended ratio is exactly 1.25 compute back as 1.249999999999999 due to ordinary float rounding in the division chain -- genuinely at the floor, silently rejected.
Solution : both watch-candidate builders now round to 4 decimals instead of 1 (`round(..., 4)`) -- shrinks the ambiguous tie-window from 0.1 to 0.0001, which real entry/target/invalidation prices essentially never land on by coincidence, while keeping the stored value clean for display (never raw double-precision noise). Not a full removal of rounding (which would need touching every downstream consumer of `rr`'s display) -- a pragmatic, well-tested precision bump that closes the reported gap. The pre-existing #231 floor mechanism itself (`meets_limit_order_rr_floor`) was already correct in isolation (its own test already covered `rr=1.25` passing) -- the bug lived entirely in the CALLER computing that input value.
`momentum_entry.py` -- 1 new regression test (`test_rsi_divergence_watch_candidate_rr_survives_the_scalping_floor_at_the_exact_boundary`) reproducing the exact real-world floating-point scenario (intended R/R 1.25, computed as 1.249999999999999, must still pass the floor after the fix). Full suite green (8604 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Item #231's R/R floor on limit-order candidates REMOVED, operator's explicit call (Item #245)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : same day Item #231's floor shipped (08:43 UTC), scalping trade volume dropped to zero -- operator, watching this live and after Item #243's rounding-bug investigation (which confirmed the floor itself, not just the rounding, was structurally new and stricter than the prior "no floor at all" state), gave a direct, explicit instruction: "supprime l'ordre limite je suis sur que c sa le problème". Not a bug investigation this time -- an operator decision to prioritize scalping trade volume/diagnostic signal over the specific guard #231 added.
Solution : `limit_orders.meets_limit_order_rr_floor` and its constants (`_RR_MIN_LIMIT_ORDER_SCALPING=1.25`/`_RR_MIN_LIMIT_ORDER_SWING=2.0`) removed entirely, along with the call site in `paper_trader.py` (right before a watch-and-wait limit order is placed) -- a watch candidate is never again rejected for having a low/degenerate R/R, restoring the pre-#231 behavior. Item #243's rounding-precision fix (round to 4 decimals instead of 1) is KEPT regardless -- accurate R/R computation is good practice independent of whether any gate currently reads it, and protects any future floor from the same bug. **Disclosed, accepted tradeoff**: the wIRON incident that originally motivated #231 (R/R=0.3, +1.0% target vs -3.7% invalidation, risking ~3.3x what's on the table) can recur -- the operator made this call directly, aware of the history (documented in this same file's Item #231 entry above, kept for full context/reference if the floor needs reintroducing later with a different calibration).
`limit_orders.py`/`paper_trader.py` -- removed 6 unit tests (`test_meets_limit_order_rr_floor_*`) and 1 integration test (`test_run_cycle_rejects_golden_pocket_watch_candidate_below_rr_floor`) that verified the now-reverted rejection behavior; updated comments in `risk_guard.py`/`momentum_entry.py` that referenced the removed constants. Full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green (floor constants were never tracked in `_TRADING_THRESHOLDS`, no registry drift to fix).

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : 24h volume floor removed from `evaluate_hard_gates`, operator's explicit call, same session (Item #246)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : immediately after Item #245 (removing the limit-order R/R floor), operator asked directly whether the 24h volume floor had also been touched ("le volume horaire minimum tu la supprimer en même temps aussi ?") -- confirmed no, it was untouched and still active. RVOL (`_check_volume_confirmation`, step 13, later in the pipeline) remains fully independent and unaffected -- it still rejects a BUY whose triggering candle isn't backed by real per-candle volume (>= 3x the 10-candle average), a narrower, later-stage check that was never part of this 24h floor.
`momentum_entry.py` -- removed 4 tests (`test_evaluate_rejects_volume_below_floor`, `test_evaluate_allows_volume_at_or_above_required_floor`, `test_evaluate_rejects_zombie_market_on_a_large_pool`, `test_evaluate_allows_volume_meeting_ratio_on_large_pool`) that verified the now-removed rejection behavior; cleaned up a now-unnecessary `_MIN_VOLUME_24H_USD` monkeypatch in `test_evaluate_ratio_check_skipped_when_liquidity_zero` (the gate it was neutralizing no longer exists). `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` updated (both constants noted as Birdeye-only now, no drift in `test_coherence.py`'s `_TRADING_THRESHOLDS` since neither constant's VALUE changed). Full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : New RSI-divergence angle/length log -- which setups get bought, perform, or get refused (Item #247)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : operator request ("créer un log qui référence l'inclinaison de la divergence en degré pour savoir lesquelles sont acheter, lequel fonctionne le mieux, lesquels sont souvent refuser"), later extended same day ("aussi la longueur de la divergence") -- `gap`/`span` (RSI points / candles, Item #183) already existed on `EntrySignal`/`RsiDivergenceDetail` but were never persisted anywhere, so no way to correlate a divergence's "steepness" (or raw candle length) against what actually happened to it.
Solution : new module `rsi_divergence_log.py` -- append-only table (contract/chain/symbol/wallet/mode/gap/span/angle_deg/outcome/recorded_at). `angle_deg = degrees(atan2(gap, span))` -- a project-specific definition (no standard TA term), documented explicitly so it's never re-derived differently elsewhere; `None` whenever gap/span aren't both known, never fabricated. 4 outcomes: `bought_direct` (divergence already confirmed at the same scan that decided BUY, `momentum_entry.evaluate_momentum_entry` -- `rsi_gap`/`rsi_span` now surfaced on its result dict, already computed on `EntrySignal` since Item #183 but never exposed there before), `bought_via_limit_order` (the CONFIRMED divergence at trigger time, re-checked on fresh candles by `limit_orders.check_rsi_divergence_watching_order`, which now also sets `sig["rsi_gap"]`/`sig["rsi_span"]` in place before returning "trigger"), `expired_unconfirmed`/`cancelled_unconfirmed` (a watch placed but never confirmed in time or invalidated first -- no angle, these rows answer "how often refused" by sheer count). Wired into `limit_orders.process_active_orders` (all 3 non-trigger exit points that existed at the time) and `paper_trader._open_new_entries_for_wallet` (direct buy, gated on both `rsi_gap`/`rsi_span` being non-None so a bonding/VC-thesis BUY is never logged here). New Telegram command `/divergence` (admin-only, read-only) shows a summary by outcome (count + avg/min/max angle AND span) plus the 10 most recent rows. Best-effort throughout (a write failure here must never break a real trading cycle).
`rsi_divergence_log.py` (new), `momentum_entry.py`, `limit_orders.py`, `paper_trader.py`, `gateway/telegram_bot.py` -- new `test_rsi_divergence_log.py` (13 tests: angle computation, persistence, outcome validation, summary/report degradation) + integration tests in `test_momentum_entry.py` (2), `test_limit_orders.py` (5: trigger sets gap/span, record_divergence called on trigger/expire/cancel, never logged for a non-divergence cancel), `test_paper_trader.py` (2: bought_direct logged with gap/span, never logged without them). Full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Rejection cache cross-pocket bug -- swing silently blocked from re-scanning contracts scalping had already rejected on ITS OWN liquidity floor (Item #228)
Date : 2026.07.30 / Problem : investigating the backlog item "why does swing never scan some tokens scalping does" -- empirically confirmed against real prod data (`momentum_scan_log`): 130 of 427 contracts scalping scanned were NEVER scanned by swing, despite both pockets sharing the exact same WebSocket candidate pool (`_drain_multi_pocket` passes the SAME `candidates` list to both, only the analyzer `mode` differs). Root cause found in `momentum_rejection_cache.py`: cached rejections are keyed by `(contract, chain)` alone, with no notion of pocket/mode -- but `insufficient_liquidity` (the one gate whose threshold genuinely depends on `mode`, `momentum_entry._MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD_SCALPING=15,000` vs `_MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD=50,000`) could be cached by ONE pocket's evaluation and then silently short-circuit the OTHER pocket's re-check for the full 2h TTL, even though that other pocket's own threshold might have produced a completely different verdict on the exact same contract (e.g. $30,000 liquidity clears scalping's floor but not standard's).
Solution : `momentum_rejection_cache.record_rejection`/`recently_rejected` gain an optional `liquidity_tier` kwarg (`"scalping"`/`"standard"`/`"fear"`) -- a new additive `mode` column (default `"shared"`) partitions the cache ONLY for `insufficient_liquidity` rejections; every other cacheable reason (wash-trading ratio, unverified profile, holder concentration) keeps the ORIGINAL shared-cache behavior, deliberately -- scoping those by pocket too would double a real paid check (holder concentration's x402 call) for zero benefit, since those thresholds never depend on the mode. `momentum_entry.evaluate_hard_gates` computes `liquidity_tier` once (from `current_regime`/`mode`, both already known, no extra network call) and reuses it for both the cache lookup and the eventual `insufficient_liquidity` write, replacing the previously-duplicated tier logic. Omitting `liquidity_tier` (no real caller left after this fix) falls back to the exact pre-#228 behavior (accepts whatever's cached, unscoped) -- fully backward compatible, all 8 pre-existing tests pass unmodified.
`momentum_rejection_cache.py`/`momentum_entry.py` -- 5 new unit tests in `test_momentum_rejection_cache.py` (tier partitioning both directions, shared reasons still block every tier, legacy no-tier-argument behavior, tier ignored for non-liquidity reasons) + 1 integration test in `test_momentum_entry.py` reproducing the exact $30,000-liquidity cross-pocket scenario end-to-end through `evaluate_hard_gates`. Full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Item #248's R/R floor on limit-order candidates REMOVED AGAIN -- operator's explicit call after a live case (Item #252)
Date : 2026.07.31 / Problem : operator asked why only 3 limit-order positions had ever triggered in the entire history and what let the +18.3% DRV trade succeed. Investigation (matching `paper_position.opened_at`/`discovery_channel` against `pending_limit_order.created_at`/`state`) found all 3 triggers (DRV rr=0.066, LSTEAK rr=0.0515, SURGE rr=1.1341) were created in the ~4-minute window between Item #245 (floor removed) and Item #248 (floor restored) the same day -- every other watch created while the floor was active, with an rr that would have failed 1.25, expired or got cancelled without ever triggering. Operator contested DRV's rr=0.066 as implausible ("cest pas possible d'avoir un r/r 0.066 reflechit") -- reverified the exact stored floats, confirmed mathematically correct (target already near a prior resistance level, invalidation widened further by the ATR floor). This session pushed back with a detailed counter-argument before the operator's final call: entry R/R and exit upside are structurally independent (the trailing-stop/staged-TP mechanism, not entry R/R, captured DRV's gain past its original technical target), the real sample was 1 latent gain vs 2 latent losses (0 closed), and concluding causation from n=3 risks survivorship bias -- recommended waiting for more data via the `/divergence` log (Item #247) instead. Operator's explicit final call, overriding the recommendation: "non enleve le".
Solution : `limit_orders._RR_MIN_LIMIT_ORDER_SCALPING`/`_RR_MIN_LIMIT_ORDER_SWING` and `meets_limit_order_rr_floor` removed entirely (function no longer exists) -- same removal as Item #245, this time without a same-day revert. **Disclosed, accepted tradeoff** (flagged directly to the operator before the final call): only 3 limit-order triggers exist in the entire history at the time of this decision, none yet closed -- too small a sample to prove entry R/R predicts exit upside either way; the wIRON-style setup that originally motivated Item #231 (mathematically indefensible at entry, R/R=0.3) can recur. If the floor needs to come back a third time, this file's own Item #231 entry above has the full original calibration (67 resolved scalping orders, median R/R 1.75) to restore it from.
`limit_orders.py`/`paper_trader.py`/`risk_guard.py` (comment) -- removed the 6 `test_meets_limit_order_rr_floor_*` unit tests and the 1 `test_run_cycle_rejects_golden_pocket_watch_candidate_below_rr_floor` integration test that Item #248 had restored. Full suite green, `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject  : Limit-order trigger dropped 6 signal fields on the floor -- pure wiring omission (entry_market_cap_usd/gp_low/gp_high/align_ema/align_macd/align_pattern)
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : `limit_orders._execute_trigger`'s call to `paper_trader.open_position` (the buy path taken by every triggered limit order, `discovery_channel="limit_order"`) never forwarded 6 fields that the two direct-buy call sites in `paper_trader.py` (`_open_new_entries_for_wallet`) already pass: `entry_market_cap_usd`, `gp_low`, `gp_high`, `align_ema`, `align_macd`, `align_pattern`. All 6 were already present on `sig` (the order's own `signal_json`, = `momentum_entry.evaluate_momentum_entry`'s unconditional return dict, persisted verbatim at `create_pending_order` time and never recomputed at trigger) -- a pure omission from the kwargs list, not a data-availability gap. Every limit-order-triggered position (the pool with the most trigger history, `rsi_divergence_pending`/scalping_v6 orders) silently persisted all 6 as None regardless of what the signal actually had.
Solution : added the 6 missing kwargs to the `open_position(...)` call in `_execute_trigger`, same `sig.get(...)` source expressions as the two direct-buy sites (verified identical). Legitimately still None after this fix for: a bonding trigger (`bonding_entry.py` has no golden-pocket/EMA-MACD-pattern/market-cap concept, its BUY dict never sets these keys) and a `golden_pocket_pending` order triggered before the golden-pocket zone had formed at signal-creation time (`gp_low`/`gp_high` genuinely unknown then, `sig` is never recomputed at trigger) -- neither is a regression, both documented inline.
`limit_orders.py` -- 2 new tests: `test_execute_trigger_forwards_market_cap_and_alignment_fields` (all 6 populated on `sig`, asserts they land on the returned position AND survive a DB re-read) and `test_execute_trigger_bonding_leaves_alignment_fields_none_no_error` (bonding chain marker, all 6 absent from `sig`, asserts None with no error). Both use `wallet="vc"` deliberately -- a "swing" order's WATCH phase routes through `check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` (31/07 fresh-divergence reconfirmation), orthogonal to this regression test. Targeted suite green: `test_limit_orders.py` 112 passed, `test_paper_trader.py` 453 passed combined (full monorepo suite not run, per task scope). `test_coherence.py` not run (no invariant touched, no `_TRADING_THRESHOLDS`/allowlist change). Committed since (`c67908a2`), not yet deployed.

[CODE] Subject  : "megacap" 7th pocket -- fixed watchlist of 10 established tokens (mcap >=100M$), additive A/B pocket alongside the 6 scan-large scalping pockets
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : operator wanted to test whether a fixed watchlist of established, deeply liquid tokens (LINK/KAITO/ICP/ENA/AAVE/VIRTUAL/WETH/cbBTC/WBTC/cbETH, all Base) scalps better than the existing 6 pockets' open-ended DexScreener/WebSocket discovery -- but a workflow found that REPLACING the scan-large pockets with this watchlist would mechanically hurt performance (0-3 trades/day vs ~18/day today). Operator's explicit call: "garde le scan large, ouvre la 7e poche pour tester" -- keep all 6 existing pockets fully unchanged, add a new, purely additive "megacap" pocket for direct comparison. Plan iterated through 7 rounds of independent 2-agent workflow validation (operator required a fresh round after every revision) before any code was written -- each round found a real, previously-unseen defect (name collisions x2, a structurally-impossible reuse of `build_scalping_pocket_entries()` that would have made the pocket silently dead-on-arrival, a global-blacklist security risk on AAVE/VIRTUAL understated as "pocket-local", a guaranteed `NameError` on 2 call sites, a same-day-independent regression that a naive uniform fix would have silently reintroduced, a missing alert-label dict entry, an ambiguous insertion instruction that would have left a stale check active alongside the new one).
Solution : new `fixed_watchlist.py` module/table (no TTL/purge, unlike `manual_candidates.py`) seeds the 10 tokens. `paper_trader.py`/`momentum_websocket.py`: megacap is NOT sourced through `build_scalping_pocket_entries()` (confirmed structurally scalping-only, every entry hardcodes `mode="scalping"`) -- built unconditionally as a 3rd static tuple entry mirroring the existing "vc" pattern exactly (`("megacap", megacap_candidates, megacap_analyzer, "standard", MAX_POSITIONS_MEGACAP)`, gated only at the OPEN attempt via `continue`, never at construction) in both files, since the two have drifted before. `momentum_entry.py`: new `_ESTABLISHED_TOKEN_SECURITY_ALLOWLIST_BASE` (AAVE + VIRTUAL addresses only, Base-only, NOT merged into the shared `is_recognized_reference_asset()` registry) covers exactly 3 confirmed-True GoPlus flags for these 2 addresses (`owner_change_balance`, `mintable`, `hidden_owner`) -- both tokens verified on Basescan as mint/burn-gated to the canonical Base bridge, same custodial pattern already exempted for cbBTC/cbETH/WBTC; every other `pattern_flags` category stays fully arbitrated. `limit_orders.py`: new `paper_trader.uses_fine_rsi_confirmation(wallet)` helper (`wallet != "vc" and not is_scalping_pocket(wallet)`) replaces 2 of 4 `wallet == "swing"` literal sites that never had scalping-specific logic to preserve; the other 2 sites (already patched same-day by an unrelated commit adding `or is_scalping_pocket(wallet)`) got only an additive `or wallet == "megacap"`, written out explicitly rather than factored, to avoid deleting that fresh fix. `_strategy_label()` fixed so a megacap alert shows "megacap", never "swing trading" (same bug class fixed same day for scalping_v1-v6). `_wallet_position_cap`/`_POCKET_LABEL`/`_TIMEFRAME_LABEL` all gained a `"megacap"` entry. Gate `ARIA_FIXED_WATCHLIST_POCKET_ENABLED` stays OFF by default -- recommended activation sequence (operational, not code-enforced): first flip with AAVE/VIRTUAL trimmed from `_DEFAULT_WATCHLIST` (8 tokens), observe a few cycles, then add the 2 lines back once the rest is confirmed clean in production.
`fixed_watchlist.py` (new, 7 tests) / `paper_trader.py` / `momentum_websocket.py` / `momentum_entry.py` (7 allowlist tests) / `limit_orders.py` (6 new tests incl. 2 direct `_order_uses_rsi_divergence_check`/`format_limit_order_placed_alert` unit tests to catch the NameError class of bug) / `gateway/telegram_bot.py` (cosmetic label) -- full monorepo suite green (8880 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green (`ARIA_FIXED_WATCHLIST_POCKET_ENABLED` registered). Commit `425d6148`. Not yet deployed -- activation is a separate operator decision requiring one more manual GoPlus re-check of the 10 addresses first.

Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : the "megacap" pocket above was code-complete but undeployed, gate OFF. Operator's explicit call to deploy and activate.
Solution : `3070d00d` trimmed AAVE/VIRTUAL out of `_DEFAULT_WATCHLIST` per the plan's own recommended activation sequence (isolates the one non-reversible risk -- a global `momentum_blacklist` write on a bad allowlist entry -- from the rest of the rollout; table confirmed absent in prod before the trim, no seed row to reconcile) -- 8 tokens live (LINK/KAITO/ICP/ENA/WETH/cbBTC/WBTC/cbETH). [CODE] Subject  : Wash-trading ratio (MAX_VOLUME_TO_LIQUIDITY_RATIO, 20x) no longer enforced on scalping pockets
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : operator's explicit reasoning after reviewing real liquidity/volume figures for the megacap watchlist (none close to the 20x threshold, KAITO's baseline ratio already at 6.19x in normal operation): "le wash trading est benefique pour nous si sa permet de gagner de largent sur de faux signaux cest juste dangereux en swing". A price move caused by wash-trading isn't inherently harmful to a FAST in/out strategy -- scalping can ride it and exit before any post-pump collapse; the risk the guardrail actually protects against (holding through a collapse once the wash-trading stops) is specific to a long-held position. Confirmed with the operator that "swing" here means the exit-discipline class (mode="standard": swing AND megacap, both long-held, same trailing-stop/staged-TP mechanism), not the literal wallet name -- megacap keeps the guardrail.
Solution : 2 call sites gated on `mode`/`position.get("mode")` (no new import, no risk of the momentum_entry<->paper_trader.py cycle this codebase already avoids): (1) `momentum_entry.py::evaluate_hard_gates` -- the entry-time check now runs only `if mode != "scalping"`, reusing the SAME `mode` param already used for the scalping liquidity floor a few lines above; (2) `paper_trader_risk.py::rescan_open_position` -- the mid-holding re-check now runs only `if position.get("mode") != "scalping"`, reusing the position's own stored `mode` column, no new field. A 3rd site (`skills/safety_screen.py`, the VC thesis screen, reuses the same `_wash_trading_ratio_confirmed` function) is untouched -- the operator's reasoning never covered vc, which isn't a fast in/out strategy either. `MAX_VOLUME_TO_LIQUIDITY_RATIO`'s VALUE stays 20.0x (unchanged, `test_coherence.py::_TRADING_THRESHOLDS` still passes) -- only the scope of its enforcement changed, documented in `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md`'s existing row for this constant (same commit).
`test_momentum_entry.py` -- 1 new test (`test_evaluate_wash_trading_ratio_never_rejects_on_scalping_mode`, confirms a sustained 91x-ratio breach never rejects in `mode="scalping"`, the same figure that DOES reject the two pre-existing `mode="standard"` tests just above it). `test_paper_trader_risk.py` -- 1 new test (`test_rescan_never_flags_ratio_on_scalping_position`, same 91x real-incident figure, `mode="scalping"` position returns `None` where the pre-existing default-mode test flags it). Targeted suite green (381 passed, `test_momentum_entry.py`+`test_paper_trader_risk.py`), `test_coherence.py` green, full monorepo suite run in progress. Not yet deployed.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : entry_atr_pct never populated on the watch-and-wait limit-order path (Item #253)
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : `entry_atr_pct` (ATR% at entry, used both by `compute_entry_alloc`'s risk/ATR sizing and by `_effective_trail_pct`'s adaptive stop width) was only ever computed on the direct-BUY branch of `evaluate_momentum_entry` -- never on the two "watch and wait" candidate builders (`_rsi_divergence_watch_candidate`/`_golden_pocket_watch_candidate`, momentum_entry.py) that back every limit order, nor recomputed at trigger time. This is scalping's ONLY limit-order mechanism (Item #199) -- confirmed live against `/opt/aria-data/aria.db` at merge time: 68 total `discovery_channel='limit_order'` positions (10 open + 58 archived), 67 with `entry_atr_pct` NULL, the sole exception a `scalping_v3` position that computes its own ATR through an unrelated mechanism (`skills/scalping_variants.py`, already fixed same-day, see the entry above the megacap one in this file). Confirmed by data, not just theory: every `limit_order`-sourced position's `cost_usd` is a round legacy-tier number (the risk/ATR hybrid sizing formula could never activate); zero of the 58 archived positions on this channel ever closed via the trailing stop (timeout/stagnation and hard invalidation dominate exits instead) -- the bug is real and structural but hadn't yet caused an observable realized loss. Iterated through 5 rounds of independent 2-agent workflow validation plus 1 targeted follow-up workflow (a price-reference mismatch found by round 5, resolved by keeping the plan's original design after a real empirical comparison) before any code was written -- each round found genuinely new issues: wrong line-range citations, test fixtures that would have silently proven nothing (flat/short candles giving `0.0`/`None` regardless of the fix), a "zero risk" mischaracterization of a partial-rollout deploy window, a test-helper call bug (`_sig(overrides={...})` instead of `_sig(entry_atr_pct=...)`), a missed downstream consumer (`_satellite_pocket_eligible`), a comment-format house-style mismatch, and a documentation instruction that would have baked an already-stale diagnostic table into this very file.
Solution : two watch-candidate builders (momentum_entry.py) now compute `entry_atr_pct = last_atr / entry` right after their own `entry` assignment (`atr_series(candles)`, zero extra network cost -- the same candles are already used a few lines below by `_invalidation_floor_pct`) -- `entry` is `price` for the RSI-divergence builder, `signal.gp_high` for the golden-pocket one (never `candles[-1].close`, verified consistent with every real downstream consumer: `_effective_trail_pct`'s `high_water=entry_price` and `risk_guard.size_by_risk_budget`'s dependence on `trail_pct`, and with the direct-BUY path's own documented `execution_price` doctrine built to avoid the exact GITLAWB staleness bug). `limit_orders.py::check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` RE-computes `entry_atr_pct` fresh on the same candles it already re-fetches for the RSI re-confirmation, right before `return "trigger"` -- overwrites the watch-candidate's snapshot (potentially hours/days stale) with a temporally-fresher one, `.setdefault`-style (never erases a real prior value if the fresh recompute isn't possible on too-short a candle set). Confirmed this single site covers BOTH watch types (`rsi_divergence_pending` and `golden_pocket_pending`) for every wallet that can produce them (`_order_uses_rsi_divergence_check`/`uses_fine_rsi_confirmation`) -- no separate golden-pocket trigger path exists. A deliberate, disclosed limitation kept as-is after a dedicated follow-up workflow comparison: the trigger-time `candles[-1].close` denominator is a DIFFERENT price reference than the `current_price` `_execute_trigger` later persists as `entry_price` (a separate DexScreener fetch) -- verified `current_price` is actually the OLDEST of the three price fetches in this call chain (captured at the very top of `process_active_orders`' loop, before two more network calls run), so chasing an exact match would relocate staleness rather than remove it; the sub-few-second drift on this ratio metric is dwarfed by the clamp bounds (`MIN/MAX_ATR_TRAIL_PCT`, 5%-40%) and the observed production range (~0.4%-28%).
No DB backfill (67 legacy NULL rows, or the 10 positions still open at merge time -- fresh count vs. an earlier draft's now-stale "3 open positions" snapshot, per this file's own doctrine against freezing DB diagnostics in permanent docs) -- consistent with this repo's existing precedent (a stale `mode="standard"` label on 5 positions, never migrated retroactively) and the "never fabricate a value" principle: an ATR recomputed today on current candles doesn't reflect the real volatility at the historical entry moment. A related, currently-dormant governance bias documented but not acted on: a position opened before this fix has structurally worse odds of `_satellite_pocket_eligible` promotion at the weekly reset than an identical post-fix peer (the stop-width difference can flip the gate's remaining-R/R check) -- verified inert for the imminent ~08/08 reset (current macro regime Neutral for both BTC/ETH, and the position's own ratcheted regime can never un-tighten once set at Neutral).
`test_momentum_entry.py` -- 4 new tests (`entry_atr_pct` on both watch-candidate builders, happy path + ATR-unavailable-stays-None, using a dedicated non-flat >=14-candle fixture, `_rising_ts_atr_candles` -- the existing `_rising_ts_candles` fixture is flat, `atr_series` returns exactly `0.0` on it, proving nothing) + 2 new tests for `_satellite_pocket_eligible` (same position, `entry_atr_pct=None` vs. a real value flips the eligibility verdict itself, not just the displayed stop width). `test_limit_orders.py` -- 4 new tests (trigger-time recompute with a dedicated fixture for the same reason `_fake_fetch_candles`'s 6 flat candles can't prove anything; preservation when the fresh recompute isn't possible; an end-to-end `_execute_trigger` proof that the recalculated value survives to the persisted position; a dedicated proof that fix #1 ALONE, without fix #2 mocked out, is already risk-bearing on real sizing/stop -- not a mere pre-trigger Telegram-estimate dict as an earlier plan draft claimed). `test_paper_trader.py` -- 1 new end-to-end test (`run_paper_cycle` -> HOLD with a watch-candidate -> `create_pending_order` -> persisted `signal_json` carries `entry_atr_pct`, covering the `order_sig = {**sig, **watch, ...}` merge site). `test_performance_breakdown.py` -- 1 new test confirming a realistic `limit_order`-sourced position now lands in a real ATR bucket instead of always `"unknown"`. Full monorepo suite green, `test_coherence.py` green (no invariant/gate/allowlist touched). Not yet committed/deployed.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : DexScreener liquidity field silently absent (not zero) on freshly-indexed pools -- misread as insufficient_liquidity (Item #30/backlog)
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : a B20-token diligence pass (Base's native token standard, factory `0xB20f000000000000000000000000000000000000`) found DexScreener omitting the `"liquidity"` key entirely from its JSON for a real pool (SOAF, ~2.04M$ 24h volume) -- `dexscreener.py`'s parser (`liquidity_usd=float(liq.get("usd") or 0)`) turns that into a plain `0.0`, indistinguishable from a genuinely empty pool, and `momentum_entry.py`'s hard liquidity floor then rejects it as `insufficient_liquidity` regardless of real volume. Initial theory (native-ETH-quoted Uniswap v4 pools, ~97% of B20 pools affected) was WRONG -- a dedicated confirmation workflow found SOAF is actually WETH-quoted (contradicting the theory's own premise), and cross-checked 14 real B20 pools live: 13/14 (93%) have the field populated fine. Real pattern: an indexing-freshness artifact on VERY recently created pools (hours old), not specific to B20 or to any quote-token type -- confirmed by the one other affected pool in the sample also being same-day-created, while every pool with liquidity present was weeks old.
Solution : scope narrowed to match the confirmed cause (still worth fixing -- the downstream bug is real regardless of the theory behind it). New `PairSnapshot.liquidity_unknown: bool` field (dexscreener.py) set when `raw.get("liquidity")` isn't a dict or lacks `"usd"` -- `liquidity_usd` itself stays untouched (still a plain `float`, still `0.0` on absence) since 60+ call sites across the codebase compare it numerically and a type change to `float | None` would risk breaking all of them; this is a purely additive flag for callers that care. `momentum_entry.py`'s liquidity gate now checks this flag before rejecting: still fail-closed either way (never a fabricated liquidity figure, same doctrine as the rest of this pipeline) -- only the `hold_reason` differs (`liquidity_data_unavailable` vs. `insufficient_liquidity`) so logs/operator can tell "real scam-floor rejection" apart from "DexScreener hasn't indexed this pool's liquidity yet, retry later."
`test_dexscreener_client.py` -- 2 new tests (`_parse_pair` flags `liquidity_unknown=True` only when the key/subkey is genuinely absent, never for a real present `"usd": 0`). `test_momentum_entry.py` -- 2 new tests (dedicated `hold_reason` when `liquidity_unknown=True`; confirms the fix is additive -- a genuinely-zero-liquidity rejection keeps its original reason unchanged). Not yet deployed.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : _execute_trigger never acquired _run_cycle_lock -- real race vs. heartbeat, plus a documented cross-process gap at deploy time (Item #31/backlog)
Date : 2026.08.02 / Problem : a dedicated audit (triggered by a validation-workflow side-finding earlier the same day) confirmed `limit_orders.py::process_active_orders`/`_execute_trigger` (called only from `momentum_websocket.py`'s 30s drain loop) is the ONE capital-allocating entrypoint in this codebase that never acquires `paper_trader._run_cycle_lock` -- unlike `run_paper_cycle`, `run_daily_trade_floor_cycle`, and `_drain_multi_pocket`, all of which share it precisely to prevent two writers reading capital/position-count before either commits. `_execute_trigger`'s own check-then-act sequence (`has_open` -> per-pocket cap -> `evaluate_portfolio_risk` -> `compute_entry_alloc` -> `open_position`) has an `await` between every step and no DB-level guard either (no `UNIQUE` constraint, no explicit transaction -- each function opens its own fresh auto-commit `aiosqlite` connection). Confirmed by an independent follow-up workflow, including a second, LARGER gap found in the same audit: at every blue-green deploy, the new container's background loops (`aria_heartbeat`, `momentum_websocket_listener`) start unconditionally BEFORE the health-check passes and the old container isn't torn down until well after (health-check + rename + nginx reload + traffic verification, `vanguard/deploy.sh` steps 5->171) -- so two full processes genuinely run concurrently against the same bind-mounted SQLite file for that whole window (>=30s, typically more), and `_run_cycle_lock` (in-process) offers zero protection there even for the paths it DOES cover. No empirical proof of an already-materialized incident found in `aria.db` (all "duplicate" position groups checked were legitimate sequential re-entries, hours/days apart) -- real risk, not yet a confirmed loss.
Solution : `_execute_trigger`'s call site in `process_active_orders` now wrapped in `async with paper_trader._run_cycle_lock:` -- scoped tightly to the trigger call alone (never the whole `process_active_orders` loop, so a `pending`->`watching` transition or a `cancel`/`expire` decision, none of which touch capital, is never held up behind a possibly-long heartbeat cycle). `_execute_trigger` already re-validates price freshness internally, so the added wait-for-lock latency never risks a stale-price buy. Verified no deadlock risk: `_execute_trigger` never calls `run_paper_cycle`/`run_daily_trade_floor_cycle`/`_drain_multi_pocket` (the only other 3 acquirers of this non-reentrant `asyncio.Lock`) internally. **Deliberately NOT fixed here, disclosed as a known limitation**: the cross-process deploy-window gap -- an in-process lock structurally can't close it; would need a DB-level guard (unique constraint + explicit transaction around check+insert), a separate, larger chantier than this one.
`test_limit_orders.py` -- 1 new test (`test_execute_trigger_waits_on_run_cycle_lock_held_elsewhere`): holds the lock manually (simulating a concurrent heartbeat cycle), launches `process_active_orders` as a background task, confirms the position is genuinely NOT yet open while the lock is held, then confirms it completes correctly once released -- proves real blocking behavior, not just that the code runs without error. Not yet deployed.

------------------------------------------------------------

[CODE] Subject  : Anti-chasing SHADOW filter -- distance to the recent low, never blocking (Item #65)
Date : 2026.08.03 / Problem : a real incident (`scalping_v3` "Fren Pet", `paper_position` id=102) bought 8.5% above the low of the last 14 candles -- the entire creux->pic move happened INSIDE a single 15min candle, so the %K confirmation could only fire after the bounce was already spent (`high_water_price == entry_price`, the position never went up again, closed -4.1%). Recalculated the %K minute-by-minute on the real pool's 1min candles: the signal confirmed exactly at the candle containing the +4.66% bond, not before, at ANY tested granularity (1/5/15/30min) -- confirms this is not a timeframe problem, a %K-style confirmation can only fire after a real move has already happened, by construction. Backtested a distance-to-stop cutoff on all 75 closed scalping trades (v1/v3/v6): a 5% threshold on `scalping_v3`'s own distance (the only variant whose stop is a real candle low, `previous_low*(1-0.005)`) would have avoided its 3 worst losses (-17.3% combined) while keeping its one winner -- but the SAME threshold on `invalidation_price` for v1/v6 (whose stops are ATR/Fibonacci-based, NOT anchored on a recent low) would have sacrificed +28.7% of real gains (DRV +17.22%, AUKI +7.17%) by conflating token volatility with chasing.
Solution : a 2-agent adversarial workflow review of the first draft plan (which proposed committing to a single 5% threshold at one execution point) found the threshold was never actually validated on the metric being shipped -- the backtest used `invalidation_price` (previous CANDLE low) as a proxy, but the real metric is `recent_low` over a 14-25 candle WINDOW; since `min(N candles) <= min(1 candle)` always, the real distance reads >= the backtested one, and the sole surviving winner (distance 4.75%, just under 5%) would likely flip over the threshold once measured correctly. The review also found the plan covered only ONE of the two real execution paths (`paper_trader._open_new_entries_for_wallet`), missing the statistically dominant one (`limit_orders.check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` -- most watching orders trigger through it, not a direct buy). Amended plan, shipped in SHADOW MODE ONLY (never blocks a trade): new `chasing_filter_shadow.py` module, `recent_low_from_candles()` (min(low) over N candles already in hand -- zero network cost, same doctrine as `entry_atr_pct`/Item #253) computed with a window MATCHING each mechanism's own lookback rather than one uniform N (14 for V3/V4 Stochastique, 20 for V1/V2/V5 Bollinger/VWAP, 25 for the legacy RSI-divergence/standard momentum golden-pocket path) -- exposed as `recent_low`/`recent_low_window` on every BUY signal and both watch-candidate builders (`scalping_variants._buy_result`, `momentum_entry.py`'s direct-BUY branch and its two watch-candidate builders). `record_check()` logs the distance and whether EACH of 4 candidate thresholds (3/5/7/10%) would have rejected the trade, at BOTH execution points: `paper_trader._open_new_entries_for_wallet` (right before `open_position`, on the same fresh price `_fresh_rr` already re-fetched -- zero extra cost) and `limit_orders.check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` (recomputed fresh on the same re-fetched candles the divergence re-check just ran on, `.setdefault`-style -- never erases a prior watch-candidate value with `None`, same pattern as Item #253's own `entry_atr_pct` recompute). Best-effort throughout: a logging failure never blocks or delays a real trade. Explicitly scoped to `strategy="momentum"` (scalping/swing/megacap) only -- VC's thesis isn't structured around a bounce from a technical low, so "chasing" has no equivalent meaning there; extending this filter to VC is a separate, unstarted question.
Also built (separate, operator request): `scripts/consult-gemini.sh` -- a manual, synchronous, on-demand second opinion via Gemini 3.1 Pro/OpenRouter, reusing the exact verified pattern (model/auth/headers) as the existing automatic post-push `devils-advocate-review.sh` hook, but invoked only when explicitly asked mid-conversation, never automatically.
Exit criteria for this shadow phase (not yet met, nothing to decide on yet): >= 30 logged BUY signals with `recent_low` computed, >= 10 in the "would have been rejected" bucket for each threshold tested, PnL gap between "kept" and "rejected" >= ~5 points on at least 2 of the 3 pockets (scalping/swing/megacap) before any threshold becomes real (blocking). Hard calendar backstop at 14 days (2 weekly resets) to force a review even if the sample is slow to build, regardless of outcome.
`test_chasing_filter_shadow.py` -- 7 new tests (recent_low math incl. window-boundary proof, distance/threshold computation, missing-data leaves `None` never fabricated, never-raises-on-write-failure, empty-contract no-op). `test_scalping_variants.py` -- 5 new assertions (one per variant, correct window wired). `test_momentum_entry.py` -- 4 new tests (BUY exposes/HOLD has none, both watch-candidates, dedicated non-flat >=25-candle fixture -- same "flat fixture proves nothing" doctrine as Item #253's own tests). `test_limit_orders.py` -- 3 new tests (fresh recompute on trigger, `.setdefault` preservation when unavailable, shadow-log call verified with the right `source`/`execution_price`). `test_paper_trader.py` -- 1 new test (shadow-log call verified at the direct-buy execution point, on the FRESH price, not the stale signal one). Full monorepo suite green, `test_coherence.py` green. Not yet deployed (paper trading currently paused via the new `/off` runtime toggle, see the entry below, while these changes are reviewed).

[CODE] Subject  : Runtime paper-trading pause -- `/off`/`/on` on Telegram, instant, no redeploy (Item #64)
Date : 2026.08.03 / Problem : during a live debugging session, GeckoTerminal was being hit both by manual investigation calls AND by every pocket's continuous scanning (scalping/swing/vc/megacap heartbeat cycles + the 30s websocket drain) -- `ARIA_PAPER_TRADING_ENABLED` is an environment variable, read once at container start, so pausing it required a full rebuild/redeploy (too slow for "stop scanning for the next 10 minutes").
Solution : new `paper_pause.py` module, structurally separate from `outgoing_pause`/`custody_pause` (both real-money-only, per Item #62's own split -- paper trading must never be blockable by either) -- fails OPEN on a corrupted state (opposite of `custody_pause`'s fail-closed doctrine: this flag guards zero capital, so "unknown" defaulting to "keep running" is strictly safer than freezing the whole diagnostic test over a corrupted debug toggle). Wired into every paper-trading heartbeat task gate (`paper_trade_cycle`, `momentum_discovery_cycle`, `paper_weekly_review_cycle`, `daily_trade_floor_cycle`, `goplus_watchlist_cycle`) and the websocket drain's own `_paper_trading_enabled()`. Two new owner-only Telegram commands, `/off`/`/on`, structurally distinct from `/stop`/`/resume` (which stay scoped to `outgoing_pause`/`custody_pause`, real-money-only -- this never touches them). Also fixed a small log-clarity issue found right after deploying this: the drain's "skipped" log line always named `ARIA_PAPER_TRADING_ENABLED` even when `/off` was the real cause -- now distinguishes the two sources explicitly.
`test_paper_pause.py` -- 6 new tests (default/pause/resume, own JSON state file distinct from the other two flags', fail-open on corruption, never shares state with `outgoing_pause`/`custody_pause`, the websocket drain and heartbeat task gates both honor it). Deployed (commit `acdacf48`), verified live via real container logs (drain skip confirmed, zero GeckoTerminal calls since `/off`).

[CODE] Subject : Scalping stop ATR + signal-on-unclosed-candle -- two real bugs found and fixed, not yet deployed
Date : 2026.08.03 / Problem : 9-pocket diagnostic (blind LLM comparison, docs/HANDOFF_LLM.md) plus operator's own market intuition. Two pistes verified against the real code and real prod data, not just LLM assertion: (1) `_effective_trail_pct`'s shared bounds (`MIN_ATR_TRAIL_PCT=0.05`/`MAX_ATR_TRAIL_PCT=0.40`) were designed 19/07 for the standard/swing pipeline, before scalping existed (26/07) -- the 7 real scalping losses moved 1.7%-3.6%, entirely below the 5% floor, so the ATR trailing stop never triggered once; every one closed via the stagnation timeout instead. (2) All 5 scalping variants confirmed their signal on `candles[-1]`, very likely the still-forming candle (standard behavior of every real-time OHLCV provider in the cascade) -- quantified on the 12 real V3 trades: the confirming Stochastic %K ranges 21-100 (the absolute top of the range), correlating with the worst losses (-4.3% to -6.7%).
Solution : (1) scalping-dedicated bounds added (`MIN_ATR_TRAIL_PCT_SCALPING=0.015`, `MAX_ATR_TRAIL_PCT_SCALPING=0.10`, same 2.5x multiplier, calibrated on only 7 trades -- explicitly not final) -- `_effective_trail_pct`/`_compute_active_stop` gained a `mode` kwarg, all 5 call sites in `paper_trader.py` updated to pass `sig.get("mode")`/`pos.get("mode")`/`p.get("mode")`. (2) `_gates_and_candles_uncached` (scalping_variants.py) now trims `candles[:-1]` right after fetch, checked against `_MIN_CANDLES_FOR_SIGNAL` AFTER trimming (not before) -- centralized in one place (same doctrine as align_score/entry_atr_pct in `_buy_result`), never a per-variant index shift. Consulted Claude Fable 5 for a second opinion on this exact architectural choice (troncature centralisee vs decalage d'indices) before committing -- validated the approach, flagged 5 points to verify, all confirmed clean (see CLAUDE.md's "Etat actif" section for the full list) except one accepted trade-off: `recent_low_from_candles` (anti-chasing shadow filter, informational-only) now reads the trimmed series too, measuring the last CONFIRMED low rather than the true intra-candle low -- documented explicitly rather than left as a silent side effect (threading a second untrimmed candle list through the whole chain wasn't worth it for a log-only signal).
**Known gap, NOT addressed by this fix**: entries still confirm structurally AFTER a reversal has already started (all 5 variants are mean-reversion signals by design) -- this fix reduces the risk of buying an unconfirmed intra-candle spike, but does not make entries earlier. Operator's own idea for that (detect on 15-30min, refine exact entry timing on 1-5min candles, same pattern as `limit_orders.check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` already does for swing at the 15-30min scale) is banked, not built -- two design variants pending a decision (reuse the existing watching-order price-target mechanism vs. a new fine-grained divergence-watch signal).
`paper_trader.py` (new `ATR_TRAIL_MULTIPLIER_SCALPING`/`MIN_ATR_TRAIL_PCT_SCALPING`/`MAX_ATR_TRAIL_PCT_SCALPING`, `_effective_trail_pct`/`_compute_active_stop` `mode` kwarg) / `skills/scalping_variants.py` (`_gates_and_candles_uncached` trim + `recent_low_from_candles` trade-off comment) -- 9 new tests (`test_paper_trader.py`: 3 scalping-mode `_effective_trail_pct` branches + floor-stricter-than-standard + non-scalping-string-keeps-standard-bounds; `test_scalping_variants.py`: trim-proof test, exactly-45-before-trim-still-rejected, V3 structural stop test adjusted for the new trim), 872 targeted tests green (`test_scalping_variants.py`/`test_paper_trader.py`/`test_paper_trader_risk.py`/`test_coherence.py`/`test_momentum_entry.py`). Committed (`b00db0d8` -- inadvertently bundled with an unrelated consult-gemini.sh fix in the same commit, content correct, message doesn't mention this one). Not yet deployed to the VPS.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : megacap pocket structurally never bought anything -- two independent guardrail bugs found live (operator: "regarde kaito")
Date : 2026.08.03 / Problem : operator flagged the megacap pocket as buggy after seeing a KAITO limit order created and cancelled ("re-vérification échouée") every ~5 minutes in Telegram. Investigation found TWO separate, compounding bugs, both confirmed against real prod data before fixing (never assumed): (1) `_reanalyze_holder_concentration`'s >=80% top-10-EOA guardrail (`momentum_entry._check_holder_concentration`, designed to catch memecoin insiders) structurally fails on `fixed_watchlist.py`'s hand-curated established-token watchlist -- real Blockscout data on KAITO showed its top 2 EOA holders alone hold ~55% of supply (CEX/treasury wallets, not insiders; the "verified contract" exemption never covers a plain EOA), so every order eventually got cancelled `reanalysis_failed`. 16+ create/cancel cycles/day on KAITO alone, zero real trigger. (2) When the operator asked to check whether other megacap tokens showed the same pattern ("jai limpression que dautre token le font aussi"), a broader query found LINK/WETH/cbBTC/cbETH churning every ~15 MINUTES via a DIFFERENT cancel reason (`rsi_horizon_expired`) -- and the same pattern across `swing` too (VVV 13x, MAGIC 12x, MORPHO 12x, dozens more), not megacap-specific at all. Root cause: `check_rsi_divergence_watching_order`'s expiry check compares fine-grained watch-phase candles (15-30min, operator decision 31/07) against `last_candle_ts`, which was set from the ORIGINAL golden-pocket signal's coarse standard-mode candles (day/4h/1h). Verified live on LINK: a single 30min-candle refetch already had 55 candles past a `last_candle_ts` set from a daily close -- 2.75x past the 20-candle horizon on the very first watch check, regardless of real elapsed time. This alone was likely blocking every `rsi_divergence_pending` limit-order trigger fleet-wide since the 31/07 fine-grained-watch decision, independent of bug (1).
Solution : (1) `_reanalyze_holder_concentration` now returns `True` (safe) unconditionally when `order["wallet"] == "megacap"` -- every other guardrail (honeypot, blacklist, liquidity floor) stays fully enforced, only this specific concentration heuristic is waived for the hand-vetted watchlist. Same waiver threaded through the direct-buy path (`evaluate_momentum_entry(waive_holder_concentration=True)`, forwarded from `paper_trader._default_momentum_analyzer`, set only on the megacap analyzer call). (2) `check_rsi_divergence_watching_order` now realigns `last_candle_ts` to the fine-grained resolution's own last candle ONCE per order (`sig["watch_candle_ts_aligned"]` flag, persisted via new `limit_orders._persist_signal_json`) before ever comparing counts -- self-healing for every order already stuck in the loop (a missing flag on an old order just means "not yet aligned"), no DB migration needed. Also fixed in passing: a combined-equity display bug found while investigating (position with an unavailable price silently vanished from the tracked list instead of being shown/marked-to-last-known) -- see `docs/HANDOFF_PAPER_TRADING.md`.
`test_limit_orders.py` (2 new/updated: realignment-on-first-check, existing RSI-watch fixtures updated to represent the post-realignment steady state) / `test_paper_trader.py` (14 existing fakes updated for the new `waive_holder_concentration` kwarg, no new behavior asserted there). Full suite green (9035 passed / 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green — `limit_orders.py`/`momentum_entry.py`/`paper_trader.py` (`e3c05335`). Deployed same session (`ebb9414e6fa7`, blue-green health-check confirmed sane through nginx).

[DEPLOYE] Subject : scalping_v1/v2/v4/v5 sat at zero trades -- entry thresholds too rare by construction, not a bug, relaxed in two passes
Date : 2026.08.03->04 / Problem : same-session follow-up to the megacap entry above -- operator asked to check how the 9 pockets were trading after the deploy, surfacing that scalping_v2/v4/v5 (`skills/scalping_variants.py`) had opened ZERO positions since creation (01/08) while v1/v3/v6 traded normally. Confirmed live this was NOT the shared-candidate-budget bug already fixed 08/01 (`momentum_scan_log` showed 2800-4200 scans/variant, all 5 actively evaluating candidates through to the current cycle) -- the entry thresholds themselves were simply too statistically rare. Live-measured on 13 currently-scanned candidates (~780 fine-candle data points, real GeckoTerminal/scalping-mode OHLCV, not simulated): V1's `%B<=0.0` hits 6.5% of the time and V3's `%K<=15` hits 16.8% (both DID trade) -- V2/V5's `Z-score<=-2.5` hits only 1.4%, and V4's combo (`%B<=0.0` AND `%K<=15` on the SAME candle) an estimated ~1.1% intersection -- neither ever traded.
Solution (pass 1, `2484c907`) : operator decision, relax both (V4 initially considered "rare by design, leave it" per its own docstring -- "double confirmation, taux de réussite visé maximal" -- but operator chose to widen it too). V2/V5: `_V2_OVERSOLD_ZSCORE` -2.5 -> -2.0 (~3.8x more frequent per the same live measurement, still stricter than V1/V3). V4: two NEW dedicated constants (`_V4_BOLLINGER_THRESHOLD=0.1`, `_V4_STOCHASTIC_K_THRESHOLD=20.0`) replacing its previous reuse of V1's/V3's own thresholds -- deliberately separate so widening V4's combo never silently loosens V1 or V3, which already trade fine at their original values. Estimated ~2.3x intersection for V4 post-change. Deployed same session (`ebb9414e6fa7`).
Pass 2 (`6775fd02`, 04/08, operator pre-authorized "n'attends pas ma confirmation assoupli un peut et redeploi") : checked ~3h20 after pass 1's deploy -- V3/V4 both traded in that window, V1/V2/V5 STILL sat at zero. Fresh live measurement (15 currently-scanned candidates, ~224-262 data points, some GeckoTerminal/Mobula rate-limiting reduced the sample vs. pass 1): V1's `%B<=0.0` crossed only 5.7% -> relaxed to 0.1 (9.2%, ~1.6x). V2/V5's `Z-score<=-2.0` crossed only 3.6% -> relaxed to -1.75 (7.6%, ~2.1x) -- still the strictest of the 5 variants. Existing tests needed NO changes this pass -- their fixture values already had enough margin at the new thresholds (verified by running the suite, not assumed). Deployed same session.
`scalping_variants.py` / `test_scalping_variants.py` -- pass 1: 4 existing tests updated (series values shifted to stay consistent with the new thresholds, same pass/fail semantics, no new behavior class introduced). Full suite green (9036 passed / 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green — `scalping_variants.py` (`2484c907`, deployed `ebb9414e6fa7`). Pass 2: `scalping_variants.py` only, no test changes needed. Full suite green (9035 passed / 17 skipped -- 1 unrelated flaky test, `test_x402_budget.py`'s concurrent-SQLite-lock test, confirmed flaky by re-running in isolation 3x), `test_coherence.py` green — `scalping_variants.py` (`6775fd02`).

[CODE] Subject : each scalping pocket independently re-fetched candles for the same candidate -- no cache, unlike security/pair-snapshot
Date : 2026.08.04 / Problem : operator idea while planning a scalping_v7 pocket ("un seul appel d'analyse qui distribue toutes les donnée nécéssaire par time frame ?") -- scalping_v1..v6 all share the same 10-candidate slice each cycle (`build_scalping_pocket_entries`) and the same `mode="scalping"` resolution, yet each pocket's own analyzer independently called `_fetch_candles` (GeckoTerminal) for the SAME (pool, mode), unlike the sibling `_security_cache`/`_pair_snapshot_cache` caches already in place a few lines above it in the same file.
Solution : new `_candles_cache` (60s TTL, same doctrine as its two siblings), scoped by `(chain, pool, mode, skip_daily)` -- never by contract alone, the candle shape genuinely differs by mode. Bypassed entirely when `min_useful_candles`/`gecko_client` are explicitly passed (smart_money.py's wallet-scoring path, Item #186 -- a distinct request, never shared across pockets). Never caches an empty/rejected result -- a transient failure must stay retryable, not get frozen as "unavailable" for the next pocket's own attempt.
`momentum_entry.py` -- 5 new tests (`test_momentum_entry.py`: cache hit skips the network call, scoped by mode, TTL expiry, bypass on `min_useful_candles`, never caches empty). 2 existing provider-cooldown tests updated (cleared the new cache each loop iteration -- they test REAL repeat calls, orthogonal to this cache). Full suite green (9042+ passed), `test_coherence.py` green — `momentum_entry.py`.
