[DEPLOYE] Subject : candles + signal-levels chart screenshot on limit-order placement -- pilot scoped to scalping_v6/v7
Date : 2026.08.04 / Problem : operator request ("c'est possible davoir un screenshot du graphique pour chaque poche quand elle etablie un ordre limite avec les signaux detecter ?") -- the "ORDRE LIMITE POSÉ" Telegram alert is text-only, no visual of the actual golden-pocket/RSI-divergence setup that triggered it. Scope explicitly narrowed by the operator (AskUserQuestion) to a pilot pocket pair rather than all 9 pockets at once.
Solution : new `limit_order_chart.py` (`PILOT_WALLETS = ("scalping_v6", "scalping_v7")` -- only these two are wired, extend explicitly once validated, never silently). Reuses the exact pool already resolved by the scan that placed the order (`sig["pool_address"]`, new field added to `evaluate_momentum_entry`'s return dict) to refetch candles via the already-public `momentum_entry.fetch_candles` alias -- almost always a cache hit against the 60s candles cache added earlier this same session, never a fresh network call in the common case. Renders with `chart_render.render_scenario_png` (real candlesticks, DexScreener look, already existed for thesis_journal/marketing_video/vc_analysis) at the order's entry/invalidation/target levels; new optional `horizon_label` param on that function overrides the "SIMULATION N sem." wording (a scalping setup plays out in hours, not weeks -- the old default would have been a fabricated-sounding unit for this caller specifically, `None` default keeps every existing caller byte-for-byte unchanged). Sent via the already-existing `telegram_bot.send_photo` as a complement to (never a replacement of) the existing text alert. Entirely best-effort: wrapped in its own try/except in `paper_trader.py` (never allowed to affect the outer except reserved for order-placement failures), and `maybe_send_order_chart` itself never raises (missing pool_address/no candles/Telegram down all degrade to a silent no-op).
`momentum_entry.py`/`chart_render.py`/`paper_trader.py`/new `limit_order_chart.py` -- new `test_limit_order_chart.py` (6 tests: pilot-scope gate, missing pool_address, empty candles, real send + temp-file cleanup, never raises on fetch/send failure) + 3 new `test_chart_render.py` tests (`horizon_label` None keeps default wording, override actually changes output). Full suite green (9077 passed / 17 skipped -- 1 unrelated flaky test, `test_x402_budget.py`'s concurrent-SQLite-lock test, same one already documented above, confirmed flaky again by re-running in isolation 3x), `test_coherence.py` green. Deployed (`e1cc826658d7`), blue-green health-check + real nginx traffic both confirmed the commit before the old container was removed.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : pool_address was dead on the actual scalping limit-order watch path -- zero screenshots ever sent despite the pilot above being live
Date : 2026.08.04 / Problem : real bug found live minutes after deploying the chart pilot (operator: "je vois pas de screenshot", 3 real scalping_v7 orders shown, none had a photo). `pool_address` had only been added to `evaluate_momentum_entry`'s FINAL `return {...}` statement -- but 100% of scalping limit orders resolve through an EARLIER `return hold` (the RSI-divergence/golden-pocket watch branch, ~530 lines above the final return, already documented in this same file as "the ONE actually exercised by scalping"), which never reaches that final block. Confirmed live in the DB: every single `scalping_v7` order created even AFTER the pilot's own deploy had `pool_address=None` in its `signal_json`. `limit_order_chart.maybe_send_order_chart`'s own `if not pool_address: return` degraded this silently to a no-op -- no exception, no log, nothing to notice short of the operator explicitly checking for the photo.
Solution : `pool_address` added to the `hold` dict at its actual construction point (the early return, not the unreachable final one). New dedicated test (`test_evaluate_momentum_entry_hold_carries_pool_address_for_limit_order_watch`) asserts on the OUTER result (what `paper_trader.py` actually reads as `sig`), not just the inner `watch`/`limit_order_candidate` sub-dict -- the exact distinction that let this bug hide. Full suite green (745 passed on the touched files), deployed same session (`b0d997095f3c`) minutes after the report -- justified as a direct deploy (bug polluting a capability already live in prod), not batched.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : RSI-divergence watch expiry inflated up to 235h on thin-liquidity scalping tokens
Date : 2026.08.04 / Problem : operator report ("check si toutes les poches ont cette erreur") after spotting a "~480h expiry" log line -- swing/vc/megacap's 480h is correct by design (daily candles), but scalping_v6/v7 (meant to resolve in ~5h) showed the same anomaly on ~1/3 of their watches (20h-235h observed live). Root cause: `watch_expiry_hours` was derived from `candles[-1].ts - candles[-2].ts` (last-pair-only) -- on a thin/low-volume token, that gap can reflect several nominal candle slots with zero trades (no candle emitted for a quiet period), not the provider's real granularity. Confirmed empirically on real prod data: AIXBT/scalping_v7 and MAMO/scalping_v6, genuinely 15min-labeled candles with a 5-12h trailing silence, inflating the derived expiry accordingly.
Solution : `_median_candle_interval_seconds()` (median of ALL consecutive gaps, robust to a handful of quiet slots) replaces the last-pair-only read for the expiry calc, plus a new `RSI_WATCH_MAX_EXPIRY_HOURS_SCALPING=12.0` hard ceiling (the generic 1h-720h clamp was calibrated for swing's daily candles, does nothing for scalping). New continuity gate in `evaluate_momentum_entry` (scalping mode only, before any indicator is computed): a candidate whose most recent candle gap exceeds 4x its own median cadence is rejected (`HOLD`, `hold_reason="scalping_candle_gap_too_wide"`) -- a token that thin isn't a real scalping candidate, and RSI/ATR computed on an irregular series are corrupted, not just the expiry. Fix designed with a Claude Fable 5 second opinion (operator-relayed) on top of the initially-proposed simple clamp. One-shot sweep executed same session on the live DB: 91 of 224 open watches (40.6%) were scalping orders with the old inflated expiry -- all cancelled (`limit_orders.mark_cancelled`, never reclamped -- a setup stale for days shouldn't be silently revived), verified 0 remaining afterward (60 scalping watches left open, all <= 12h).
`momentum_entry.py`/`test_momentum_entry.py` (`9aa917a6`), deployed same session, sweep run directly against prod via `docker exec`. Full suite green (9114 passed, 17 skipped), `test_coherence.py` green.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : wstETH (reference asset, LST) sourced as a momentum candidate -- 4 orders in 3 days
Date : 2026.08.04 / Problem : real gap documented since the 24/07 5-agent audit ("LST case: stETH<->wstETH... out of scope") finally recurred live -- operator screenshot of a swing wstETH limit order, skeptical ("le graphique est deja reparti a la hausse depuis longtemps"). Claude Fable 5 (operator-relayed) diagnosed the real issue: wstETH's ratio against WETH is near-deterministic (staking yield only, ~3-4%/year, doesn't oscillate) -- its USD chart is really just ETH's chart, so any "golden pocket"/RSI divergence on it is an ETH macro read dressed up as a Base momentum setup, no token-specific edge. [CODE] Subject : DexPaprika 401 (invalid configured key) had no fallback -- silently degraded the whole tier
Date : 2026.08.05 / Problem : operator noticed API saturation while backtesting; investigation traced a chunk of it to DexPaprika returning HTTP 401 on every call. Verified against DexPaprika's own docs + a direct curl test (with the configured `DEXPAPRIKA_API_KEY` -> 401 "api key verification has failed"; same call with no key at all -> 200 OK) -- this tier requires no auth, the key had simply been revoked/invalidated since it was added 04/08. `_get_json` had retry logic for 429/5xx/timeout but nothing for 401 -- same anti-pattern already fixed for Blockscout on 07/20 (an invalid config silently breaking a tier that works fine keyless).
Solution : on a 401 with a key present, `_get_json` now retries the SAME request once with no Authorization header, and a new module-level `_key_marked_invalid` flag makes `_auth_headers()` skip the key for the rest of the process (never resend a known-bad key). Operator separately removed the invalid `DEXPAPRIKA_API_KEY` from `vanguard/backend/.env` -- the code fix is a safety net for any future bad/rotated key, not a substitute for that.
`dexpaprika.py`/`test_dexpaprika_client.py` (2 new tests), targeted suite green (19 passed). Committed, not yet deployed.

[CODE] Subject : scalping_v8 -- wick-confirmed reversal pocket (operator carte blanche) + wick shadow filter on v6/v7
Date : 2026.08.05 / Problem : operator carte blanche ("cree une poche v8 avec tes convictions selon les donnees recoltees... fait en sorte qu'il trade beaucoup au debut en etant souple") after the day's empirical session: 58 real closed trades reconstructed candle-by-candle showed signal-candle lower-wick ratio >= 0.3 as the ONLY entry discriminator surviving confound checks (60% WR vs 25.6%, Fisher p=0.026, consistent across pockets AND periods), while tight fixed SL/TP grids (12/12 combos negative EV), RVOL gating, volatility squeeze, regime/weekday/align all failed empirically. "Timeout stagnation" closed 45 real trades at 0% WR; 34% of trades never traded above entry; median max gain +0.47%.
Solution : new `evaluate_v8_wick_reversal` (scalping_variants.py, registered in VARIANT_ANALYZERS -> pocket auto-created lazily at $1M like v1-v5): entry = hammer wick >= 0.30 (`indicators.hammer_wick_ratio`, new) on the last CLOSED candle + fresh setup (RSI divergence pivot <= 5 candles old, or in BOOTSTRAP mode a fresh <= 5-candle local trough with divergence optional-and-traced -- the free 8.1 experiment: wick-only vs wick+divergence sub-populations separable by reason/rr) + anti-chase (live price <= +2% over signal close) + ATR stop 1.5x; NO fixed TP (target=None, V5 pattern, sizing_rr 2.0 with divergence / 1.5 without); shorter stagnation timeout 1.5h via new per-wallet seam `_SCALPING_STAGNATION_OVERRIDES_BY_WALLET` (paper_trader.py, v1-v7 byte-for-byte unchanged). New `wick_filter_shadow.py` (chasing_filter_shadow pattern) logs the same wick ratio on every v6/v7 limit-order trigger WITHOUT blocking -- v6 (logged, ungated) vs v8 (gated) = natural forward A/B before ever gating existing pockets. `entry_signals.RsiDivergenceDetail` gains additive `bars_since_recent_pivot`. VPS-side: /opt/aria-data/v8-watch/run.sh cron every 2h (accelerated observation, nominal 6h to restore later) -- append-only v8-log.md for sessions + Telegram alert only if cumulative v8 PnL < -10k$ (one-shot flag).
scalping_variants.py/paper_trader.py/limit_orders.py/entry_signals.py/indicators.py/new wick_filter_shadow.py + tests (13 new across 5 files, 1 registry test updated -- deliberate invariant change, same commit).

[CODE] Subject : scalping_v8 -- entire pocket paused, divergence tier shares bootstrap's failure signature
Date : 2026.08.07 / Problem : the 07/08 ~12h30 decision (this file, above) killed bootstrap (34 trades/0 wins) but left the divergence tier running on "0/6, too small a sample to judge" -- it has since grown to 9 real closed trades, still 0 wins, and shares the EXACT signature already diagnosed for bootstrap: avg peak gain vs entry -1.6%, n_touched_positive=0/9 (not one trade ever traded a single tick above its own entry price before closing). Combined both tiers: 0 winners in 43 real closed trades. The one tier with an actual backtest basis (60% WR, Fisher p=0.026) is failing forward exactly like the one that never had any -- not a sample-size question anymore.
Solution : new `_V8_ENTRY_PAUSED = True` flag, checked right after the combo-signal shadow log (which keeps running -- only the buy path is cut) -- `evaluate_v8_wick_reversal` now returns a deterministic HOLD (`v8_entry_paused`) regardless of signal quality. Diagnosis: not exit-tuning (same conclusion already reached for bootstrap) -- it's the entry-timing bug the anti-chase/giveback gates (both already active, both ATR-scaled) were meant to close and evidently don't: `entry` is still a LIVE price sampled at evaluation time, un-anchored to `signal_close`. Real fix banked, not built here: simulate a LIMIT order anchored to signal_close +/- k*ATR (Devil's Advocate report 2db20159, cited in the giveback-gate's own comment) in SHADOW mode first (combo_signal_shadow.py/wick_filter_shadow.py pattern) before ever routing a real trade through it -- built the same session, see the next entry below (operator: "il n'y a pas de session dedie a v8, c'est ton agent, tu le code en temps reel"). `scalping_variants.py` (+29 lines, mostly the diagnosis comment) -- new dedicated test (`test_v8_entry_paused_blocks_every_signal`) locks the pause as a hard stop, not a bypassable gate; all 11 pre-existing BUY/HOLD-gate tests updated (autouse fixture opts back into `_V8_ENTRY_PAUSED=False` so the underlying gate logic -- still fully correct, worth locking in even while paused -- keeps being exercised). Full aria-core suite green before deploy.

[CODE] Subject : scalping_v8 -- limit-order shadow, forward-validates the real fix for the entry-timing bug
Date : 2026.08.07 / Problem : the pause above (same day) stops the bleed but doesn't fix anything -- v8's real defect (LIVE price entry, un-anchored to the signal candle) needs a replacement mechanism validated on forward data BEFORE ever routing a real trade through it (doctrine already established for this pocket: never retune in place without live evidence).
Solution : new `v8_limit_shadow.py` -- unlike combo_signal_shadow.py/wick_filter_shadow.py (stateless, one log line per observation), this is a STATEFUL shadow: a confirmed wick+divergence signal opens a PENDING simulated limit order anchored to `signal_close` (recorded from `evaluate_v8_wick_reversal` right after wick+ATR are validated, BEFORE the pause check -- so pausing v8 doesn't also starve the data needed to ever safely re-open it). `process_shadows` (throttled to once/5min, injected `price_atr_fn` reuses `scalping_variants._gates_and_candles`'s own cache -- zero extra network calls) fills a pending order once live price returns to `signal_close` (window 3h, else marked "never filled" -- itself a useful data point) and manages a filled one with the EXACT same exit mechanics as real v8 (imports `paper_trader._advance_high_water`/`_compute_active_stop` directly rather than re-deriving them -- ATR trailing stop, 1.5h stagnation timeout, 2h absolute hold cap). Purely observational: separate table, touches nothing else in the pipeline, best-effort throughout. 12 new tests (`test_v8_limit_shadow.py`: record/dedup/fill/expire/stop-close/stagnation-vs-max-hold distinction/throttle/summary) + 1 integration test locking that a real signal still feeds the shadow while paused. Full aria-core suite green (9471 passed) before deploy. Next: let it accumulate real forward data (days, not hours) before judging whether to re-open v8 on this entry mechanic -- accelerated check-in cadence via ScheduleWakeup while the mechanism itself is unproven, never a parameter retune before there's enough data to justify one.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : v8 limit-order shadow -- process_shadows was gated behind the same rare signal it exists to unblock
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : caught live during the accelerated observation cadence (ScheduleWakeup check-ins every 1h post-deploy) -- the first real shadow (TOSHI, opened 00:46 UTC) sat frozen in "pending" across two consecutive checks (01:23, 02:24) despite its 3h fill window having room to resolve. Root cause: `process_shadows` (the step that fills/expires/manages every OPEN shadow, not just the one just recorded) was only called from inside the SAME `if signal_close > 0` block as `record_signal` -- itself reached only when THIS candidate's own wick+divergence signal fires. An already-open shadow for a DIFFERENT contract could only ever advance when some OTHER candidate happened to produce a fresh signal in the same cycle -- exactly as rare as the signal itself (911 evaluations/40min produced zero signals in the first observation window), so an open shadow could sit indefinitely without ever reaching a verdict.
Solution : moved `process_shadows` earlier -- right after `combo_signal_shadow.record_evaluation`, which runs on every real v8 evaluation reaching valid candles, regardless of wick/divergence outcome. `record_signal` (opens a NEW shadow) stays exactly where it was, still gated to a real signal -- only the advance step needed to run unconditionally. New regression test (`test_v8_advances_the_shadow_even_without_a_fresh_signal`) locks that `process_shadows` fires even on a pure no-signal HOLD. `scalping_variants.py` -- full aria-core suite green before deploy.

------------------------------------------------------------

[DEPLOYE] Subject : scalping_v8 -- RSI-reversal shadow (60min), forward-validates a backtested replacement for wick+divergence
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : operator request ("lance une simulation complete sur un time frame de 15 30 et 60 minutes et detecte quel outil et quel reglage aurait pu fonctionner... pour attraper en bas du graphique et revendre en haut") -- a from-scratch backtest across the same 35 unique real v8 tokens (43 real closed trades), 500 real CoinMarketCap candles each (~5-21 days depending on timeframe, empirically confirmed live, no persistent candle-storage table existed so all history was re-fetched), 52 indicator/period/threshold combos, 3 timeframes. Result: v8's real wick+divergence+ATR-trail system backtests NEGATIVE avg return on all 3 timeframes (-0.34% to -0.49%, 19-26% WR) -- matches its real 0-winner/43-trade prod history, confirms the problem is the method, not bad luck. A plain RSI oversold-exit/overbought-exit round trip on the 60-MINUTE timeframe clearly outperformed it, and the signal strengthens with longer timeframes. Operator explicitly required cross-token robustness verification before building anything (not just aggregate stats): RSI(21) 30/70 hit 16/35 tokens with ZERO losing tokens (17 trades, +7.43% avg, 100% WR); RSI(14) 25/75 hit 17/35 tokens with 14/17 winning (20 trades, +4.58% avg, 80% WR) -- both broad enough to not be 1-2-outlier artifacts, unlike RSI(7)/15m (27/35 tokens but 12 losers, more fragile). A separate confluence pass (do points where several indicators agree mark better entries?) was inconclusive -- slight winrate lift with more agreeing indicators but no clear return lift, and likely biased by high-signal-volume indicators (MFI/Fisher) dominating high-confluence clusters -- banked as a lead, not acted on.
Solution : new `v8_rsi_reversal_shadow.py` -- tracks BOTH settings (rsi14/rsi21) independently in shadow mode, same doctrine as v8_limit_shadow.py/combo_signal_shadow.py. Resamples v8's OWN already-fetched 15/30min candles up to 60min internally (`_resample_to_60m`, detects real candle spacing since mode="scalping" can silently fall back 15m->30m per candidate) -- ZERO extra network calls. Entry = RSI crosses up through the low threshold on the last closed 60min bar; exit = RSI crosses back down through the high threshold, or a 72h max-hold timeout (backtest window topped out at ~21 days of real history, a position that never resolves within 3 days is no longer comparable to what was backtested). Wired into `evaluate_v8_wick_reversal` right after the existing shadow calls (same "runs on every real evaluation reaching valid candles" doctrine). New table `v8_rsi_reversal_shadow` (contract/chain/symbol/variant/status/entry+exit price+rsi/pnl_pct). VPS-side: `/opt/aria-data/v8-watch/run.sh` (existing cron, every 30min) extended with a per-variant summary line rather than a new dedicated cron -- avoids duplicating monitoring infra. 12 new tests (`test_v8_rsi_reversal_shadow.py`: resampling factor-4/factor-2/leftover-drop/passthrough, open/close/timeout/dedup state machine, one real end-to-end pass through `record_evaluation` against the actual `rsi_series`, summary aggregation). Full aria-core suite green (9484 passed) before deploy. Next: let it accumulate real forward data before considering it as v8's real entry/exit mechanic.

Post-deploy addendum (same day, Devil's Advocate report dd72b372, one point verified and acted on): the 17-trade/100%-WR RSI21 result is a small sample, classic overfitting shape -- backlog #266 (Monte-Carlo permutation test, promoted the SAME day) had never actually been run against it before building on it. Applied here: 2000 permutations per variant, random entry index (same token, same holding length as each real trade) vs the real trades' mean return. RSI21: real mean +7.43% vs null distribution mean -0.56% (std 2.34%), real result at the 99.9th percentile, p=0.0010. RSI14: real mean +4.58% vs null -0.64% (std 2.07%), 99.3rd percentile, p=0.0070. Both well under the conventional 0.05 threshold -- the signal is not an artifact of small-sample luck, though this doesn't replace the forward-shadow validation still in progress. (The same report's claim that `signal_conditions.py` already expresses RSI crossing was checked and found imprecise -- that module has static threshold comparisons only, no cross_up/cross_down primitive yet, per its own "Alternative radicale" section; the 4-module shadow duplication critique itself stands as a legitimate backlog lead, not acted on this session.)

[DEPLOYE] Subject : VC pocket sourcing/TA -- GeckoTerminal-only with zero fallback, silently zeroed the pocket for hours
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : operator-reported "vc est encore sur une vieille version" after the VC scan was diagnosed returning 0 candidates. Root cause confirmed live (reproduced the real HTTP call, not assumed): `base_crawler.discover_top_pools` (sourcing) and `skills/acp_onchain_scan.py`'s TA block (`ohlcv_client.get_ohlcv`, used by both `token_absorber` and `vc_analysis`) each called GeckoTerminal directly with ZERO fallback -- unlike `momentum_entry.py`'s multi-source sourcing (base_crawler+Birdeye+token_profiles+token_boosts) and its 6-stage candle cascade (GeckoTerminal->CoinMarketCap->Mobula->DexPaprika->Codex.io->DexScreener synthesis->Dune) with a shared adaptive circuit breaker, both built 26/07-04/08 and never ported to the VC pocket. Prod logs (48h) showed near-continuous GeckoTerminal 429s across every call site despite the throttle being correctly architected (single shared `asyncio.Lock`, no duplicate client) -- the real cause is aggregate demand from every pocket (v8/v9/swing/vc/momentum/bonding, all active simultaneously) exceeding the account's real ceiling, not a code bug in the throttle itself. Exact same incident class already fixed once for smart_money.py's wallet-scoring path (29/07, Item #186, production live-lock) -- never applied to VC.
Solution : `acp_onchain_scan.py`'s TA block now calls `momentum_entry.fetch_candles` (the sanctioned public alias for the 6-stage cascade, same pattern smart_money.py already uses) instead of `ohlcv_client.get_ohlcv` directly -- `ta_timeframe` now sourced from `get_last_candle_provenance()` (display-only field, no downstream calc depends on its exact value, verified before the change). `base_crawler.discover_top_pools` gains a Birdeye fallback (`services.birdeye.discover_base_tokens_bulk`, own independent throttle/quota, same liquidity floor) tried only when the GeckoTerminal stage returns nothing -- new injectable `birdeye_discover` param (same testability pattern as the existing `fetch` param), no age filter possible on this fallback (Birdeye doesn't expose pool age) -- an honest gap, not a silently ignored parameter. 2 new tests in `test_base_crawler.py` (fallback triggers on empty GeckoTerminal result; fallback skipped when GeckoTerminal already succeeded, never doubles load on a healthy provider) + 2 pre-existing tests updated to inject a no-op fallback (deterministic, no live network call from the test suite). Full aria-core suite green before deploy (1 unrelated pre-existing flake in `test_x402_budget.py`, confirmed passing in isolation, untouched by this change). `base_crawler.py`/`skills/acp_onchain_scan.py`/`tests/test_base_crawler.py`.

Separate, unrelated finding surfaced during this same diagnosis (not acted on, operator's call): Groq LLM at its daily token cap (100k TPD exhausted) and Grok at its monthly credit/spending limit -- both failing repeatedly in prod logs for hours, `vc_analysis` already degrades cleanly (`llm_used=False`) but the LLM layer itself needs operator attention (recharge/limit raise), out of this session's scope.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : GeckoTerminal shared quota -- reduce aggregate demand, not just add resilience (operator follow-up)
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : the VC pocket fix above (same day) made VC resilient to GeckoTerminal 429s but did nothing to reduce them -- 48h of prod logs still showed near-continuous 429s across every call site (aggregate demand from v8/v9/swing/vc/momentum/bonding all running simultaneously exceeding the account's real ceiling, throttle itself correctly architected). Operator explicitly asked to act on this root cause, not just the symptom.
Solution : two independent changes, both aimed at reducing real HTTP calls against the shared quota rather than absorbing failures better. (1) `base_crawler.discover_top_pools`: new short-TTL cache (`_top_pools_cache`, 300s, same pattern as `momentum_entry.py`'s `_candles_cache`) around the `top_pools` fetch -- this endpoint barely moves minute to minute, so re-fetching it on every VC crawl cycle was pure waste. Only applied on the real `_fetch_gt` path (never when a test injects `fetch=`, keeps the suite deterministic) and never caches a failure (a 429/outage isn't frozen for the full TTL -- the next call can still retry GeckoTerminal or reach the Birdeye fallback immediately). (2) `services/geckoterminal.py` and `services/ohlcv.py`: removed the 3-attempt retry-with-backoff on HTTP 429 (was #157/14/07's "retry once is enough for an isolated burst" policy) -- under the SUSTAINED saturation actually observed, a retry almost never succeeds while still spending a real request against an already-exhausted quota, pure amplification (each failed evaluation was consuming up to 3x the quota it needed). Gives up on the first 429 now, in both clients. 5 new/updated tests in `test_base_crawler.py` (cache hit/TTL-expiry/never-caches-a-failure) + 2 tests rewritten in `test_geckoterminal_client.py` and 2 in `test_ohlcv_client.py` to lock the new no-retry behavior (a deliberately changed invariant, updated in the same commit per the coherence doctrine). Full aria-core suite green (9501 passed, 0 failures -- the earlier session's unrelated x402 flake did not reproduce) before deploy. `base_crawler.py`/`services/geckoterminal.py`/`services/ohlcv.py` + 3 test files.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : narrative-signal shadow -- forward-tests "trade the news, not the chart" (operator direction)
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : operator direction ("enfaite elle doit trader le bruit et pas le graphique" then "tu dois debloquer les info pour aria trouve comment") after a 4-token exercise (CLANKER/MAMO/WIRE/aeon) showed every real pump had an EVENT cause (Farcaster acquisition, Coinbase listing, Base-app integration) invisible to the technical-only entry gates -- while the same session also showed narrative alone is fabricable (gitlawb) and says WHAT, never WHEN (CLANKER: 11 months between visible link and pump). Two sources were verified live as actually unlockable: DefiLlama exposes real per-protocol fees (clanker $274k/30d vs gitlawb ~$8k -- the exact "economic sense" that was missing on WIRE/MAMO), and Coinbase Exchange's public /products catalog exposes listings in launch phase (post_only/limit_only, 21 live examples) -- the exact MAMO signal (+42% BEFORE the official trading hour).
Solution : new `narrative_signal_shadow.py`, same never-route-a-trade shadow doctrine as v8_limit_shadow/v8_rsi_reversal_shadow. Two signals, ZERO network calls per candidate (global catalogs cached module-wide, DefiLlama 6h TTL / Coinbase 1h TTL, single-attempt fetches -- a failed refresh keeps the stale cache, never blocks, never retries into a saturated provider): (1) `defillama_revenue` -- candidate's token belongs to a protocol with >=$10k/30d real fees (address match strong via /protocols, symbol match weak+tagged -- only 4134/8002 protocols carry an address, verified live); (2) `cex_listing_phase` -- symbol newly appeared in Coinbase's catalog (persisted diff, first run seeds silently) or sits in launch phase. Each signal row records price-at-signal; later evaluations of the same contract fill price_after_24h/price_after_7d -- the summary() answers "do narrative candidates outperform forward" BEFORE any strategy change is proposed. Hooked best-effort into `evaluate_momentum_entry` right after best-pair resolution (every pocket's evaluations feed it). Third, generic detector added the same session (operator: "tous signaux suffisamment importants sur n'importe quel token, pas juste coinbase ou defillama"): `conviction_research`'s per-candidate diligence (X buzz/website/GitHub/Farcaster/Telegram/Tavily) logged as a dated `conviction_research` signal via new generic `record_external_signal()` whenever potential_score >= 7/10 -- honest limit documented in the module: it only runs on candidates already reaching a technical BUY (standard mode), so an aeon-at-the-ATL (active GitHub, no setup) still never reaches it; mass pre-BUY coverage costs real X/Tavily budget and is a separate chantier, as is extending the CEX detector beyond Coinbase (LBank/MEXC/Gate -- the aeon rebound's most probable catalyst was its 27/07 LBank listing, 94% of its volume today). Live-verified end-to-end before deploy: AERO -> Aerodrome V1 $830k/30d, signal recorded. 13 tests (`test_narrative_signal_shadow.py`). `narrative_signal_shadow.py` (new)/`momentum_entry.py`.

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject : what actually causes a micro-cap pump -- the aeon case study, two-layer model (carve-out for a future social-reach detector)
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : the 08/08 "trade the news not the chart" session needed a real, dated causal chain rather than a plausible story. Four tokens were traced; aeon (aeon.fun, agent framework, Base, $865k mcap) turned out to be the fully-documented case because its GitHub, its X account (@aeonframework -- NOT @aeonfun, which does not exist) and its price history could all be cross-checked to the day. Two of my own readings were WRONG before verification and are recorded here so a future session doesn't repeat them: (a) a DexScreener 1h chart made the 30/07 wick look like "the" pump -- the 4h view shows the real pump was mid-MAY (ATH 22/05), the July wick being a minor blip; (b) I first credited the May pump to visible ecosystem/commit acceleration -- operator pushback ("les degen ont juste tradé les commit? ils sont pas aussi intelligents, il y a dû y avoir une annonce plus officielle") was correct, and the real driver was found on X.
Solution (findings, no code) : TWO DISTINCT LAYERS, and conflating them is the analytical error to avoid. **Layer 1 -- the fact, machine-readable, free, timestamped, EARLY.** For the Aug rebound: commit `feat: deploy-uni-hook skill (Uniswap v4 hook generate+audit+simulate+deploy)` (PR #804) landed 2026-07-31, publicly, FOUR DAYS before the official partnership announcement -- and two days after aeon's own all-time low (29/07). Same pattern in May: 30 commits over 9 days, `ECOSYSTEM.md` listing 39 projects built on aeon + 34 skills ported from derivative instances (23/05). **Layer 2 -- the amplification, human, where the price actually explodes.** May: a 21k-view X post on 17/05 ("$AEON will become the next Launchpad for AI agents just like @virtuals_io", with $SYNTH as the first self-launched agent confirming usage the same day) -- an explicit COMPARATIVE THESIS referencing a precedent everyone knew ($AIXBT on Virtuals), which is the language that actually moves degens, not commit logs. Aug: @blknoiz06 (Ansem) "called" $aeon on the v4-hooks thesis BEFORE the 04/08 official Programmable x aeonframework announcement. Whether Ansem personally read the commits is unknowable and beside the point -- what is PROVEN is that the information existed publicly and timestamped before the move. Doctrine that follows: ARIA can never beat Ansem on layer 2, but layer 1 is exactly where a machine beats a human -- a commit adding a capability tied to a hot theme is detectable, free, and days early. Concrete carve-out for a future session (NOT built, needs its own chantier): a social-reach detector measuring (i) REACH of posts mentioning the token (views/engagement, not just existence -- `conviction_research.py` already does budget-bounded X research, `x_substance.py` already scores an account's substance, neither measures reach of third-party mentions), (ii) whether an INFLUENTIAL account (not the project's own) mentions it, (iii) comparative-thesis phrasing ("next <known winner>"). Plus a layer-1 detector: ecosystem-acceleration (fork growth + commit frequency + external contributors -- aeon shows 223 forks for 617 stars, an abnormally high ratio betraying real usage; `project_activity.py` already reads GitHub, the fork/contributor delta is what's missing). Verification hygiene confirmed the hard way this session: three homonym projects (AEON Pay/$8M YZi Labs raise, AEON.XYZ on Bitget Launchpool, aeon.fun) contaminate every generic search -- always pin the contract/repo/X handle before attributing an event.

Addendum (same day, operator "et pour le pump de mai ?") -- the May chain was traced with the same method and it CORRECTS the layer-1 definition above. Dated facts: `feat: instance fleet skills -- spawn-instance, fleet-control, fork-fleet` landed 2026-04-08 (the self-launch capability itself); MCP adaptor 13/04 + A2A Protocol Gateway 15/04 (interoperability); `show-hn-draft` 03/05 and `star-momentum-alert` 05/05 (launch tooling -- the project literally shipped a skill projecting its own next GitHub-star milestone date, i.e. it tracked its own adoption momentum); `product-hunt-launch` skill 15/05; then 17/05 $SYNTH becomes the FIRST self-launched agent on aeon -> the 21k-view "next Virtuals" post -> ATH 22/05. **The correction: the capability existed 5-6 weeks before the pump and moved nothing on its own.** What triggered was its FIRST PUBLIC USE BY A THIRD PARTY. So layer 1 is not "a commit adds a capability" (a commit can stay dead code for months, a weak signal) but "an outside party visibly exercises that capability for the first time" -- which retroactively also explains August: the 31/07 `deploy-uni-hook` commit only mattered because Programmable, a third party, announced building on it on 04/08. Practical consequence for the future detector: watching commits alone would have fired 5-6 weeks early on aeon (unusable); the signal to build is third-party adoption events -- new forks that actually ship, external contributors, a named project publicly declaring usage (aeon's own `ECOSYSTEM.md`/39-project table is exactly that data, self-published) -- which is both stronger and still machine-readable and free.

[CODE] Subject : multi-source signal cascade with human-in-the-loop triage -- architecture carved, NOT built (operator design, 08/08)
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : the aeon case study above proved layer-1 signals (third-party adoption events) are free, machine-readable and days early, but left two unsolved problems: (a) no machine criterion exists for "is this advance actually significant" (a "send an email" skill and a "deploy+audit Uniswap v4 hooks" skill are one commit each, worth wildly different amounts), and (b) with 2 observed cases on 1 project, any hardcoded threshold would be invented, not calibrated. Operator design answers both: a cascade PER SOURCE (X / web / Farcaster / GitHub / ...), each with several filtering stages, and CLAUDE CODE as the final triage stage at first -- emitting a follow-up alert for a future FICTIONAL buy -- then handing the role to ARIA once it demonstrably works.
Solution (architecture agreed, to build in a dedicated session -- nothing coded yet) : **grid layout** -- sources as columns, filter stages as rows. Stage 1 COLLECT (mechanical, 24/7, no judgment) -> stage 2 QUANTITATIVE FILTER (scale, velocity, third-party credibility; must cut ~95% of noise -- the pipeline evaluates ~100 candidates/cycle several times an hour, an unfiltered feed would drown the human stage and cost real tokens) -> stage 3 CONVERGENCE (single shared table counting concordant sources per token) -> stage 4 PERSISTENT QUEUE read by Claude Code at session start. **Three corrections to the raw design, all verified this session:** (1) the 9 collectors ALREADY EXIST (`services/project_activity.py`, `skills/github_substance.py`, `services/farcaster.py`, `services/telegram_channel_verify.py`, `radar_x.py`, `skills/x_substance.py`, `services/twitsh.py`, `services/tavily.py`, `x_research_budget.py`) -- the chantier is NOT building collectors, it is triggering them CONTINUOUSLY and decoupled from the technical signal (today they only run on a candidate that already reached a technical BUY via `conviction_research`, which is exactly why aeon-at-its-ATL with a fresh $SYNTH fork was invisible). (2) Sources are NOT cost-symmetric and must not be built in parallel: GitHub and Farcaster are FREE, Tavily is budget-bounded, X is PAY-PER-USE and deliberately cut since early July (`x_research_budget.WEEKLY_REQUEST_CAP = 100`) -- build order GitHub -> Farcaster -> web -> X, each only after the cheaper ones prove value. (3) A shared convergence point is mandatory, not optional: separate per-source queues would lose the strongest signal, which is MULTI-SOURCE concordance (aeon's real signal was GitHub commit + $SYNTH announced on X + self-published ECOSYSTEM.md simultaneously) -- the concordance counter is free and is the most discriminating filter available. **Two additions that make the human stage falsifiable:** (a) capture the REASONING, not just the verdict -- an annotated "why" per decision is what later becomes ARIA's prompt with real labelled examples; a bare yes/no transfers nothing. (b) THE test before any handover: compare forward returns of items Claude VALIDATED vs items Claude REJECTED. If validated ones outperform, the criterion has value and can be transferred; if they don't, the judgment is no better than chance and must NOT be given to ARIA -- without this test the project would automate a bias while believing it automates a skill. Known accepted limit: Claude Code sessions are intermittent, so the queue must be a persisted table (never a volatile notification) and triage happens in deferred time -- acceptable against aeon's ~5-day exploitable window (17/05 $SYNTH -> 22/05 ATH), but structurally blind to few-hour moves.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : bonding volet-3 (liquidity floor) sat ABOVE the entry gate -- 3 identical mechanical exits
Date : 2026.08.08 / Problem : operator screenshot showed 3 bonding swing positions (ASTRAEUS/HYDREX/STONKCAT), 3 different contracts, all closed within the SAME 12-min cycle at the SAME -0.7% P&L. Traced live: entry prices near-identical because every fresh Virtuals-bonding token starts on the exact same standard curve (already documented Item #167, 92.7% of launches cluster at ~9,591$ reserve, a fixed launch-config deposit, not a market signal). Root cause: `bonding_entry._MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` (entry gate, lowered to 5,000$ on 28/07 specifically because 10,000$ vetoed that standard launch config) sat BELOW `paper_trader.BONDING_LIQUIDITY_FLOOR_USD` (exit floor, left at 10,000$ on 28/07 on the stated logic "exit floor stays above entry, never the inverse"). That logic itself was the bug: any position opened in the 5,000-10,000$ band (the typical launch reserve) was therefore ALREADY below the exit floor at entry -- volet 3 fired mechanically on the very next management cycle, with no real liquidity movement involved, identical across any token sharing that launch config.
Solution : `BONDING_LIQUIDITY_FLOOR_USD` 10,000$ -> 5,000$, matched to `bonding_entry._MIN_LIQUIDITY_USD` instead of deliberately kept above it -- a position accepted at entry can no longer be doomed by the exit check before anything has actually happened. `test_coherence.py`'s invariant lock + `docs/trading-thresholds-calibration.md` updated in the same pass; 2 `test_paper_trader.py` cases adjusted (liquidity fixtures that were `< 10,000` and are now `< 5,000`, `3,000$`) so they still exercise the intended branch. — paper_trader.py/test_coherence.py/test_paper_trader.py/trading-thresholds-calibration.md.

[CODE] Subject : on-chain rescue for holder-concentration's x402 fallback -- total Blockscout outage silently starved it
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : real production outage (base.blockscout.com returning HTTP 500 on EVERY endpoint, verified live via direct curl -- free/no-key, Pro key, `/stats`, and even WETH itself all 500'd, RPC and DexScreener unaffected -- isolated to Blockscout). Operator flagged a wall of "ACHAT REFUSE -- securite invérifiable" Telegram alerts. Root cause found by reading `_check_holder_concentration`: the paid x402 fallback (built 21/07 for the DIFFERENT case of "Pro credits exhausted") only returns raw per-holder balances, no decimals/total_supply of its own -- the caller has always needed a separate `client.get_token_metadata()` call to interpret them, and THAT call goes through the same `base.blockscout.com` host as the free holders endpoint. During a total outage (not just a quota), the metadata call fails first and the guardrail degrades straight to "unavailable" WITHOUT EVER ATTEMPTING x402 -- confirmed zero x402 calls in 3h of prod logs despite the fallback existing and its budget being 87% unspent ($4.35/$5 remaining).
Solution : `base_onchain.fetch_erc20_metadata(token_address)` -- reads `decimals()`/`totalSupply()` directly on-chain via RPC (public Base mainnet endpoint, already used read-only elsewhere in this module for bonding-curve reads), zero dependency on Blockscout, free or paid. `_check_holder_concentration` tries it (via `asyncio.to_thread`, same convention as the existing on-chain caller in `acp_onchain_scan.py`) only when `client.get_token_metadata()` itself failed -- zero incremental cost/latency in the normal case where Blockscout metadata already succeeds. Makes the x402 fallback genuinely reachable during a TOTAL outage, not just a Pro-credit exhaustion. — base_onchain.py/momentum_entry.py/test_base_onchain.py/test_momentum_entry.py, 3 existing tests updated (double/triple-failure fail-closed paths now also force the on-chain rescue to fail, preserving their original intent) + 6 new tests (on-chain read direct/zero-supply/missing-address/RPC-error, rescue used when Blockscout metadata fails, rescue never called when it already succeeds).

**Real-world follow-up the same day**: deployed and live-tested during the actual outage -- the on-chain rescue works (metadata fetched successfully via RPC), but the x402 payment itself only succeeded ~1/3 of the time ("toujours 402 après paiement" on the other attempts) -- Blockscout's outage also intermittently affects their OWN payment settlement backend (`api.blockscout.com`), not just the free endpoint. So this fix alone doesn't guarantee buys resume reliably during a severe outage.

[DEPLOYE] Subject : auto-armed, auto-expiring outage bypass for holder-concentration -- replaces the manual .env/redeploy flow
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : the manual bypass (`ARIA_HOLDER_CONCENTRATION_OUTAGE_BYPASS_UNTIL` env var, 06/08) required an operator `.env` edit + a full redeploy every single time it needed (re)arming -- real, repeated friction during the SAME 10/08 Blockscout outage as the entry above (already needed re-arming once that day). Direct operator feedback: "pourquoi il faut le relancer c'est chiant sa je vait finir par le supprimer se truc" -- a real risk that the whole guardrail gets deleted out of frustration with the OPERATIONAL mechanism, not the guardrail's own judgment.
Solution : new module `holder_concentration_outage_bypass.py` -- detects a SUSTAINED outage from the guardrail's OWN real failure signal (3 consecutive real failures across DIFFERENT candidates, every path exhausted: free/Pro Blockscout + the on-chain rescue above + paid x402 -- same threshold as `services/blockscout.py`'s own circuit breaker, zero coupling to its internals). Arms itself for 2h (extended automatically on further failures while already armed, so a longer outage stays covered), disarms itself the INSTANT a real verdict succeeds again (Blockscout recovered) OR the window expires -- whichever first. State persisted in its own SQLite table (never the env var -- that manual override stays available untouched as an independent path, this only adds an automatic one). Zero operator action needed for a typical outage going forward -- the previous case (this exact 10/08 outage) would have self-armed and self-disarmed without ever touching `.env`. — new file `holder_concentration_outage_bypass.py`, `momentum_entry.py` (`_holder_data_unavailable_verdict` now async, checks the manual override first then the automatic one, records real successes to disarm), new `test_holder_concentration_outage_bypass.py` (6 tests: not-armed-initially, below-threshold-never-arms, reaches-threshold-arms-once, real-success-disarms-and-resets, window-self-expires, continued-failures-extend-window) + 2 end-to-end tests in `test_momentum_entry.py` (sustained failures across different contracts auto-arm and let a never-seen token through; a real recovery disarms immediately).

[DEPLOYE] Subject : GeckoTerminal throttle made adaptive -- replaces 7 manual edit-commit-deploy cycles in 2 weeks
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : `_MIN_INTERVAL`/`_AUTHENTICATED_MIN_INTERVAL` (the client's fixed per-call throttle) was hand-edited and redeployed 7 times between 21/07 and 04/08 -- each real 429 incident in prod needed a human to notice it, diagnose, edit the constant and redeploy (see the module's own dated comment trail above the constant for the full history, including the 04/08 5x-widen-then-revert-minutes-later episode). Found while auditing the codebase for other manual-intervention mechanisms after the holder-concentration/GoPlus fixes the same day (operator: "il y aurait pas d'autre mécanisme à automatiser?").
Solution : `GeckoTerminalClient` now tracks a mutable `_current_interval` (the live pace) alongside `_min_interval` (the operator's own last hand-calibrated value, now a HARD FLOOR never sped past automatically). A real 429 tightens `_current_interval` immediately (x1.5, capped at 3x the floor); a run of `_CONSECUTIVE_SUCCESSES_BEFORE_EASING` (30) real successes eases it back down in small steps (x0.9), never below the floor. Deliberately ASYMMETRIC on the operator's own explicit 04/08 warning ("GeckoTerminal sits behind Cloudflare... a sustained aggressive rate risks a Cloudflare-level IP block... not a risk to re-test casually") -- tighten fast (every single 429), ease slow (only after sustained proof), and never automatically exceed the last human-approved pace. `wait_for_shared_rate_limit()` (the vanguard/backend shared entry point) benefits automatically -- it already calls the same `_throttle()`. In-memory only (process-scoped, no persistence) -- a fresh deploy always restarts at the safe floor, never inherits a tightened-but-stale state. — `services/geckoterminal.py`, `tests/test_geckoterminal_client.py` (12 new tests: floor on start, tightening/cap, easing never below floor, isolated successes below threshold never ease, gradual easing, rate-limit resets the streak, repeated cycles never drop below floor, both wired through the real `_get_json` path).

[CODE] Subject : backtest overfitting check (permutation/Bonferroni/binomial) -- applied to v8's wick-gate, confirms the pause was justified
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : backlog #266 (Monte-Carlo permutation + walk-forward overfitting detection, banked 08/08, never built) -- v8's own wick-gate threshold (60% vs 25.6% WR, Fisher p=0.026) came from a SINGLE 58-trade retrospective sample where ~6 unrelated filter families (SL/TP grids, RVOL gating, volatility squeeze, regime/weekday/align, wick ratio) were tested against the SAME sample and only the wick-ratio survived -- a textbook multiple-comparisons setup that inflates the real false-positive rate well above the nominal 5%. Live v8 (43 real closed trades, hard-gated on exactly this threshold) had already been paused earlier this week after showing 0 winners (commit 1d275d4e) -- but no rigorous statistical check had confirmed 0/43 was actually inconsistent with the claimed edge, versus just an unlucky short run.
Solution : new module `backtest_robustness.py` (pure stdlib, no numpy/scipy) -- `binomial_consistency_check()` (exact binomial test), `bonferroni_correction()` (family-wise error correction, `n_comparisons` decoupled from how many p-values are actually known -- handles the real case where only 1 of ~6 tested filters' p-values was ever recorded), `permutation_test_winrate_diff()` (Monte-Carlo, distribution-free, reusable on any future two-group comparison once raw trade outcomes are in hand). Applied to the real numbers: 0/43 live wins is essentially impossible under the claimed 60% backtest rate (p=7.7e-18) -- AND still implausible even against the wick-gate's OWN "below threshold" control group from the original backtest (25.6%, p<0.0001), meaning live v8 underperforms not just the winning filter's claim but the losing one too. The original p=0.026 finding does not survive Bonferroni correction for ~6 comparisons made on the same sample (corrected alpha 0.0083 < 0.026). Confirms the pause was the right call and the wick-gate threshold should not be trusted as a standalone edge without a genuine walk-forward split on a larger sample before any future 8.1/8.2 variant reuses it. Open question, not resolved here: WHY live underperforms even the control group (regime shift between the 05/08 sample and the 05-07/08 live window? a difference between the 58-trade sample's win definition and live pnl_pct>0? something in v8's exit rules, not just entry, diverging from the backtest's scope?) -- flagged for the next v8/8.x session, not guessed at. — new `backtest_robustness.py` + `test_backtest_robustness.py` (11 tests: binomial edge cases, Bonferroni with explicit vs inferred family size, permutation test detects/rejects a real difference, reproducible with a seed).

**Same-day follow-up, the open question above is now resolved**: 42 of the 43 live v8 trades NEVER traded above their own entry price even briefly (`high_water_price > entry_price` true for only 1/43), and the average loss is negative and consistent across all 3 live trading days (05/08 -3.03%, 06/08 -2.0%, 07/08 -2.52% -- no single catastrophic day explains the aggregate). Rules out both the "exit-rule divergence" and "one-off regime shift" hypotheses -- the entry signal itself never captured a positive move, uniformly across the whole live window. `indicators.hammer_wick_ratio()`'s formula was re-read and is simple/correct (no implementation-vs-backtest mismatch suspected). Combined with the original backtest's own breakdown (9W/6L = only 15 trades in the "above threshold" bucket that produced the 60% claim), the most likely explanation is unavoidable given Occam's razor: a 60%-on-15-trades finding was very likely small-sample noise inflated by the multiple-comparisons setup already flagged above, not a real edge -- now directly falsified by a 3x-larger live sample. Methodology rule locked for any future v8/8.x filter candidate: see CLAUDE.md's scalping_v8 section.

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject : Base propagation-congestion coverage checked against the real circuit breaker (backlog #261) -- gap confirmed but low-stakes today (paper-only), not fixed
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : backlog #261 (real 31/01/2026 Base incident: bad transaction-propagation config, month-long rollback+rework) asked whether the momentum pipeline's existing circuit breaker (#186) covers a network propagation-congestion degradation, not just a price/volume anomaly.
Solution : traced the real circuit breaker -- `risk_guard.py`'s (#186) is a PORTFOLIO DRAWDOWN breaker (equity high-water-mark, halts new entries past a loss threshold), entirely price/PnL-based, no network-health signal at all. The only OTHER breaker in the pipeline is `momentum_entry.py`'s adaptive PER-PROVIDER one (#95, line ~1820) -- but it only trips on an outright API failure (429/5xx), never on "the API returned 200 with genuinely stale/congestion-delayed data" -- confirmed via grep: no candle/price TIMESTAMP-freshness check exists anywhere in the pipeline. **Real gap confirmed, not fixed in this pass**: a Base congestion event that degrades data freshness without outright failing the API call would go undetected by every existing guardrail. **Why not fixed now**: today's actual exposure is low -- the 1M$ test is 100% paper (no real transaction ever broadcast on a congested network), and the only real-money path (`agent_wallet_pilot.py`'s $10-25 CDP swaps) delegates execution to Coinbase's own infrastructure (0x Argon Router, cf. backlog #273 entry above), which already has its own congestion handling ARIA doesn't need to duplicate. A candle-timestamp staleness check is a real, scoped improvement worth building (not a guardrail/capital-réel file, within the standing paper-trading-audit autonomy) but deserves its own design pass rather than a rushed addition at the end of an already-long session -- left open for a dedicated future session. Backlog #261 stays open (not marked RÉSOLU) with this finding recorded so the next session doesn't re-derive it from scratch.

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject : FinHarness methodology compared against momentum's LLM gates (backlog #274) -- threat model doesn't structurally apply, gates are stateless single-shot classifiers with zero tool-calling autonomy
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : backlog #274 (arXiv 2605.27333, verified via the paper's own abstract/HTML) asked to compare FinHarness's methodology against `_llm_confirm`/`_llm_security_gate`/`_llm_confirm_and_gate` (momentum's 3 LLM gates) -- specifically whether it catches "benign-then-pivot" mid-trajectory attacks the current gates were never explicitly tested against.
Solution : verified FinHarness's real architecture (WebSearch, paper abstract) before comparing: it wraps a TOOL-CALLING agent across a MULTI-TURN session -- Query Monitor fuses "cross-turn intent drift" (a session-level risk cumulant across several turns), Tool Monitor evaluates each prospective TOOL CALL's permission tier/parameters/sequence, Cascade adaptively escalates verification. The "benign-then-pivot" attack it targets requires an agent that can (1) hold state across multiple turns/tool calls within one session and (2) autonomously invoke further actions mid-trajectory. Verified against the real code (`momentum_entry.py` lines ~2708-3020): none of the 3 gates hold that shape -- each is a STATELESS single-shot call (fresh context every candidate, no session/conversation carried across calls), returns only a boolean (`_llm_confirm`) or `(bool, str)` (`_llm_security_gate`/`_llm_confirm_and_gate`) -- confirmed by tracing every call site (line 4306, 4326): the LLM never sets a numeric parameter (position size, price, slippage -- all deterministic elsewhere) and never invokes a tool itself; the actual buy/no-buy branch is decided by surrounding Python code reading the boolean, not by the LLM taking an action. **Conclusion: the "benign-then-pivot" attack class doesn't apply by architecture, not by an explicit check** -- there is no multi-turn session for intent to drift across, and no tool-calling capability for a pivot to exploit. This is a stronger baseline than what FinHarness is built to protect (an agent WITH tool-calling autonomy), not a gap needing FinHarness's machinery. No code change needed. Should ARIA ever build a true multi-turn tool-calling agent with real financial actions (none exists today), FinHarness's Query/Tool Monitor pattern would become directly relevant and should be revisited then. Closes backlog #274.

[CODE] Subject : candle-staleness SHADOW observer built (backlog #261) -- logs a candidate freshness verdict on every real fetch, never gates yet
Date : 2026.08.10 / Problem : follow-up to the #261 audit entry above -- confirmed real gap: neither `_candles_price_consistent` (scale sanity) nor the scalping-only continuity gate (`candles[-1].ts - candles[-2].ts` vs median cadence, detects TRADING SILENCE) measures how OLD the most recent candle is relative to wall-clock now. A provider stuck re-serving a stale tail (e.g. during Base propagation congestion) would sail through both -- internal gaps between old candles stay perfectly regular; only the gap to NOW grows unboundedly.
Solution : new `candle_staleness_shadow.py`, exact same pattern as `wick_filter_shadow.py`/`chasing_filter_shadow.py` (append-only table, best-effort writes that never raise into a real fetch, `list_recent()`/`flagged_rate()` readers) -- deliberately SHADOW-ONLY, per the v8 wick-gate methodology rule locked this same session: no calibration data exists yet on real staleness frequency, so a candidate threshold gets logged and observed before ever being promoted to a hard gate, never guessed straight into production behavior. `STALENESS_SHADOW_MULTIPLIER = 5.0` (reasoned starting point: the existing scalping continuity gate already uses 4.0x median interval for trading-silence; staleness-to-now gets a bit more slack for normal fetch/processing jitter, applied uniformly across modes unlike the scalping-only continuity gate). Wired into `momentum_entry.py::_fetch_candles` right where the price-consistency check already lives -- records `age_seconds = time.time() - candles[-1].ts` and `_median_candle_interval_seconds(candles)` (existing helper, reused not duplicated) on every non-empty real fetch, inside its own try/except so a shadow-logging failure can never turn a valid fetch into an empty result. No gate, no behavior change to any real trading decision today -- `flagged_rate()` gives a cheap way for a future session to check how often the candidate threshold would have fired before deciding whether/how to promote it.
`candle_staleness_shadow.py` (new), `momentum_entry.py` (`_fetch_candles` wiring) -- 8 new tests (`test_candle_staleness_shadow.py`: flag/no-flag/boundary/unknown-median/empty-contract/db-failure/flagged_rate), 2 new tests in `test_momentum_entry.py` (shadow observation recorded with the right fields; a broken shadow observer never blocks a real fetch). Full suite green (9843 passed, 17 skipped). Not yet deployed -- push/deploy gated by the 8000-raw-line accumulated-diff threshold (Devil's Advocate).

[CODE] Subject : GeckoTerminal base/quote mismatch was silently corrupting golden-pocket/RSI signals -- candles now rescaled onto the real spot price instead of trusted or discarded
Date : 2026.08.11 / Probleme : real bug caught live by the operator from a Telegram screenshot (HeyAnon/"Anon" swing limit order chart showing one diagonal line falling off a cliff, no golden-pocket levels visible unlike a normal chart). Traced to `pending_limit_order` id=2100: `price`/`price_at_order_placed`=0.2938 but `target`/`invalidation`/`gp_low`/`gp_high` all ~0.0112-0.0119 (~25x off), `rr: null` (unlike this same token's 2 earlier orders, both internally consistent). Root cause confirmed against BOTH real APIs live (never assumed): DexScreener gives Anon's real spot price (0.2938$, confirmed via a direct curl against the exact pool); GeckoTerminal, for the SAME pool address, labels fBOMB as "base" and Anon as "quote" (confirmed via a direct curl against `.../ohlcv/hour` -- `meta.base=fBOMB`, `meta.quote=Anon`) -- the candles it serves are fBOMB priced in Anon, not Anon priced in USD. `_candles_price_consistent` (Item #222, 30/07) exists precisely to catch this CLASS of mismatch, but its tolerance (1000x, calibrated against that incident's ~330,000x) is far too wide to catch a ~25x mismatch -- it silently passed through as if correct, corrupting `entry_signals.detect_entry`'s golden pocket/RSI/target/invalidation (all computed on the candles' own unit, only `execution_price`/R/R's entry point was ever the real spot price -- two different units mixed in the same signal).
Solution : operator's own idea, better than the initially-proposed "reject unless the other side is a stablecoin" fix -- golden pocket/Fibonacci retracement/RSI are ALL scale-invariant (computed from the candles' own RELATIVE shape, cf. `entry_signals.py`), so multiplying every OHLC value by a constant factor changes nothing about the signal, only the unit. New `_candle_price_scale_factor()` computes `pair.price_usd / candles[-1].close` whenever the ratio exceeds `_CANDLE_RESCALE_MIN_RATIO=2.0` (below that: normal cross-provider timing noise, left untouched -- rescaling an already-correct series would just inject float noise); new `_rescale_candles()` rebuilds the whole series (open/high/low/close × factor, volume/timestamp untouched -- `Candle` is frozen). Wired into `_fetch_candles` right before the existing `_candles_price_consistent` reject check, which keeps its ORIGINAL 1000x threshold as the last-resort reject for a mismatch too extreme to trust even after rescaling (probable real data corruption, not just a base/quote unit swap -- Item #222's own 330,000x incident stays rejected, unchanged). Anchors the last candle exactly on the known-real spot price (DexScreener, already independently confirmed reliable in real time) -- recovers a usable signal on every future case of this GeckoTerminal base/quote quirk instead of either trusting a wrong unit (the bug) or discarding a real one (the pre-fix mitigation for this same class of incident). Not limited to this one token -- GeckoTerminal is the OHLCV cascade's first and most-used stage across momentum standard, scalping v8/v9, and the dormant VC pocket; any candidate where GeckoTerminal happens to order base/quote opposite to what ARIA tracks was silently affected the same way before this fix.
`momentum_entry.py` (`_candle_price_scale_factor`, `_rescale_candles`, `_fetch_candles` wiring) -- 6 new tests in `test_momentum_entry.py` (`_candle_price_scale_factor`: none within normal noise, computed above threshold, none without pair/price; `_rescale_candles`: multiplies OHLC, leaves volume/timestamp untouched; `_fetch_candles`: rescales a moderate real-shaped mismatch onto the exact spot price, existing reject/accept tests re-verified unchanged). Full suite green (9863 passed, 17 skipped). **Known limit, not fixed here**: the short-TTL candle cache is keyed by pool/mode only, not by whether a `pair` was supplied -- a caller that never passes `pair` (`smart_money.py`'s wallet-scoring path, by its own docstring) could in principle prime the cache with un-rescaled candles a `pair`-carrying caller then reads back within the TTL window; pre-existing for the reject check too, not new here, left as a scoped follow-up rather than touching the shared cache in the same pass.

[CODE] Subject : one more DB-failure gap found in the same pattern-search -- Birdeye discovery cache had an unenforced "never raises" promise (backlog #106/#105 follow-up)
Date : 2026.08.11 / Problem : after finding the same anti-pattern 3x in one day (`system_issues.py`/`momentum_rejection_cache.py`/`holder_concentration_cache.py`, see `docs/HANDOFF_AUTOMATISATION.md`), ran a targeted agent audit across every other DB-backed module in the momentum/capital-tracking path claiming "never raises"/"best-effort" behavior. Found exactly one more real case: `_load_persisted_birdeye_cache`'s docstring already promised "never raises, a fresh scan is always a safe fallback" but had zero try/except around its `aiosqlite.connect` call; `_save_persisted_birdeye_cache` had the same gap without an explicit promise. Not exploitable today (the one real caller, `_discover_birdeye_base_tokens`, already wraps this in its own try/except) -- but the function lied about its own robustness, a real trap for any future direct caller. Every other module checked (momentum_scan_log/wick_filter_shadow/combo_signal_shadow/gate_audit_log/kill_incident_log/circuit_breaker_log/candle_staleness_shadow/narrative_signal_shadow/manual_candidates/wallet_scan_queue/limit_orders/paper_trader/smart_money) was already correctly protected -- confirms the earlier 3 fixes were the real gaps, not a systemic pattern across the whole codebase.
Solution : wrapped both functions in try/except, same doctrine as the 3 earlier fixes (a missed cache write/read only costs one avoidable Birdeye re-scan next cycle, never a wrong discovery result). 2 new regression tests in `test_momentum_entry.py`, verified red-then-green against the pre-fix code. Full suite green (9952 passed, 17 skipped). — `momentum_entry.py`, `test_momentum_entry.py` (+2 tests).

[ETAT ACTUEL] Subject : circuit breakers (pocket + macro) never exercised with a genuinely raising price_lookup -- confirmed already correct, coverage locked
Date : 2026.08.11 / Problem : an earlier Explore audit (backlog #106) flagged that every "crashed" scenario in `test_risk_guard.py` (e.g. `TestMacroReminder.test_reminder_after_macro_trigger`) actually returns a VALID price and simulates the crash by tampering with the persisted high-water-mark file -- `paper_trader.portfolio_summary`'s real `except Exception: price = None` fallback (used by both `risk_guard.evaluate_portfolio_risk` and `evaluate_macro_risk`, the pocket and the portfolio-wide breakers) was never exercised with a genuinely raising `price_lookup`.
Solution : found the opposite of the working hypothesis, same pattern as #104/#107 -- the existing fallback (value an unreachable position at cost, never a fabricated gain/loss, never a crash) is already correct and deliberate. Added 2 regression tests (`TestEvaluatePortfolioRisk.test_real_price_lookup_failure_falls_back_to_cost_never_crashes`, `TestMacroCircuitBreaker.test_real_price_lookup_failure_across_all_pockets_never_crashes`) with a real raising `price_lookup` across an open position (pocket breaker) and all 3 pockets at once (macro breaker) -- both passed on the first run, no code change needed, just locks the correct behavior against a future regression. Full suite green (one unrelated pre-existing flake in `test_circuit_breaker_status.py`, confirmed passing 3/3 in isolation, untouched by this change). — `test_risk_guard.py` (+2 tests).
